    “Take off your boots.” She said.

    Lena said nothing as she shrugged her way out of her hiking boots, numbed to the bone now.

    “You should probably get in the water…”

    Again, Lena acquiesced.

    She slipped out of everything except her undergarments and then padded calmly into the soft mud of the lakeshore until she was in up to her neck. The water that surrounded her was warm— she wasn’t sure at first, but it almost felt like being hugged. Lena looked back towards the shoreline and then gestured with a tilt of her head for Ruby to perhaps join her. Ruby replied with a shake of her own head “no” and wrapped her arms around her waist. Lena felt poorly for the young girl again. Apart from the danger she faced every single day of her life, and then losing her mother, and then being wrangled by a verified maniac to extort her gift from her… Out of all of those things, she held anxiety over her weight? Lena couldn’t imagine the torment she might have faced from other children her age— or even adults, should they be so cruel. Lena assumed that they were. She held a hand out of the water then to wave at Ruby, “You were right. It feels better… Come on.”

    Ruby paused for a moment and then ducked around the corner of the boathouse with a frown. Lena might have felt sad that she had declined, if not for the brown, giggling flash that leapt across the small dock a few moments later to splash down into the water beside her. Lena laughed as she toed her way across the soft, muddy lake bottom to reach the dock. She held on there and laughed some more with Ruby, who drew her sopping wet hair out of her eyes and then sighed, as if she remembered only too quickly where they were, and why, and what had happened.

    They were quiet again for some time, just bobbing there in the water beside one another. Lena felt that much more of their shared silence might drive her mad and was opening her mouth to speak again sooner than she realized.

    “I wonder if Frank’s alright?”

    “I dunno,” Ruby replied as she scrubbed diligently behind her ear, “…I can’t hear him from this far away.”

    “Wow. So, you really can speak with them?”

    Ruby nodded. “Yeah.”

    For whatever reason, Lena began to chuckle again. Something wild that lived inside of her begged to be set free all of a sudden, and she was quite helpless to stop it. Soon, both of them were bubbling into that same sort of hee-hawed laughter once again, that is until Lena began to shift dreadfully into a sob, and then into another, and then she began to cry uninhibited with her face pressed into a gnarled leg of the dock.

    Ruby, being the sweet girl that she was, decided just to let her cry that time.

    Chapter End Notes

    I've decided to split this tale into two acts (not meaning to say that I'll be posting them separately, it'll still be all right here). We'll circle back around to Kara's POV in a little bit. Lena's got more of "herself" to find. I've been wanting the opportunity to write something featuring a very sweet surrogate mother/daughter type relationship between Lena and Ruby. So yayyy :D

    Thanks for reading! <3

    Chapter 8: Home Is the Thing That Thumps

    Chapter Notes

    TW: violence
    I dunno why I keep putting these smaller warnings here for this story, the whole thing's gonna be pretty punchy/bloody. Maybe in case you're having a bad day, I guess, or haven't been feeling all that well in the mental health department. A heads up, if you will. Take care of yourselves. <3

    Chapter Eight: Home Is the Thing That Thumps

     

    Lena wondered what Kara’s hair would look like in direct sunlight.

    She wondered a lot of things.

    How it might shine, or drape over her ear, or how smoothly her fingers might run through it if left to do so. Pure and unfiltered sunshine seemed like such a natural habitat for someone who could beam so brightly with something as simple as a smile.

    Steering her thoughts towards more positive things took a great deal of energy, to be sure. The night had been cold, and she had missed her bed again, but every time she thought about Kara down in her cell, away from any natural light or even the faintest hope of seeing the outside world again, it was nearly too much to handle. There were boxes in Lena’s mind. Kara could only fit neatly inside the ones that didn’t overflow with doom, and despair, and the end of the world as she knew it. What little left of it there was, anyway.

    It kept her awake, yes, but it also kept her sane.

    Lena had no idea where to go from here. There was so much more to learn, yet the risks of doing so not only involved herself now, but a child as well. It had become abundantly clear as time went on that Ruby was very good at putting on a brave face. What might have seemed like a helping hand back in the forest was beginning to look a bit more like a cry for help. Perhaps it was that she didn’t quite know how to say it— Lena knew that Ruby was scared.

    There sprang up that deep gorge lain with fear again at the back of her mind, and Lena was faced yet again with a choice. To dive into it, or simply build a bridge across. While the bridge took so much more effort, it was the only thing that led back to Kara.

    The scrape of a door brought Lena’s attention front and center again. It had finally begun to rain, so the unfortunate boathouse missing its pitiful roof had them searching elsewhere for someplace dry to lay their heads. She and Ruby had ended up inside the remains of what looked like a women’s shelter. Why the need to house so many women in one place before the war was beyond Lena, but she supposed that it was a place well worth preserving in its own right. Pre-war strife was a subject of much debate more than two-hundred years later… History certainly had a way of hiding its shameful bits.

    Ruby clambered inside with the front of her shirt full of round objects. Some big, some small. She dumped them out at the center of their tiny encampment and then sat back against a thin mattress with noisy springs. Apples, and walnuts. Lena was pleased.

    “Morning.”

    “Good morning…” Lena sat up from the lumpy pile of blankets where she had attempted to sleep. “This looks delicious. Thank you. Trouble finding it all?”

    “Not really.” Ruby shook her head as she twisted the stem off a greenish apple.

    The sudden claw-like scrape which came next was that of a pack of cougars. They were sandy colored except for the small white patch that fit around their muzzles, and had paws the size of Lena’s boots. Three massive predator cats, each of them almost big enough for Ruby to ride if she wished. Lena hadn’t even realized they were there until they came in. Her first instinct was to freeze, but she had learned by now that if anything came with Ruby, and was also animal-shaped, that usually meant that they held little to no interest in her at all.

    “I don’t know if I’ll ever get used to that.” Lena gestured with a flourish of her hand towards the cat currently rolled onto its back. Ruby had smiled and leaned downed to rest her head upon the soft undercoat of its belly.

    “No big deal. My mom took a while to get used to it, too.”

    “Is that right?” Lena asked as she reached for the dried husk of a walnut.

    “Yeah, I sorta came into my powers a little early. She learned to get along with everyone after a while.”

    “How early do you mean?”

    That knowledge-seeking part of Lena’s brain didn’t exactly have an off switch. She almost regretted asking, feeling as though she were being too dishonest about her true origins, which included her line of work.

    “Um,” Ruby chewed around a bite of her apple, “I was a baby still, so I don’t remember much, but she used to tell me that I’d draw a flock of ravens every time I cried a lot.” She huffed a bit of a laugh then, “Said they’d just sit there in our windowsills and judge her.”

    “Hm. That’s actually fascinating.” Lena bobbed her head enthusiastically. She popped a small piece of walnut in her mouth and tucked it into her cheek, “Would you find it interesting to know that a flock of ravens is called an ‘unkindness’?”

    Ruby stopped her idle scratching of the large cat’s belly and quirked her chin, “An unkindness? Why?”

    “More than likely has to do with the use of imagery in novels. That would be my best guess.”

    “Doesn’t seem fair.” Ruby replied.

    “Oh?”

    “Ravens are the kindest birds I’ve ever met. Blue jays on the other hand… huge assholes.”

    Lena chuckled, “Language.”

    “What?” Ruby laughed along, “My mom lets me cuss.”

    “I rather doubt that.”

    “She’d like you, I think.” Ruby’s voice turned sad again.

    Lena had, up until now, been respectful of Ruby’s personal life and wished not to dredge up old wounds, but she was concerned now. There was this twelve-year-old girl now, who was —for all Lena knew— completely alone in the world. She couldn’t stomach any more of her avoiding the subject.

    “Ruby, I want to ask you about what happened to your mother. How long have you been separated?”

    “Mm, a week maybe.”

    “A week? That’s- well, it’s only just recently that she’s gone missing, then. How did that happen?”

    Ruby sat up and squirreled a peculiar look upon her face. “You know, I really can’t figure you out sometimes. Why don’t you just get inside my head and look for yourself?”

    “I—” Lena fumbled, “Well, that would just be rude, now, wouldn’t it?”

    Ruby shrugged. “I guess.” The cougar laying on its side now gave a toothy yawn and closed its eyes again.

    Lena decided to make an educated guess. One that made her feel absolutely sick to her stomach. “Someone took her, didn’t they?”

    Ruby nodded.

    Oh, no… please, no.

    “That’s why I made you run so much- I’m sorry.” Ruby hiccupped past a teary-eyed sob, “Livewire exposed us- and- I was scared that we’d be next—”

    “Ruby, hey…” Lena swallowed down her fear of the enormous cats lounging about their bedding and scooted closer to Ruby, hugging her around the shoulders, “We’re safe because of you. I owe you so much because of that.” Lena’s heart ached for her. She wished so much for her to stop crying. Suddenly, she had use of those boxes that lived inside her mind once again. “Listen, why don’t you tell me what you and your mom would be doing right now if she were here? Where would you be? What would you be doing?”

    Ruby sniffled and sat up straight again. The cougar beside her had nuzzled closer, also wishing to console her as best as they could. “Um… I dunno.” She answered.

    “Try and think about it.”

    “We- I guess we’d be living somewhere else. A farm, maybe. We’d have room for everyone who wanted to stay,” She reached back to scratch at more of the fur which surrounded her, “And she would cook things that smell really, really good- and we wouldn’t be afraid anymore.”

    “That sounds very nice.” Lena replied.

    “What about you?” Ruby asked, looking upwards at Lena now with full, glistening eyes.

    “I, well—”

    “I bet you’d live somewhere really cool. And I bet it would look super clean, too. You’re kind of a neat freak, you know.”

    Lena laughed out loud at that. “Am I?”

    “Understatement of the year.”

    “I’ll take that as a compliment, then.”

    “So…?”

    “So.” Lena bowed her head forward in thought and pulled some of her hair behind her ear whenever it fell forward. She remembered the way that Kara had watched her so intently each and every time she did that. Her heart gave a great and awful pang again to miss her so deeply. There returned her unyielding concern that Kara was still back at the compound. Alone. Probably thinking that Lena had found all the answers she needed and was content just to leave her down there to rot… Their last conversation had been so dour. It had left quite the hole in her heart. Torn between finding more of the truth and running back towards the lies was a constant source of dread. Lena threw herself into her boxes again. “I suppose we would be somewhere that looks less like a farm, and more like a very big aquarium.”

    “…What’s an aquarium?”

    “Something that houses fish, basically.”

    “Isn’t that what the ocean is for?”

    Lena smiled. “It’s a long story. Would you like to see an aquarium one day?”

    “Sure,” Ruby shrugged again, “I guess. Fish in big bowls. Sounds rad.” Ruby nibbled one last time around the core of her apple and then looked back at Lena, “What are they like?”

    “Aquariums?”

    “No, your person.”

    “My—” Lena blinked, “My person, right. Well… she’s… very smart.” She smiled almost instinctively then, “And kind, as well. And so charming that you would think she came straight out of a mystery novel, making you altogether unsure if she were actually the hero or the villain…”

    “Yeah, but is she cool?”

    “…Cool?”

    “I mean,” Ruby gestured vaguely with her hands, “What does she do?”

    “Oh, I see.” Lena smiled again and took a small look around at their surroundings, “Well, I wouldn’t be at all surprised if she could lift this building right over our heads.”

    “No way!” Ruby gasped excitedly, “Wow.”

    “Yeah.” Lena agreed, “She’s super cool.”

    “Where is she?”

    There went that stone-like plunking feeling from her heart dropping straight towards the floor again. “She’s…” Lena frowned, “Waiting for me. At home, I mean.”

    “Back at Three Sisters?”

    It felt so much worse now to continue lying to this innocent child. Lena lowered her voice and sighed, “Yes.”

    “And you’re sure you don’t want to go back yet?”

    “I- erm, well… There’s still some things I have to do here, actually.”

    Ruby yawned and leaned back down upon the cat-shaped bed that slept soundly near her knees. “Okay.” Her eyes drooped heavily, and Lena pulled the small, tattered sheet from the mattress to drape it across her.

    “We’ll figure out what to do in a little bit. Get some more rest.”

    “M’kay.”

     

     

    “You must understand, if we’re seen—”

    “Do you regret each of your decisions so readily? Or have I overwhelmed you?”

    “…No.”

    “Then tell me you would not regret meeting me here, and I will do as you ask. I arrived with such haste only to see you wishing to flee again. How many times must we do this?”

    The sharp pricks of hedge leaves stuck into the tender backs of her arms. Lena was pressed up against a tall shrubbery, and she was shaking nearly enough to uproot it entirely.

    “I should tell you that it would be impossible to regret anything in the form of you, Kara.”

    “Do you trust me?”

    “I do.”

    “Then keep still, will you? You tremble like spider’s silk.”

    Lena felt so very light-headed. Her panted breath was surely working its way down even past the tight knot of Kara’s elaborate cravat. The warmth she felt just from the touch of their cheeks was enough to send her reeling. Every inch of Lena’s skin was alight with a fire completely unknown to her. It sent the fiercest of vapors straight to her head, and her eyes fluttered rapidly then with her sudden faintness.

    “Kara…”

    “Hush, now.”

    The brushing of Kara’s lips over the steady pulse in her neck felt like forever held inside of a second. Lena had wanted nothing more in her entire life than to be kissed. And not just by anyone—

    Lena felt a shift of some kind underneath her. Whatever soft surface lying beneath her head began to growl.

    The rumble of it shot her eyes wide open; it didn’t take long to remember that she was snuggling an apex predator at least twice her size. The cougar she had been using as a pillow slunk upwards and onto all fours then, curling its whiskered upper lip into a well-placed snarl. Lena felt that she truly might faint after all. At least she was already on the floor. Regency-era Kara was nowhere around.

    Beside her, Ruby snapped awake as if someone had shouted directly into her ear. “What—” She blinked her eyes clumsily open, one alternating with the other as if she were still half-asleep.

    “What’s happening?” Lena whispered.

    Ruby scuttled to her knees and then crouched her way across the small room with several empty bedframes to look out the nearest window. The sound of slamming vehicle doors got Lena’s full attention then. Ruby turned back and stared hard at the cats prowling around in a readied circle, “You have to go away now.”

    The cougars seemed rooted in their protectiveness over Ruby. She wanted them to run away for some reason, and they appeared to want to stay.

    “Go on! I mean it!” Ruby hissed. Reluctantly, the cats obeyed. A sad -yowl- marked their exit, and Ruby looked as though she might cry.

    There came a loud -bang- as soon as they were gone— a boot against a boarded-up door. People were pouring inside and they were wasting little to no time in doing so. Lena gathered up her rapier and was busily tying it around her waist when Ruby ducked suddenly beneath the window again. Lena mimicked her every move. What graced Lena’s ears next was a familiar sound. The static interchange between two short-wave radios. A specific frequency, no doubt… Lena dared to peek just through the broken glass of the window and saw the same, squared back end of a truck. Same canvas bed cover. Same all-terrain tires.

    “Seekers…”

    Ruby was near to hyperventilating where she sat on her feet beside Lena, “We have to get out of here—”

    “What are seekers doing all the way out here?”

    “What?” Ruby gave her a confused look then, “What are you talking about?”

    “I—”

    “Those are the assholes that took my mom.”

    Finally. Lena had what she needed… Even though it was actually the last thing that she wanted: Confirmation.

    It damn near killed her just to hear it.

    “Are- are you sure? They could have just—”

    “Why’d you call them that? And how come you’re a meta who doesn’t seem to know who those people are?”

    “Because—”

    “That’s the Children of Liberty- What is wrong with you?” Ruby’s eyes shot toward the breaking down of another door. They were dangerously close to being found, and Lena began to panic.

    “Ruby- Ruby—” Lena took her hands to each side of Ruby’s face, directing her eyes forward again, “Listen very carefully to me,” Voices could be heard in the next room now, “I want you to slip out the back and no matter what happens, do not come after me. Okay?”

    Ruby seemed to blank on what to say for a moment. Her jaw simply traveled up and down and then her eyes began to well with tears. “What are you gonna do?” She began to cry.

    “I’m going to make sure you’re safe. Promise me you won’t do anything.”

    “No- please, don’t leave me—”

    “Promise me.”

    Ruby could hardly answer for all of her sobbing. “I promise.”

    Lena threw her arms around Ruby then and squeezed her as tightly as she could. She poured everything that a child like herself never had, and everything that a child like Ruby truly deserved into it, hoping beyond hope that one day they might meet again. If the world were only fortunate enough to have more Rubys in it, then surely it couldn’t be so bad of a place then, could it? How horribly her father had lied to her… Lena didn’t know if she would ever be able to forgive him.

    “Please don’t,” Ruby pleaded, “Lena, they’ll kill you—"

    “What’s your mother’s name?”

    “What—?”

    “Your mother’s name,” Lena looked over her shoulder quickly towards the men still left standing outside, “Quickly.”

    Ruby sniffled, “Samantha. Samantha Arias.”

    Lena looked at Ruby one last time and backed away, slowly turning for the door, “Whatever happens, Ruby, just remember… I am your friend.”

    There grew a devastated look upon Ruby’s face all of a sudden. “You’re not a meta, are you?”

    This was no doubt going to shred any available trust that Ruby still managed to keep. What a terrible, terrible thing, Lena thought. She stood up and quickly took herself to the door, rerouting what sadness she felt into something that she could use, like contempt. Lena moved swiftly towards the front of the building and when she placed her hands upon the last door which hung pitifully upon one hinge, she ran straight into the scratchy hook-and-loop front of a tactical vest. She shrieked, and then smacked the heel of her hand right above the seeker’s sternum. He gave a surprised “oof” and then caught her by her swinging arms.

    “It is about time you showed up!” She shouted.

    “Ma’am—” The seeker jawed openly, shocked, “How—”

    “Don’t just stand there, get me out of here!”

    “Sorry, ma’am, it’s just that we’ve been looking for you for days—”

    “Well, congratulations, you found me.”

    The seeker adjusted the chin strap of his helmet and leaned to look past Lena and into the next room, “We got word of a couple metahumans lighting up a town not far from here- How is it that you came to be here, if I may ask?”

    Lena quickly shifted her position and went to block his view, “I thought I’d take a stroll.” She replied sarcastically, “Really, now, do you think I just wandered all the way here because I felt like it? I got myself lost. Imagine how it will sound when your crew of what- half a dozen skilled seekers can find anything in the world except for the Keeper’s daughter. Hm?”

    That certainly changed the man’s tune. He clicked his heels together and gave a stiff salute. “Yes ma’am. Right this way.”

    A shrill whistle rang out a second later, no doubt to call his team back from the building. Lena chanced a quick glance back towards the window where she and Ruby had hidden themselves behind… There was Ruby’s face. Upon it, a look of utter betrayal. Lena yanked her elbow free when one of the seekers aimed to help her into a seat of the truck and instead of punching him directly in the side of his neck like she wanted to, she shoved her fist into a pocket of her dirt covered pants. There the delicate chain of her necklace came to be wrapped around the tip of her finger. Surprised, Lena pulled it free right down to its emerald teardrop. She clapped a hand over her mouth once the truck began to drive away, and she turned in her seat one last time to look for Ruby…

    But she was gone. In her heart, Lena doubted that Ruby would ever forgive her.

    After all, why should she?

     

     

    Her return had lacked its expected fanfare. Her father hadn’t even come out to greet her.

    He seemed to be preoccupied with his usual flock of overachieving scientists, bleating around his ankles every chance they could get. So many of his staff wished to prove their work superior to that of their peers; it was nauseating, to say the least. Half of them were eggheads that could hardly strain an original thought from between all that lumpy, mostly useless gray matter. The other half were worth their weight in research and development, but were too afraid of overstepping, for some reason. Her father had one desire, and one desire only. To be the face of the new world. He thought himself a savior…

    Except now Lena understood what that truly meant.

    It was difficult going anywhere on the compound without a shadow, she realized. After having been escorted rather hastily to her room, where she showered, ate, and dressed, she tried and failed at least three times to leave it again of her own free will.

    There was always a sentry there in the hall, interested in whether or not that she needed an additional escort. It was only around three in the morning that she was finally able to slip away and hurry herself towards the nearest detainment building. There were several corridors which possessed empty cells, and those that seemed to have occupants paid her no mind as she whispered the name of Ruby’s mother beside each door. It pained her greatly to be forced to give up, but she would more than likely be missed by the morning bell, as well as the sentry that was sure to be stationed outside her bedroom door. She merely breezed past him in the hallway with a dangerous narrowing of her eyes once he realized that he’d been duped. Instead of balking his surprise, he set his own eyes forward across the hall again and pretended not to have seen anything. The smart ones she liked. Lena was pleased, at the very least, not to have to injure him.

    In what felt like a mere blink, Lena lifted her head away from the drool spot on her pillow once there was a loud knock at her door. She groaned and sat herself up onto her elbows.

    “Coming…” She sighed.

    Two sentries this time, thumbs tucked behind their flak vests with stern looks upon their faces.

    “The Keeper wishes to speak with you.”

    “Oh, are you quite sure he has the time?” Lena’s poor attitude certainly wouldn’t do her any favors, she knew that. Regardless of how she felt, she grumbled and positioned the small silver belt buckle at her waist to be centered with the snap of her jeans. Her navy-colored short sleeved shirt was a bit wrinkled from where she had laid in bed so tiredly, but that was pretty low on her list of worries as far as the day was concerned. Her father was a harsh man, of course. He would no doubt have some harsh words for her. “Alright, gentleman. Let’s go.”

    Surely, she thought that they would lead her across the facility and straight into his office, but instead they wheeled her around towards the north end of the compound where many of the heavy equipment and tanker trucks were kept. Was she leaving? There were no convoys scheduled until another year from now, at least. Her trepidation worsened when her escort stopped just shy of the north bay door. She saw her father stand from a chair there and made his way slowly with his cane towards her. He didn’t need so much assistance with walking most of the time. Only whenever he was ill, which he was more frequently than he would have people believe. The sentries went to stand near the door and her father merely stared at her.

    Silent.

    And so far beyond disappointed.

    A shiver ran up Lena’s spine. Lionel Luthor’s silence was almost worse than his wrath.

    “You left some blood on my sword.” He said.

    “I…” Lena caved instantly, her spine immediately gelatinous, “I’m sorry.”

    Instead of a response, Lionel pinched one of his black leather gloves from his fingertips and then slapped her across the face with his bare hand— hard enough to thrust stars into her eyes. A single tear tracked out from the corner of each eye as she looked back at him, completely appaled. Her neck almost hurt for how severely she’d been struck. She couldn’t believe it.

    “Daddy—”

    “Don’t you dare. You will listen and say nothing, you ungrateful child.”

    Lena ignored him. “What are we doing to these people?”

    Lionel, exercising whatever patience he seemed to have left for his only daughter, took a slow breath through his nose and then exhaled with his jaw clenched. The leathery -squeak- of his other gloved hand where it squeezed the silver pommel of his cane almost made her jump. “They are not people. You would do well to remember that.”

    “Their DNA is 99% human- I would say that categorically counts as ‘people’.”

    “Yes, but do you see any humanity left in them at all?” Lionel seethed through the bitter yellow of his front teeth, evidence of his years’ long cigar habit, “The way they destroy one another, and continue to destroy what’s left of this world—”

    “Where do they go after they leave here?”

    Lionel took the rounded pommel of his cane quickly to the notch that lived just below Lena’s throat and shouted, “Enough!” His eyes were dark, full to the brim with rage now, “Your insolence will cost you dearly should you continue your disrespect. I expect results from you, Lena, and all you give me is arrogance, and useless data with which I could fill entire rooms of this place!”

    Lena elected to stay silent that time, feeling herself swallow thickly behind the hard end of her father’s cane. She had never seen him so beside himself with anger before.

    “I have decided simply to help you, instead of punishing you for your misguided odyssey. My word is gospel upon these stations, and you will heed every single syllable of it, do you understand me?”

    “Y-yes, sir.” Lena shook too fiercely to nod; she could only tuck her chin once.

    Lionel took his cane back to the floor finally and pulled his glove back over his hand, taking an extra moment swipe some of his long, still-graying hair from his face. Then he turned towards the sentries posted at the door. “Bring it up.” He said.

    One of the men pulled at the large chain of the overhead door while the other ushered Lena beneath it, pushing her stiffly along the way towards the flat surface of a loading dock. There were a few of the facility’s water tanks staged there towards the side of the building…

    …and hanging above one of them from an oversized crane hook was Kara.

    Lena gasped and felt her feet move as if they would break out into a run. She was caught by the back of her neck that time and held in place.

    “Let’s see if any of your notes can be validated with some rigorous testing, shall we?” Lionel said snidely.

    Lena fought to twist around and face him, “Daddy- daddy, please! You can’t possibly do this- its torture."

    He merely smiled back at her and shielded his eyes from the sun, “No, my darling. This is scientific inquiry.”

    Then, with a wave of his hand, the crane arm holding Kara up gave a violent bounce just once, and shook her loose of its hook.

    “No!”

    Kara fell with a splash that nearly drenched the people standing nearby upon the tank’s elevated catwalk. Some of the sentries within earshot were heard laughing over the sounds of Lena’s screams, and it wasn’t until she had thrown caution to the wind that she decided to bring her heel down hard upon her captor’s instep, and additionally, her fist directly into his groin. He doubled over with a gagging sound from his mouth, and finally, she was able to sprint towards the crane.

    “Put it down!” She screamed, frantic and wild eyed, “Let the crane into the water, please!”

    Every sentry standing on that crane platform looked back towards the loading dock, directly at Lionel.

    “Do it, now!” Lena tore her way up the metal grated steps towards the crane cab and landed her knee into the gut of the first who tried to stop her, and then her elbow beneath the jaw of the second. Both of them dropped like flies. She took her hands inside the doorframe of the cab then and growled through her teeth at the man operating the arm, “You put that hook down into the water right now, or so help me, I will make you wish that you had never been born.”

    One of the sentries rolling around the platform behind her wheezed something akin to the words “just do it”, and so the operator did. The crane immediately rocked forward slightly from its base once Kara seemed to latch onto it. The slow movement of its long arm drawing her back out of the tank felt like hours. Kara was able to hold on long enough just to be hoisted over the side and above the dirt, where she eventually fell hard enough to shake the ground beneath the crane itself.

    Lena raced back down the steps and pushed with all of her might to help Kara right herself onto her knees. Her wrists were bound with their usual manacles, and the shock collar around her neck appeared to be intact as well. She was dressed only in her chest binder and sweatpants, and she choked violently for several moments to wretch all the water from her lungs. Once she was able to breathe, she looked up through the drenched curtain of her burnished gold hair and gawked, almost as if she were shocked.

    “Lena—”

    “I’m here- I came back.” Lena did her best to lift one of Kara’s arms up with her own, but it moved very little for all of her effort. “You have to stand up now. Hurry.”

    “Lena…”

    “Stand up, Kara.”

    Kara was doing her best to wipe the water from her eyes with the backs of her hands when a sentry peeled towards them quickly. Reactively, Kara stepped in front of Lena, which only earned her a well-timed jolt from her collar.

    “Stop!” Lena shouted at him and raced to yank the remote control free from his deliberately cruel hands, “Enough of this!”

    Lionel’s voice carried from the loading dock right about then, “Let them through- it’s alright. I believe we’ve all had enough excitement for one day. Take her down, Lena, and then come straight to my office.”

    Lena pushed at the small of Kara’s back to get her walking forward. She felt that she would feed any sentry their teeth if they dared to so much as step in their way now. That, and she would make damn sure that her father would rue the day he ever decided to turn his daughter into a weapon.

    Everyone gave them a wide berth all the way into the building again, where she eventually turned Kara towards the freight elevator and then closed the long gate over it. It shifted with a jump beneath their feet once it began to move, and then they were on their way down. Remote stuffed into her back pocket, Lena turned to face Kara and watched as the rows of daylight began to draw away from them now with their slow descent.

    Kara swallowed hard and then narrowed her gaze towards the plane of Lena’s cheekbone, inspecting it with some manner of concern. She reached up and swiped gingerly with the pad of her thumb over the small, tender cut there. “What did you do?” She asked.

    Little did Lena know that everything she had experienced inside the past three days would terminate right then and there with an awful sob, and then another. Her face twisted with that awful dam’s worth of regret and just as it broke, she leapt forward onto her toes and threw her arms around the tops of Kara’s shoulders.

    Kara seemed surprised at first, but Lena allowed her space enough to loop her arms over and to hold her in return. Lena felt all of the pressure behind her crying suddenly lifted, and simply let herself be held there while the elevator continued its long, downward trip. She turned her uninjured cheek to lay flat at the still damp pectorals that lived above Kara’s breasts. She was so warm— but unsurprisingly so, as Kara’s baseline temperature was frequently read in the area of one hundred degrees Fahrenheit. Not a significant increase in comparison to human physiology, but just enough to be noticeable. Lena was much more interested in the way that every part of Kara’s body seemed to be so rock solid and yet also so soft at the same time.

    “What happened to you?” Kara asked, lifting Lena’s gaze by her chin.

    Lena still didn’t have the heart to answer. Where would she even begin? The secret operating room? Ruby? Her first true peacemaker’s duel? It was all Lena could do just to slip her chin away and to place her cheek back down upon Kara’s heated chest. It was comforting, due in large part to knowing what lay right behind it. She could feel it thumping there rhythmically into her ear, speaking to her as if it were a language all their own. Lena sighed and wrapped her arms more tightly around Kara. Where they could reach, anyway. She was carved in a very distinct “v” shape from her deltoids all the way to her lower abdominals.

    Lena’s voice was quiet whenever she finally decided to speak. They would only have so much time before they could stand like this inside the next elevator…

    “It doesn’t matter. I’m getting you out of here.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Hooray for holiday weekends!

    Chapter 9: To the Infinite

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Nine: To the Infinite

     

    The sound of birds chirping was nice.

    Kara took in a deep breath, this time no longer afraid that she was on her way towards her imminent death. The chaos of being ripped from her cell and then paraded onto a crane hook had been the most unfortunate part of her stay… Humans certainly didn’t lack imagination for being so poorly propagated over the course of two centuries. If one thing remained true, it was that cruelty possessed no end, and no beginning. It simply ran free in all living things. Except birds, of course. Kara took her eyes to the sky again and sighed. No sign of Peck.

    “Walk slower, please?” Lena whispered where she walked directly beside Kara.

    “Oh- sorry.”

    “We won’t have another chance like this for a while- I had to make a pretty strong case just to get you out here.”

    Kara averted her eyes from the nearest guard tower. Three sentries posted there had their weapons poised and at the ready. “So, what are we looking for, exactly?” She asked somewhat tentatively.

    “I don’t even know, to be honest. I was hoping to leave that up to you.”

    “Me?”

    “Yes,” Lena sidled up a bit closer, “Preferably something that ranks very low on a scale of necessary violence. See that gate over there?”

    Kara made sure to look with her eyes and not with a turn of her head. “Yeah…”

    “Avoid that at all costs.”

    The guard tower overlooking that point of the perimeter seemed to be heavily defended. A good sign of a main entry point… if the cannon-end of a large-scale artillery gun was any indication, that is. It stuck upwards at a forty-five-degree angle into the wilderness beyond.

    “You really think an anti-tank mortar will stop me?”

    “I didn’t say that. I would simply thank you to choose a path with a bit more subtlety.”

    Kara held a chuckle in her throat at that. “I don’t know if you’ve noticed, but I’m not exactly what you’d call subtle.”

    “No, you’re a tank.”

    The shock collar around Kara’s neck was beginning to annoy with its particularly clunky design. An optical condition of their afternoon stroll, according to Lena. Maintaining the appearance that Kara was still very much a prisoner was paramount. Kara chanced a quick shift of her eyes to the left and then kicked idly at a small rock in their path.

    “Question…”

    “Hm?”

    “Is there a blacksmith here?”

    Lena puzzled her brow for a moment and then directed Kara over towards a small patch of grass. “Yes, I think you know there is. Why do you ask?”

    “Just curious.” Kara replied nonchalantly, “Pretty decent work on the bars in my cell, that’s all.”

    “She’s very skilled, I’ll say that.”

    Kara huffed quietly and spoke beneath her breath, “You have no idea.”

    “I’m sorry?”

    “Nothing.”

    “Alright…” Lena chose to carry on then with a bit more of her arm looped inside of Kara’s. There wasn’t exactly much ground that the watchful eyes of sentries didn’t cover, but when there was, Lena took every available chance to run her hand up the length of Kara’s forearm. She seemed to like to squeeze there on occasion, feeling out the true extent of the muscle that lied beneath Kara’s sleeve. “See anything, yet?”

    “Huh?” Kara asked, distracted.

    “Really, are you even paying attention?”

    “I am!”

    “I’m serious- I’m still at a loss on what to do here.” Lena huffed another distressed breath, “Kara, I don’t know if I can do this…”

    Kara dared to look behind them and then shuffled in front of Lena, taking a moment to look the other woman directly in the eyes. She looked nervous, understandably so. “Yes, you can.” Kara encouraged her, “I know all of this seems so daunting, and that you might doubt whether you have what it takes to go against everything you’ve ever known… but I believe that you do.” She nodded then to further emphasize her point, “I believe in you.”

    Lena softened finally and gave an inquisitive upturn of her brow. “You do?”

    Kara smiled back. “If anything, just remember that it’s impossible to swim for new horizons until you have courage to lose sight of the shore. That should keep you on the right track.”

    “Oh—” Lena sat her hands at her hips all of a sudden and appeared to think hard, “Oh, I know this one.”

    Kara shrugged and then turned to continue their slow pace. “I’m sure you do.”

    “I do, though!”

    “Mhm.”

    “It’s, erm—” Lena skipped quickly to catch up, “Frost, right? Robert Frost?”

    Kara pulled in a sharp breath through her teeth. “Mmm… sorry. William Faulkner.”

    “Dammit.”

    “Wow, your little walkabout taught you all sorts of things, didn’t it?”

    Lena rolled her eyes.

    “Cuss some more- it makes me feel all fuzzy inside.”

    Lena then gave a smart pinch at the bend of Kara’s elbow in lieu of a laugh.

    “Ouch!”

    “Oh, really now,” Lena was still chuckling, “You can supposedly deflect bullets like they’re nothing but a pinch hurts?”

    “My feelings- yeah.”

    “My goodness, you are a lot to take in.”

    Kara piped up with an appreciative hum from her throat. “Hm. I guess so.” Then her feet rambled to a stop once again— there were large drainage pipes along this part of the perimeter that seemed to lead beyond the wall. The steel grates that capped each mouth would do little to nothing to stop someone like Kara. “There- that culvert over there, what do you think?”

    Lena pulled at Kara’s arm again to keep them both walking. “That direction leads south.”

    “Yes, dear. I can see the sun.”

    “I suppose it would be the best route…” Lena thought out loud after a moment, “You won’t have any problem getting through?”

    “Tank, remember?”

    Lena bowed her head then with a nod. “Okay. Then that’s what we’ll work with.”

    The sound of a bell through loudspeakers all of a sudden made every bird in the near vicinity burst into flight. Kara could only guess that it was time for dinner, mostly by the way that her stomach growled. Being reacquainted with life aboveground did wonders to reset her internal clock— there were never any indications of the time whilst she was sequestered to her cell.

    “So…” Kara took yet another deep breath through her nose and tilted her face towards the feeling of sunshine again, taking as much of it in as possible, “Feel like stopping by my place for a bite to eat?”

    “I wouldn’t mind the company.” Lena couldn’t help the dimple that imposed itself into the middle of her cheek whenever she smiled again.

    There was an apparent change in shifts with the bell, Kara noticed. Dark uniforms traveled from one post to another, exchanging quiet greetings and idle conversation.

    “How long until the next shift change?”

    Lena whispered her reply as they dodged the scowl of a heavily armored sentry, “Exactly six hours from now.”

    “Got it.”

    Once they were nearly back to the loading warehouse where Kara’s subbasement lodgings were kept, Kara witnessed a pair of men turn to follow them as they walked inside. It seemed that they chose to stay on past their shifts. Kara shook her arm gently to alert Lena, who immediately removed her hand from its perch. Lena then appeared to take advantage of the situation…

    “Pulling some overtime, gentleman?”

    “Orders were to wait for your return, ma’am.” One of them replied.

    “Very well.” She feigned a quick turn upon her heel whenever she started forward again, as if she had forgotten something, “In that case, there is a favor I might ask of you. It doesn’t seem there’s anyone available to bring a dinner tray down to the sublevel, so would you mind?”

    “Uhh—” The other sentry stammered, “We- I mean, I could see about—”

    “There’s also a new batch of beer that some of the lab techs have been brewing. Bit of a secret, actually- they were going to bring it up with the bell this evening. A shame I have to miss it, but, you know…” Lena bobbed her head towards Kara then, who only shrugged with an air of apology.

    That apparently got both of them interested.

    Kara was impressed.

    Each of the men seemed to stumble over the other on their way back out of the building, calling back promises of “they’ll only be a little while” and that they “didn’t realize how hungry they were”.

    Kara turned to look dead-on at Lena once they were inside the large, cargo-sized elevator. “Smart, but the same person always brings my dinner every night- I mean, if it’s not you.”

    “They don’t know that.”

    A small laugh went through her nose. Kara realized that she might actually miss Lena’s quick wit and somewhat dry sense of humor once she was free… She would be gone from this place and headed down south soon, back towards the people that needed her. So why did that make her so sad all of a sudden? Stockholm syndrome fit very well in this scenario, but Kara refused to believe it at first. No, Lena had to have been more than just her captor.

    Listen to yourself…

    Isn’t that exactly what a person suffering from Stockholm syndrome would think?

    Lena seemed to be busily watching the gears behind Kara’s eyes as they turned, investigating her heartsick machinations with a long-held breath. Her cheeks were awash with that gorgeous red color they so often kept, so complementary of the natural blush of her lips… Kara could tell that Lena was just as flustered. The apparent elephant in the room was a clumsy and exuberant juvenile, tripping over its large ears and trumpeting loudly just to be acknowledged.

    They remained silent right up until the door of the second elevator came open, and then they began their slow walk down the long corridor to Kara’s cell. The lights in the ceiling were spaced far enough apart that there was just a foot or so of shadow at certain lengths. In and out, they were thrust into darkness for the mere space of a breath, and before long, Lena suddenly stopped walking as if she’d been all but glued to the floor. The next thing Kara knew, her momentum was used against her and somehow her back ended up against the wall. Lena was very quickly upon her— each hand pinning Kara’s arms to keep her there with some small amount of urgency. As if she could actually hold her there… Kara was tickled with the thought. Seconds later, she became overwhelmed with the situation itself.

    The look upon Lena’s face could have stopped the world from turning.

    “What are you doing?” Kara asked, wreathed in shadow.

    “This is the only blind spot on the entire level.” Lena replied, “Kara, I need you to do something for me.”

    “What do you mean? I dunno what to—”

    “I need you to kiss me.”

    Kara nearly choked.

    “I need you to kiss me because, oh- honestly…” Lena blustered shamefully for a split-second, “Because I’m terrified and I need some of your courage.”

    “You think courage lies in a kiss?” Kara asked as she forced down a smile.

    “Where else?”

    Kara took a few deep and contemplative breaths with that. It was hard to see clearly there in all of the dark of their available shadow, but she considered it. She more than considered it, actually. For once in her life, she was simply dying to do exactly as she was asked. She just needed to be sure of one thing first…

    “I won’t force you—”

    “Okay.” Kara felt the breath from Lena’s sigh land at the point of her chin.

    “…Really?”

    “Yeah, but on one condition.” Kara lifted her arms and looped them around Lena’s waist, catching the other woman equally off guard. Her manacles jangled together quietly where they rested against the small of Lena’s back, and Kara pulled her close— so close that she could feel the violent pounding of Lena’s heart right through her own chest. The sound of the elevator coming to life again at the front of the corridor hastened her reply, “You tell me the real reason you want me to kiss you, and I will.”

    Lena began to stammer, “I- I just—”

    “You can say it.”

    “What?” Lena finally dropped all of her pretense with a huff, “That I’m some silly little girl who’s so far beyond obsessed that it’s all she can do just to dream about being kissed by you?”

    Kara softened her expression, “You’re allowed to want things.”

    “Fine!” Lena made a move to dart her eyes towards the slowly approaching elevator, anxious and nearly out-of-time, “I want you to kiss me, Kara...” She swallowed thickly and then leapt at the chance to add, “…please.”

    Kara snapped forward all of a sudden and pressed her lips against Lena’s, taking them both by surprise. It was that word again, sent like a spear straight through the very middle of her heart.

    Please.

    All of the tension in Kara’s body soon began to melt. Time itself could have waxed full into eons of changing shapes, colors, sounds, and any other number of celestial ingredients that made a universe a living, breathing, entity of unimaginable quality. Surpassing all notions of the human experience and what makes “life” so unbearably vivid and ludicrous, Kara felt that it was only dust that inhabited the space around them at that very moment. Heavenly detritus filled her lungs, and she was all the happier for it. Suddenly, they were no longer that of one human and one remnant pressed up against one another and gasping for every last available molecule of oxygen…

    They were the silent expanse between one thought and another. The ether between realms.

    Kara felt that she might refrain from slanting her mouth to deepen their impassioned kissing, being slightly hesitant to lay more of herself upon Lena than she might be ready for, but Lena seemed to rattle apart at her bones whenever Kara went to pull away from her. Her fingers skirted upwards and into the fleece of Kara’s hair and they scratched there gently, begging her to come back. Kara obliged. The subtle -smack- that drew upon the last touch of their lips gave way to an eventual breath, and Kara found that she was dizzy— almost gloriously so.

    Lena trembled as she placed a palm flat at the notch between Kara’s collarbones. “Wait for my next message, alright? I’ll have something figured out soon. I promise.”

    “Okay.”

     

     

    —Three Days Later—

     

    While it took longer than Kara would have preferred, she knew that aiming for the most opportune window held the best chance of success, as well as the least amount of resistance. She also knew that if she were to do this, then it had to be done Lena’s way.

    Kara was instructed to wait for ten minutes after Lena’s departure from her cell that evening. Counting each second was unbearable, but her path was clearly laid out in front of her. As much of it that Lena could provide, that is. Fastidiously scrawled in a hefty copy of Gardener’s Almanac 2095: A month-by-month guide for gardening enthusiasts were turns, camera locations, sentry posts, as well as the very clear and concise stipulation that Kara was to abstain from any violence whatsoever.

    There were some things which she could promise, sure… but not that. Those who stood in Kara’s way would be forcibly removed, though, she would —at the very least— attempt not to injure them in a more permanent fashion. Bruised egos she could settle for.

    Hoping that Lena had not actually forgotten to disengage the biometric security system on her way out, Kara ruefully popped the newly welded joints of her bars free and proceeded to climb through and into the other side of the room. The intrusive thought just to sit there in Lena’s chair for a second or two had almost won out, but instead, she pried her fingertips into the doorframe and pulled free the unlocked door.

    The corridor towards the elevator was empty. Once inside, she climbed through the hatch above her head and waited on top of the moving car as it ascended, just as instructed. Following the same procedure for the second elevator, Kara was able to shimmy through enough of the wall space alongside the shaft in order to drop out of a vent grate. She was quite pleased at that point; she had broken nothing and only bent one large electrical conduit out of her way.

    There stood Lena with her arms crossed near an open door, pacing a tight back-and-forth stretch right where she said she would be.

    “Not too bad.” Kara whispered as she came up behind her.

    Lena startled suddenly and patted a nervous hand over her chest whenever she spun around. “Oh, it’s you.”

    “Who else would it be? The Dalai Lama?”

    “…Who?”

    Kara shook her head. “How’s it look out there?”

    Lena palmed nervously at her forehead. “Not good.” She replied. At least she seemed dressed for the occasion; her shirt was a midnight color as well as the cargo pants she wore. Both good choices for moving under the cover of darkness. “We don’t have as much time as I thought we would—”

    “What do you mean?”

    “They split up the returning party for some reason- they’re doing the intake in two shifts instead of one. Your window’s been cut in half. I’m sorry.”

    “Shit.” Kara peeked through the door towards the outside. There was the water tank she’d been dropped into like a lead weight. She hated that fucking tank. “Okay,” She shook her head, “It doesn’t matter. Where’s the forge?”

    Lena finally turned around to face her and blustered, “What? Why—”

    “I just need to know where the forge is, that’s all. I can make my way out after that.”

    “No, you can’t- the forge is all the way around the other side of the compound—”

    “I don’t have a choice.” Kara hunched around the doorframe one last time to make sure the coast was clear, and then she moved to take the first step.

    “Wait—!” Lena put her hands out, “No- you’ll be caught. Your opening is on this side, Kara. We’ve planned this.”

    “I’m going.”

    “You won’t have time!”

    “Like I said,” Kara insisted with another step, “I don’t have a choice.”

    “Dammit- stop!” Lena immediately corrected the volume of her voice and then ran in front of Kara, placing her hands there at the firm tops of her broad shoulders. She took a slow breath through her nose and spoke more calmly that time, “Fine. If you’re so set on getting there, then at least let me show you the way.”

    “That would be both helpful and kind.” Kara bobbed her head up and down enthusiastically.

    Lena gave her a half-hearted scowl in return. “Now is not the time for you to be charming.”

    Kara suddenly dipped forward and placed a kiss at the center of Lena’s cheek, and Lena’s ire instantly faded. Kara smiled, “How’s that for charming?”

    “You’ve had so much practice it should almost be criminal.”

    “Thank you.”

    “That wasn’t a compliment.”

    “…Wasn’t it?”

    Lena gave an impatient roll of her eyes and then pushed Kara towards the far exit, away from their previously intended path. “Come on.”

    It took several stops to dart between passing patrols and a few seemingly aimless sentries, but they both eventually reached a point they couldn’t surpass without Lena providing a distraction. The forge was within running distance, but much to Kara’s disappointment, she couldn’t see any signs of life coming from the inside. Where there should have been fire, there was only the chug of moistened smoke.

    Damn…

    The place seemed to be quenched and shut up for the night. Kara finally slipped through and was rummaging around the small piles of railroad spikes and rounded sheets of pre-formed armor when Lena jogged inside to meet her, heaving one heavy breath after another. There wasn’t a damned thing that seemed to boast of Alex’s handywork, not from what Kara could find in so little time, anyway. Anyone could pound a sword pommel.

    “This isn’t fair—” Kara said with a vexed breath, “Why isn’t she here?”

    “Who, the blacksmith?” Lena panted with another apprehensive look back through the door, “Alright,” She caught Kara’s hand then as she passed her by to look into the adjoining room, “I want to know what’s going on.”

    “It’s—” Kara pinched her lips into a straight line once she came to a frustrated halt, “I can’t just leave her here.”

    “You can’t be serious- Who?!”

    “My sister!”

    “Your—” Lena loosened her grip of Kara’s thumb, “I- I don’t believe it… You lied to me?”

    “I didn’t lie.” Kara went to take up Lena’s hand again, feeling poorer now for having lost it. She pressed it firmly over her heart and began to confess, “I didn’t know she was here—”

    “You told me your family was all gone, that- that they were all dead.”

    “It’s…” Kara bowed her head with a nod, “It’s complicated. I didn’t know that she was alive- but it’s too much to tell you right now- please, you have to help me find her.”

    “There is no time, Kara!” Lena insisted emphatically upon each word, “Someone’s going to notice the camera feeds are down as soon as those stations are manned again, and it won’t matter who’s alive or who isn’t, because they will kill you if you’re caught out here!” She sat her hands upon her hips then, “You have to make a choice.”

    “What- now?”

    “Yes!”

    Kara’s eyes went to stare past Lena’s shoulder, “I can’t go back.”

    “Then you have to leave.” Lena begged, emphasizing her words again with every fiber of her being, “Please, I cannot stand to go on in a world where you do not exist!”

    Kara reared her head back at that, surprised. “Do you mean that?”

    “I…” Lena appeared to fluster all of a sudden, “I just mean, that—”

    A shrill whistle called out into the night air, and the violent flash of spotlights began to burst with life at different stretches along the perimeter. Kara took Lena into her arms for a moment to hug her, and then she said something that shocked even herself.

    “Come with me.”

    “What…?”

    “Come with me,” Kara cupped the side of Lena’s face, “You can get away from here- from all of this… I’ll take care of you. I swear I will.”

    Kara knew that she was only giving Lena mere seconds to answer a question that would no doubt uproot her entire life and throw it completely into chaos. She wouldn’t blame her if she declined, but to her immense surprise, Lena simply nodded her head in agreement.

    “Okay.”

    “Okay?”

    “…Yes.”

    “Really?”

    “Just, one more second and then we’ll go…” Lena looked over her shoulder towards a rack hung from top to bottom with different sword shapes. Scimitars, Falchions, even the odd Gladius or two— a double-edged blade which Kara herself had always favored for its short length and devastating maneuverability. Lena, of course, plucked the unpolished sabre from the lot and with one short jolt from the mouth of its scabbard, the blade came leaping free and went into her opposite hand with a flourish.

    Kara gawked for a moment, absentminded for the growing threat of being captured once again. “That’s hot.”

    “Alright. I’m ready.” Lena replied with a -slock- of her sword back into place.

    Fortunately, the source of the danger seemed to be emanating from the cargo facility where Kara was once housed. The sentries in search of her were branching out from there, so she and Lena stayed close to the inside wall of the compound as they worked their way back around to the culvert. Lena climbed down in front of the nearest drainage pipe first and then stepped back so Kara could clear their way.

    When Kara paused to look back one last time, Lena refocused her attention with a gentle brush of her hand, “We’ll come back for her.” Her eyes squinted towards the racing beams of spotlights, “We’ll come back for all of them.”

    “That’s a guarantee.” Kara agreed.

    “…I just realized how much I’ll miss my jellyfish.”

    Kara would have smiled if not for hammer-against-anvil beating of her heart as it forced its way into her throat. She took both of her hands then to the thick metal grate, and with three strong pulls, it peeled away from the mouth of the pipe much like that of a tin can lid. Kara waved for Lena to make her way through, “I’ll get you some more if we make it that far- now come on!”

    The searching beams of light had begun to draw closer, and Lena took that as her cue to climb into the pipe. It was just tall enough for her to stand and run inside of, but Kara had to duck for the whole of the way until it dropped them out on the other side. Hopefully the way she had pulled the grate shut behind them would buy them some more time… They had an incredibly long journey ahead.

    Thousands of miles awaited them, across broken country and warring kinships, hostile fauna and dizzying flora. They would tread through forests, mountains, and then deserts to reach the city of Laredo; Home of wayward metahumans.

    Home of the Infinite.

     

    —End of Act I—

    Chapter End Notes

    Chapter 10: Lost, and Then Found

    Chapter Notes

    Just kidding, we'll be having three acts now. Welcome to Act II. (Psssst... I've updated the total chapter count. The outline, she be finalized.)

    Just a heads up, the POV may switch mid-chapter sometimes from here on out.

    ACT II

     

    Chapter Ten: Lost, and Then Found

     

    Lena could count less than a dozen words shared by the next morning.

    Between climbing through endless thickets of saplings and treading carefully around all the boggy earth which threatened to swallow them whole, she was surprised to still feel so alert. The forest had been crawling with men the whole night through, shouting at one another to head this way and that. The constant state of vigilance should have exhausted her, but she kept right on, hand in hand with the titan in front of her.

    “Control your breathing—”

    “Stop.”

    “This way…”

    It wasn’t until the trees began to thin and the bird song began to fade that Kara finally slowed her pace. The forward motion of Lena’s legs had become so reflexive; so mind-numbingly automatic that it hardly felt like she even existed inside of her own body after so long. It was cumbersome holding conversation at the same time— the ground being so uneven for so many kilometers.     “Would you care to try?”

    Kara took another deep breath in through her nose. “The short version is… nobody really knows how it works. It’s like we can sense it in our blood.”

    “Does it work the same with metahumans?”

    “Yeah, but it’s weaker, for some reason. With us, you just know when there’s another remnant nearby. Feels sort of magnetic, if that makes sense. It’s funny…” Kara huffed a quiet laugh.

    “What is?”

    “Some people think its magic.”

    “I can see how it might feel that way.”

    “It doesn’t scare you?”

    Lena nuzzled her brow closer to the warm curve of Kara’s neck, “No.”

    They were quiet again, all except for another few yawns. Kara’s voice soothed Lena’s last lingering moments of consciousness, “If I get too heavy just wake me up. I’d hate to crush you.”

    Lena smiled, mumbling around her reply “Duly noted.”

     

     

    —Five Days Later—

     

    Kara whistled again, as loud as she could that time.

    “Would you come down from there?” Lena shouted, “I thought we were avoiding drawing any attention!”

    “Ahh,” Kara called back, “…there’s nobody around for miles.”

    “Then what are you whistling for?!”

    Kara looked down the length of the slightly unsound broadcast tower to see Lena staring straight back up at her, both hands shielding her eyes from the sun.

    We could go anywhere…

    Just the two of us.

    It was easy to lose herself in a daydream, lately. The idea of a better life —a peaceful life— seemed so much more attainable whenever she caught herself looking at Lena. Kara shook the fantasy from her head (like always) and began to climb down.

Kara had kept them at a brisk pace of at least twenty miles a day (by her Imperial count) only allowing them to stop long enough to trade inside a few of the small, hidden villages they came upon, and to rest, and eat. She admired Lena’s newest choice of clothing; very practical in the way of pockets. Lena preferred her short-sleeved shirts but seemed to venture outside of her comfort zone as far as cargo pants and a canvas shoulder bag. People had stopped them every so often to ask about the sword that hung at her side, and many had gone as far as to beg her to trade for it, but Lena wouldn’t dare part with it. It was strange, because she could also hardly look at it for some reason. Kara grew more and more curious about the refined weapon Lena carried as the days went by, but had (for the most part) settled just to keep the other woman’s inner demons at bay. Whatever they were.

    Kara laughed, “I’m just,” She took one last glimpse at their surroundings from where she hung onto the steady A-frame of the tower, “…looking, that’s all.”

    Peck was still nowhere to be seen.

    Kara didn’t blame him… She’d been gone for months. She despaired inwardly at times, thinking that he’d probably forgotten her by now. No other friend had ever kept her so honest before. Kara knew that her bond with Peck was of the highest order; a complete privilege to which she had no doubt squandered now, having abandoned him for so long.     “Right, well, if anyone didn’t happen to hear all of your whistling, then they most certainly would have heard that.”

    “Don’t worry, you’ll point your little sword at ‘em and scare ‘em off. I have every bit of faith.”

    Lena instantly cast her eyes down towards the tops of her boots and then away from the small, empty encampment, almost as if she wished to look anywhere else besides Kara. Kara took notice of Lena’s apparent shame and tried leaning into her field of view. The way she averted her eyes every time Kara mentioned her weapon… even still.

    Alright.

    Something’s definitely wrong.

    “Why do you do that?” Kara tried asking.

    Lena pulled her lips into a thin line and shook her head. “I don’t know what you mean.”

    “You’re a terrible liar.” Kara craned her neck to give it a few loud cracks and then bent down to right a chair back onto its legs. She pointed at it then with a stern look upon her face, “Have a seat.”

    Kara crossed her legs and sat on the ground. There was a smattering of playing cards that blanketed the patchy grass… probably a full deck if she were to stop and count them. “We’re gonna do a little role reversal here.” Kara explained, “I’ll be the annoyingly smart scientist and you be the broody pain in the ass.”

    Lena laughed and then crossed her arms defiantly. “Oh, no.” She shook her head, “No, let’s not.”

    “Sit.”

    Knowing that Kara would not be moved from it, Lena slipped the strap of her shoulder bag off and then adjusted the sabre upon her hip to sit in the chair. Kara couldn’t help the smile that worked its way across her face… She also couldn’t help the small -click- sound she made from the back of her mouth once she held her hand in a “pen” shape. With her other hand laid out flat in front of herself like a pad of paper, Kara furrowed her brow and then pretended to write. “So, tell me, how exactly does a geneticist learn to swordfight?”

    “No, no, no, wait…” Kara reached out for her with a genuine look of apology and begged, “I’m sorry. I won’t tease you anymore, I promise.” She bowed her head gently, “That was kinda mean. I wasn’t thinking.”

    “What? Why not?”

    “I dunno.” Kara shrugged. “I guess it’s just an art I could never master. Plus, it would’ve helped to have been able to see the metal like she did.”

    …and knew that it was more than a mere gift that she’d been given. It was a sign from that damned and awful “God” who had forsaken her so long ago…

    That He wasn’t done with her after all.

    Chapter 11: Faith Is a Fine Invention

    Chapter Eleven: Faith Is a Fine Invention

     

    “Come on,” Kara extended a hand over the skinny gap along their switchbacked trail, “Only way to get over a fear is to face it head on.”

    Lena hummed with uncertainty, “Mmm…”

    “Trust me.”

    “I do—”

    “Do you?” Kara asked. She emphasized with her hand once more and maintained eye contact with Lena as best she could in order to keep her from looking down. The roar of the waterfall just within reach was enormous, so she shouted again, “I’ve got you- just one little leap, okay? It’ll be worth it.”

    With one last held breath, Lena closed her eyes and swallowed down the last of her hesitation. Then she opened her eyes again, set her brow with a determined stare, and jumped.

She made a slow gesture of running her hands down Lena’s back and then smiled directly at her. Calm, once again.     “Well, you’ll have to forgive me if I don’t have hundreds of years of practice throwing myself around a mountain side.”

    “Do you?”

    “Tell that to your stomach.”

    “Hm.” Kara looked down and gave a sheepish sort of grin, “We can stay a little longer if—”

    “I happen to be every once in a while.”

    “Alright,” Kara said with a similarly playful roll of her eyes, “Don’t let it go to your head, doc. Hey, wait a minute—” She stopped all of a sudden, “You’re technically a doctor, right?”

    “For metahumans, though?”

    “Metahuman physiology…” Lena quirked her chin with a curious flair, “Why?”

    “How would you like another job?”

     

     

    —Another Week Later—

     

    “It just isn’t done. Not so much anymore, anyway.”

    “Sounds almost taboo- the way you describe it.” Lena replied as she walked her fingertips along the rocky wall of the ravine.

    It had taken two extra days to find a pass through the tail end of the Cascades safe enough to traverse. The gap between the two very tall ridges was wide enough maybe for a caravan, for which Kara witnessed the broken wheels and tattered remains of at several points. It was dangerous, for sure, but if they continued along at a good enough pace, they held the chance of making it out the other side more or less unscathed.

    “Think of it like dating your classmate’s kids. Watching them grow up, and then possibly grow interested in you…” Kara shook her head, “No. There’s reasons why most remnants chose to stick together for so long.”

    “And there’s only a handful of you now?”

    “Yeah- but there were those who did try to have lives with metas, though. For a while, anyway. Slowly we all began to find one another, and people inevitably began fall in love the same way that they always do, being consenting adults and all. It just…” Kara kicked at a large rock to move it from their path, “…it rarely turned out well. To be stuck like this,” She gestured at herself, “While the person you love most in the world grows older and older, and then eventually dies- and you’re still stuck there, exactly as you are… only broken. And hopeless. And angry, given the right circumstance.”

    “That sounds horrible. Truly.” Lena replied. “Did you ever…?”

    Kara glanced at Lena, “Me?” She shook her head, “No, but—”

    “But Alex?”

    Kara nodded that time. “Alex. Yeah, she did.”

    “Is that…” Lena seemed to take a moment to reword her question more carefully, “…is that how you lost her?”

    “No, she was stronger than that. It killed her to lose Maggie, but- no, she wasn’t in with the ones who wanted to end it all. She was actually the one who tried to stop them.”

    Lena’s feet came to a slow and eventual stop, and Kara had a hard time lifting her eyes away from the ground at that point. The color of sand and granite rock had blurred her vision for so long, it was easy to fall victim to her pain again. She knew what question was coming. Lena had been dancing around it for weeks.

    “Can I ask- how did it happen? Were you with her?”

    Kara placed her hands at her hips, and when that didn’t feel comfortable enough, she crossed her arms over her chest. Finally, she lifted her gaze and then gave a quick nod. “A building fell on top of us…” Her eyes slowly dwindled down towards the ground again, “…and then, I fell on top of her.” She paused to hang her head once again, “I managed to climb out a day later, but I couldn’t take her with me.”

    “I’m not so sure, sometimes.”

    “I’m sure Alex would want you to be. Purely out of spite or not.” Lena placed her hand beneath Kara’s chin as a request for her to look upwards again, “Kara, you are the most—”

    Trapped.

    “Yes, you can.”

    “You’ll be fine. I’ll make sure of it.”

    “But what about you?”

    Kara looked down, carrying on with her slow, meandering pace. She knew not to show any sign of anxiety, as that would only cause Lena’s to worsen. No, having a stiff upper lip was the only choice. Hope would see them both through. It had to.

    Lena yawned past a smirk, “We are coming up on the 24th century, you know. It’s alright to use the metric system like everyone else.”

    “What- and lose my flair for unabashed stubbornness?” Kara joked, “Isn’t that my best quality?”

    “Absolutely not.”

    “Fair enough.”

    Despite her admittance of Lena’s freeze response being on a need-to-know basis, Kara still couldn’t seem to put it from her mind. Lena’s breaths spaced more evenly where she rested in Kara’s arms, and her mind began to wander the longer she walked. All she knew was that Lena had left one way, and then came back another. While yes, she was still that starry-eyed scientist with a heart of gold, that fire that raged inside of her seemed to have been all but snuffed out. Kara could only hope that she’d somehow find a way to reignite it. Lena’s life would depend on it.

    After a long while of bootscraping silence, Lena pulled herself away from her oncoming sleep and tilted another inquisitive eyebrow up at Kara, “Is your olfactory sense really that finely tuned?”

    “…Huh?”

     

     

    “What are they?” Lena asked with her hand flat against the glass. The slow-moving blobs of translucent jelly on the other side were mesmerizing.

    The scratchy chin hairs of her father’s beard tickled at her neck as he crouched behind her, and she giggled to hear him speak so fondly, “They’re everything we’re meant to be, darling. Self-sustaining. Impervious. Forever.”

    “Hmm- okay?” Lena mumbled somewhat incoherently, still unsure if she was truly awake or not.

    The sound of Kara’s voice quickly soothed her with a quiet hush, “Shh, we’re alright.”

    The bulk of Kara’s bicep scooted beneath her head then; arm bent at the elbow so that Lena might use it as a pillow. She did, and sighed contentedly just to feel Kara pull her close again. Lena yawned and forgot what it was that she had meant to say next.

    “Out.”

    Her father’s tone was callous. He never liked to be disturbed while he was working.

    “Out!”

    “But daddy, I wanted to—”

    Suddenly he was standing, and then he was barreling towards the two large doors that concealed his office. Her study of coral colonies and their rapid rates of self-restoration had impressed her tutor, so why not him? What else was a twelve-year-old girl trapped on a base near the Baltic Sea to do? Fish?

    Her father said nothing as he pushed her out of the way of the doors with one hand and then slammed them both closed with his other.

    Lena jumped at the sound of a small, rodent-sized screech, and then again with a grand pair of wings taking flight again. She settled as soon as she felt Kara’s sleeping breaths against the side of her face. It was easier now to bury her woes there into her neck. Nothing could harm her.

    She only wished that she could stop crying… One swipe of her hand after another went to her cheeks, but the tears just kept coming. Lena didn’t want to move homes again. She liked watching the wind blow through all of the heather that lived outside her bedroom window. Ireland was peaceful.

    It wasn’t fair. After all, “home” was where she was supposed to feel safe. Wasn’t it?

    “Mom…?”

    Lena felt her heart nearly stop. She brought a hand to her face and then touched lightly at her lips, but the voice, she realized, had not been her own. She hadn’t called out for her mother in years… Not since she was much, much younger.

    “Mommy!”

    -crack-

    The sudden impact against glass spun Lena around towards her window again, but the view from it was different this time. The pale pinkish color of her heather was gone, replaced by the deep blue and mint green hues of evergreens. With her breath held, Lena looked down towards the shards of glass lying about her bedroom floor, and there in the middle of it all lied a raven— neck bent, and with the mournful length of both wings spread wide.

    Lena opened her eyes again as the calm “kraa” and “caw” noises from high in the trees worked their way down. The sunrise gave life to everything within those first few shimmering rays of daylight, and Lena was able to see more clearly where Kara had laid them down to sleep.

Her hands laid upon several small, oval cones the size of goose eggs and she was amazed to roll them around upon the dry and brittle ground. How incredible was it that these tiny, unsuspecting seed cones would one day turn into towering giants like the ones which stood before her…

    “How do you feel?” Lena asked.

    “Mm- hungry. I mean, tired.”

    “Told ya.”

Kara was always so gentle, yet so hesitant as if she were scared to break her. Lena wasn’t afraid of her whenever they first met, and she certainly wasn’t afraid of her now.

    If you’re truly forever, then would you at least be mine?

She breathed heavily with small kisses around the front of Lena’s throat, and Lena could have died for how sensational it felt. Desperate to hand over her very foundations —right down to her last cornerstone— Lena’s shaky hands went to unsnap the button of her jeans.

    “Wait- wait, wait, wait.” Kara panted quietly, “Please,” She shook her head, “…don’t do that.”

    “Why? I—” Lena felt herself leap towards the first available explanation, “I want to. Don’t you?”

    Kara nodded and then dropped her brow tiredly upon Lena’s sternum, fastening Lena’s jeans again with slow, trembling fingers. She gulped loudly once, and then looked back up with sorrowful eyes, “I do. Believe me, I do.”

    Kara looked back down again.

    Immediately disheartened (and somewhat ashamed, as well) Lena took herself out of Kara’s lap and went to sit beside her, clutching her knees close to her chest. “It’s because of how we met…” She guessed out loud.

    “No.”

    “…No?”

    Kara rubbed at the corners of her eyes and went to pull the rest of her shirt from the waist of her pants— half of it having been subconsciously set free by Lena’s clawing hands. She sighed then and looked over to take Lena’s hand into her own. “Do you want the truth, or a lie?”

    “The truth, of course.” Lena replied quickly, though, she wasn’t so sure upon her answer after all. Maybe a lie would hurt less…

    “The truth is…” Kara sighed, “You won’t be here another century from now.”

    “I see.” Lena nodded. Disheartened, ashamed, and now incredibly selfish. When would her clumsy habit of blustering forward end? “I didn’t think about that…”

    “It’s okay, I wouldn’t expect you to.”

    Lena ran her fingers through her hair, fixing it calmly over her shoulder, and then looked up towards the tree canopy where it stood hundreds of feet above them, “Being close to you is the only thing that makes me feel less horrible.” Lena stared straight at Kara again, “I can’t stop thinking about what might happen to them…”

    “You mean the metas back at the compound?”

    “What if we’re too late by the time we find people willing to help?” Lena despaired, “Who’s even going to want to? The odds of it just seem so—”

    “I wouldn’t worry about that.” Kara replied, crossing her legs to sit straight across from Lena then.

    “Oh? How can you be so sure?”

    “We’ll have help.”

    “From wherever it is we’re going…?” Lena guessed again.

    Kara nodded. “I keep telling you just to have a little faith. You should try it.”

    Lena withered a bit with that, “From someone who previously claimed to be so adamantly non-religious, it’s hard to take suggestions like that seriously.”

    “Well, it’s… just another complicated relationship, I guess.”
