    “Do you mean to say that you’ve changed your mind?”

    “I’m not sure.” Kara shrugged with a sort of exasperation and then took her own look up towards the sky. “I hated the thought of it so much- religion- and for such a long time, too. What kind of evil thing would spread so much hate and pain and suffering around the world, and for nothing? Tell me, what kind of a loving god starves infant children and sits idly by as the world he loves so much sets itself on fire? I think about how much I hate the idea of it all, but then…”

    “Then?”

    “Then… two hundred years go by and just when it all seems said and done, and that ‘He’ never even existed to begin with, you show up.”

    Lena flustered for a moment, “…Me?”

    “Yeah.” Kara reached out to thumb lightly at Lena’s cheek, “You show up- and suddenly I’m reminded again of what humanity looks like.” She smiled, “It’s like every time I look at you, all I see is God.”

    Chapter End Notes

    I don't know what it is about the notion of "God" being so utterly romantic in fiction, specifically romance, but I sort of hate how well it fits-- seeing as how I myself am abhorrently against all religion to begin with. That's why I love writing. I'm just an aromantic gal who specializes in the deepest of purple prose, and an atheist who writes about sex and God as if they go together like bread and butter. Nothing makes sense, and that's the way I like it.

    Chapter title is another Emily Dickinson snippet. Remember when I said this one was gonna be a slowy-slow burn? Lena was right. Oof.

    Thanks for reading! <3

    Chapter 12: Buttes of Wrongness

    Chapter Notes

    TW: violence, blood

    Chapter Twelve: Buttes of Wrongness

     

    Listening to Kara speak was the highlight of her every day.

    The things that she said and the way in which she said them… It was like music to Lena’s ears. Kara was a grand storyteller, to be sure. The way that she reminisced upon the past was like being transported to a bustling square. Lena could almost hear the busy city thoroughfares in every one of Kara’s tales. It was a world not yet completely broken, but well on its way there.

    “My dad had a pretty important job- so, I didn’t get to see him all that much around that time.”

    “That must have been hard.” Lena replied where she strolled beside Kara, both of her hands clasped behind her back. She twiddled her thumbs together, hoping to ask yet another question. “But you managed to stay together after the Great Deterioration, I hope?”

    “We did.”

    “That’s good. I know the metamorphoses that you and your sister went through were difficult- I’m glad your parents were there to see you through it.”

    For some reason, it wasn’t enough to know Kara’s suffering after the end of the war. Her life-altering transformation was a painful topic, yes, but Lena wanted to know Kara’s hopes and dreams, as well. Her deepest wishes, her regrets…

    Obviously disheartened, Kara hung her head and frowned.

    “…I’m sorry.” Lena apologized.

    Anytime Kara’s family and the subject of the vaccine were both mentioned within the same breath, Lena noticed that Kara had a peculiar habit of withdrawing— almost as if she were ashamed. She also showed similar signs of emotional crises any time Lena peppered statistics into their conversations. The ones that illustrated death tolls or survival rates, anyway. To her, the lingering one percent of the human race and their evolutionary successors were fascinating subjects, but to Kara, it was all just a catastrophic reminder.

    Lena felt like kicking herself… Again.

    Change the subject.

    “I know you want to keep where we’re going a secret and I’m still okay with that- but could you at least give me some clues? You could be walking me right off the edge of the world for all I know.”

    Kara chuckled all of a sudden. “You know, people used to think that Earth was flat.”

    “Oh, sure. Back when—” Lena paused for a moment, sputtering with disbelief, “Wait, you don’t mean…?”

    “Mhm.”

    “You can’t be serious.”

    “Afraid so.”

    “Mm-mm, no,” Lena drew her lips into a thin line and then shook her head, “I know you’re just trying to distract me from learning where it is we’re going.”

    Kara shrugged.

    “I’m assuming it’s the place you call home?”

    “More or less.”

    “So, what’s it like?”

    “It’s… hot.”

    “Yes,” Lena replied, “I gathered that much with our steady southern direction.”

    “…and dry.”

    “Oh.” Lena blustered with a playfulness, knowing Kara’s guardedness all too well by now. “How much longer do you think, then?”

    “Few more weeks, at least—”

    Kara suddenly flailed forward with her hands. There was the sound of a loud -clank- to mark her fall and then a timely -thud- whenever she caught herself face down upon the ground. She buffeted a breath to turn her cheek against the dirt and decided just to rest there, seemingly unamused with the last legs of their trek through the forest.

    “Scratch that.” Kara huffed. She made a fist and started knocking along the ground in search of something.

    As per usual, Lena was confused with the vast majority of Kara’s charming one-liners and odd turns of phrase. “Scratch what?”

    Kara seemed to find what it was that she was looking for and knocked against the ground again, giving out a round of metallic -ping- sounds with a prideful smile. She brushed with a long sweep of her arm finally to clear the leafy ground cover and revealed a smooth length of steel rail. Lena squatted to mindlessly pluck a few pinecone scales from Kara’s shirt, amazed all at the same time with their most recent discovery.

    “This track probably hasn’t been used in years…” Kara explained.

    That much seemed to be clear with the shallow root systems of all the nearby sequoias growing over the railroad and beyond. The wooden crossties that lied beneath were no doubt turned to rot after being subjected to the spreading forest for so long. Like many places in the world, nature had long since risen up to take itself back— along with anything else that stood in its way.

    Lena felt a small pang of fear arriving just in time with her realization, “We should stay away.”

    “Why, because your ‘Seekers’ like to use them?”

    “Yes, and—” Lena stopped. There was that sense of fear replaced by hurt again…

    Kara noticed as they stood up together. She reached out to take Lena by the arms and apologized, “That was…” Kara shook her head shamefully, “I’m sorry. I didn’t mean to say that like I—”

    “It’s alright.” Lena tried forcing a smile as she swept the backs of her knuckles across Kara’s cheek. “It’s understandable that you would still harbor some anger in that regard.”

    Kara nuzzled closer, “I just want you to know, Lena… I don’t blame you for any of it.”

    “That’s sweet.”

    “I mean it.”

    Lena lowered her eyes and nodded. The trouble was that even though she believed Kara, it still didn’t feel right. How could she ever be forgiven? She may have been just a pawn in her father’s grandiose game of “fixing the world”… but that still didn’t erase the fact that she was very much a part of it all. It was her data compiled, and her notes used for nefarious reasons. Lena knew that knowledge would stay with her for the rest of her days. She would never be able to escape it.

    A prison of her own making; subject to her own miseries.

    “Let’s keep going?” Lena tried smiling again through the harsh stinging of tears in her eyes.

    “Sure, um,” Kara held fast to Lena’s hand as she turned to walk away, “We do actually need to follow these tracks, though.”

    “What- why?”

    “The Children of Liberty aren’t the only ones who use the railway. And if you’d like to turn a ‘few weeks’ into a ‘few days’… then this is how.”

    Lena scratched lightly at her forehead and paused to consider it.

    “We’re still too far inland from the coast,” Kara went on to explain, “None of this track gets used because it can’t be defended. If we push further west from here, we can find a station and then hop a car from there.”

    “Won’t that put us right in the path of the people we’re currently running from?”

    “Yeah, but there’s no better place to hide than in plain sight.”

    Lena crossed her arms, anxious just to think about it all. “I don’t know, Kara…”

    “Listen to me,” Kara took a step closer, “I know these railroads like the back of my hand- the ones still in use, anyway. If we can ride far enough south, then we’ll be in the clear. They won’t dare to go any farther than that… Not where we’re headed.”

    Lena sighed and then gave a small gesture with her hands in defeat, “Alright.”

     

     

    “I’ve changed my mind!” Lena exclaimed as the warm, passing wind fell against the side of her face.

    “Too late—” Kara replied, “Just hold onto me.”

    Though the train had slowed down to the breezy pace of a jog, Lena still felt that it was more than she could handle even if she were to hit the ground running. That was why Kara had insisted that she grab hold of her and not let go. She would leap them both from the moving railcar and use herself as a buffer before landing against the hard, drought-cracked earth.

    “You ready?” Kara asked.

    Lena shook her head adamantly that time, “No!”

    “As soon as your feet hit the ground, tuck yourself into a ball, okay?”

    “Kara, this is a bad—”

    “Hang on!”

    Thankfully, they landed right past the rocky ballast and —just as Kara had said— rolled into a ragdolled lump of arms and legs, eventually coming to a stop inside a cloud of dirt and clay dust. Lena opened her eyes to Kara’s saccharine, sapphire blues and quickly realized that she was lying on top of her again, just as she had back in the forest. It was odd to feel the solid earth beneath them after nearly three days of constant rumbling. The observation car where they had hidden themselves amidst several stops was quickly growing smaller into the distance, and soon Lena had to squint as it left them behind.

    Kara’s hands went to reach comfortably around Lena’s waist then, and she laid her head back down against the dry ground, pleased just to be still again. She huffed a laugh through her nose and seemed to admire the sunlight where it draped a halo around Lena’s head, high in its noonish setting. Lena puffed a few errant strands of her own hair away from her mouth and then crossed her arms over Kara’s chest, resting her chin there as she had done several times before. It was a comfortable sort of familiarity, the way they rested against one another. It gave Lena a sense of control that she’d never felt before. Control of herself, control of her situation… in whatever form that it chose. She was left to steer her feelings either one way or the other.

    It just killed her to always have to right the wheel in the direction of “other”.

    I can’t believe I get to look at you…

    You’re so beautiful.

    Lena was consumed with the urge to wrap her lips around Kara’s again, but one thing would inevitably lead to another, and she would find herself crashing to a halt in no time. She wobbled upright onto her forearms and stared.

    It would be unfair to continue throwing herself at someone who couldn’t afford to return the feeling, but knowing that sad fact only made Lena desire Kara more. She cursed inwardly and blinked a few times. Giving her body over so freely would be, singlehandedly, the most selfless and selfish thing that she could ever do. It was her virginity; eased along the sharp edge of a blade. How devastating of a thought it was. Lena would live her life happily for having given herself to someone like Kara, but Kara would have to sit there with it forever, long after Lena was gone. She understood the cruelty of it the more that she thought about it…

    …but still, Kara’s lips were an alluring spring, and Lena was dying of thirst.

    Oh yes, she knew that not-so-deliberate physiological response of hers was on full display again. How could she help it except by sheer willpower? She had none. Lena felt her fingertips trace along the folded collar of Kara’s shirt, and before she knew it, she was cheek to cheek with her again, thrumming with the mind-bending desire just to meet in the middle. The tip of her nose skirted along the prominent end of Kara’s jaw, and she was sure that Kara could hear the shallow, hopeless breaths that fell there. The feeling was of it was so overwhelming. All she had to do was tilt her head and close her eyes, but Kara would stop her.

    Wouldn’t she?

    Stop.

    Of all things, why did this have to feel so right?

    You mustn’t.

    Lena immediately sat up and scuttled herself away, taking a moment to work her mouth around an apology, “I… F-forgive me.”

    “It’s alright.” Kara replied as she righted herself and dusted off her elbows. She cleared her throat to dispense with the somewhat tense moment and then sighed, “You’re only human.”

    Lena hugged her knees close to her chest, “So are you.”

    “Barely.”

    “No. Much more than you think.”

    “If you say so.”

    Lena swatted suddenly at something flying near her eyes— a bug in search of moisture. Surely enough, they were far out of the forests by now. They stood up onto their feet again and Lena took a good look around. There was flat ground in all directions, sandy in composition and barren of life all except for the rolling of tumbleweeds and sparse patches of cacti, thistle, and flowering bush. In the place of hills there were buttes. Lena was captivated by the tall plateau-like islands made from rock and sediment and saw that some were even shaped like hands thrusting straight up from the ground. Pools of water seemed to gather in some of the lower trenches between them, no doubt the watery remains of another years’ long winter. The ground was so hard that it had apparently struggled to take up so much of the melted snow, resulting in flooded areas. Dwindled now to mere puddles, Lena pointed to one nearby and suggested that they head towards it.

    “Might be good to drink… What do you think?”

    “Better than nothing.” Kara agreed with a simultaneous nod of her head.

    “Okay. Are you hungry?”

    “Always, but I’ll be fine for a little bit.” Kara reached to dig around in one of the baggy pockets of Lena’s pants, “Here,” She handed her a slightly smashed orange and then smiled, “For the road.”

    Lena shook her head, “Why don’t you have it?”

    “Weren’t we just talking about who’s more human?” When Lena went to protest a second time, Kara waved her hands as if she were decided on it. “I can manage until we find something else. You can’t, so take it.”

    “…You’re sure?”

    Kara snapped forward to steal a small kiss from Lena’s cheek. “I’ll get by.”

    Lena was surprised— and confused, as well. Kara certainly wasn’t helping their situation, being so damned charming and all. The very recent memory of Kara’s unsubtle growling at the base of Lena’s neck made her feel weak all of a sudden… It was all she could do just to rip the rind from the orange and to shove a few of its dripping pieces into her mouth. After a moment of chewing, Lena hummed a short note in reply and then turned to embark upon the desert portion of their journey.

    No time like the present, I suppose.

    Keep your hands off of her, and everything should be fine.

     

     

    Lena flung herself forward to catch Kara as she tripped again.

    It wasn’t as if she thought that she could actually prevent her from falling. If anything, it was an instinctual sort of reaction— like reaching out to catch a glass right as it tipped over the edge of a table.

    “That’s the third time you’ve stumbled today.” Lena said very plainly, “Should we stop and rest again?”

    “Nah, I’m good. Just have a lot on my mind, is all.”

    “More so than when we were back at the compound?”

    Kara frowned a bit and then shook her head, shoving both of her hands into the back pockets of her jeans for good measure.  “No.”

    “Kara, you’re tired,” Lena insisted, “…and we don’t have any more food.” She crossed her arms and then spun in place, looking around for any sign that they might be nearing civilization again. “I knew we should have doubled back to find more supplies before getting this far.”

    “It’s fine. You’re getting yourself worked up over nothing.”

    “Don’t speak to me like I’m a child,” Lena fired back, “I’m perfectly capable of assessing the situation for myself, and you’re—” She stopped with a quiet gasp as she reached upward to finger lightly at Kara’s damp hairline, “You’re sweating?”

    Kara took Lena’s hand into her own and hushed calmly over the sound of more of her fretting, “It’s not my intention to treat you like that, I’m sorry. I’d just like it if you calmed down and listened to me, alright?”

    Lena pulled in a deep breath through her nose and then gave a quiet, affirmative harrumph in return. She was worried. Kara’s vitals were veering sharply away from all of the data that she had compiled over the past handful of months. The stores of Kara’s energy running dangerously low made her sluggish, and a bit slurred in speech as well. Her posture had lost its broad-shouldered centeredness as well, and she slumped heavily forward with each of her winded exhales. But even with all of that, nothing seemed to stop her from putting one foot in front of the other.

    “Alright.” Lena agreed, “I’m listening.”

    “Thank you. It should only be another day or so and we’ll be out on the other side where there’s grass, and trees, and things to eat. Okay? You still have some of those desert pears from yesterday?”

    Lena nodded and gave a small pat to the newly fashioned bag hanging at her side. “They’ll hardly sustain you for much longer—”

    Kara waved her hand again and Lena returned to being silent. “I’m not gonna drop dead just because I haven’t eaten anything in a few days.”

    “So, what do we do?”

    “The only thing we can do.” Kara shrugged, “Keep going.”

    “Alright.”

    “Better?”

    Lena pursed her lips, “Mm, no. But nice try.”

    Kara laughed.

    “It’s not funny, Kara. I’m concerned.”

    “I know you are.”

    “What happens if you don’t eat anything else for the foreseeable future?”

    “I, well…” Kara scrunched her face with an unpleasant thought, reaching up to scratch at the back of her neck, “I just slow down.”

    “Like, in the literal sense?”

    “It becomes hard to move my body, yeah.”

    “Kara!” Lena exclaimed all of a sudden, but Kara’s hands were at the tops of her shoulders again to keep her planted firmly upon the ground.

    “Would you relax?!” Kara pleaded, “We’ll make it out of this in one piece if we just keep a steady pace, okay?”

    “You’re asking me to relax when you knew damn well that you could practically turn to stone if you kept pushing yourself like this—”

    “Lena.”

    “Of all the—”

    “I’m getting the sense that you don’t handle prolonged stress very well…”

    “You could say that again—!”

    Suddenly, a terrible quake shook the ground beneath their feet, and Lena felt herself falling backwards.

    “Lena!” Kara grabbed her by the front of her shirt just in time and pulled her upright again.

    It was as if the dirt had shifted on its own, splitting apart and pulverizing itself into more sand as it made for them inch by inch. Kara pushed them further away from the slowly crumbling ground and wrapped her arms further around Lena to keep her from being pulled down. Lena had never seen nature behave so queerly… Perhaps there was a cavern beneath them, and they were standing on top of a rapidly decaying ceiling?

    “What’s happening?” Lena asked as she watched on in terror. A lounge of sunbathing lizards quickly fell into the sinking sand of it all, swallowed up in an instant.

    “Whatever happens, just stay behind me.” Kara said with her eyes narrowed into daggers towards a small butte in the distance.

    “Kara, tell me what’s happening right now—”

    “There’s a remnant.”

    Lena froze. She recalled all too well Kara’s chilling explanation regarding her kind’s proximity to one another, describing it as more of a “sixth sense” than anything. Lena had spent several sleepless nights after that formulating possible theories involving magnetoreception. She felt it was likely that Kara —and other remnants, as well— could locate one another through the earth’s magnetic field somehow.

    “Where?” Lena whispered, noticing only too late how she was shaking like a leaf.

    A voice like a clap of thunder finally called out to them over the dry ground, carrying on and on past them like a well-traveled echo…

    “Surprised to see you all the way out here.”

    Lena felt the taut ropes of Kara’s triceps stiffen for a second, and she was suddenly more scared than she had ever been in her life. Her peripherals widened upon her heightened state of awareness, and her sense of sight and sound became attuned to the smallest detail.

    “How long has it been, James?” Kara called back, “Eighty years? Ninety?”

    Trembling, Lena peeked around Kara’s frame and saw a man approaching the other side of the sinkhole. The rich ebony of his skin gleamed in the sunlight, as he possessed no shirt and only some tattered green pants rolled up along his each of his calves. His feet were bare, as well. The man named James seemed to lack any care at all towards his own well-being, including that of anyone else’s, apparently. Lena wasn’t quite sure how, but she knew that the earth dissolving under her very feet was at his behest. Not close enough yet to see the whites of his eyes, Lena stayed directly behind Kara as they continued to speak across the cavernous gap.

    “Who’s counting?” He replied, “Don’t tell me you’re here to see if I’m finally ready to come crawling back…”

    “No,” Kara shrugged, “You made it pretty clear when you left that we shouldn’t come looking.”

    James scratched at his chin for a moment, seemingly agitated by his own curiosity. Lena guessed that it must have been a very long time in between his socializing. “So, you lost or something?” He asked, “Because I’ll tell you one thing, eighty years is still too soon.”

    “We’re just passing through.”

    “Is that right?”

    “Yeah. That’s right.”

    James took a step back and then burst into a sprint to leap across the sinkhole. He landed with a short slide upon his knee and then stood back up without a single scratch.

    “Who’s that you’re with?” He leaned a bit to see around Kara, who took another step in order to shield Lena further.

    “Another rescue.” Kara shifted somewhat anxiously, “James, you can always choose to just let us go. We don’t have to do this.”

    Lena felt herself begin to panic. Her sword hand dropped to the smooth, rounded hilt of her sabre, and every part of her body became difficult to move again, right down to the throaty -gulp- she made in an effort to swallow her fear.

    “What, because killing me would be so much more effort?”

    “You know I won’t do that.”

    “You mean you can’t.”

    “Jimmy—”

    “Don’t call me that!”

    A sudden jolt ran through Lena’s limbs at the booming of his voice. He appeared to be much taller now that he was standing nearly face to face with Kara, towering over her by half a foot, at least.

    “Only Clark and my mom got to call me that, and they’re both gone- or have you finally forgotten?” James had gone from perturbed to furious in a flash. He leaned again to speak around Kara towards Lena, “You know she’s lying to you, right?”

    Kara groaned, “This again.”

    “Says she’ll take you to a place where you can be safe and sound- but really it’s to distract you from the truth.”

    Lena felt herself asking before she realized, “What truth is that?”

    “Lena—”

    “That we’re not meant to be here!” James pounded angrily against his chest with the flat of his hand, “That we’re not supposed to be like this!”

    “Like what?”

    “Wrong!”

    Kara held her hands out to warn against his steady advance, “Please, we can still stop this—”

    “Try it!” James shouted with a hard shove against Kara’s shoulders. The way her body moved in response; one would think that she’d been thrown by a giant. Kara landed hard against the ground, far out of reach now, and James immediately clambered towards Lena in a way that was meant to intimidate her. She stayed still just to spite him.

    “I’m not afraid of you.” Lena spat.

    Sure, she was terrified, but she wouldn’t so much as give this man the satisfaction of admitting it.

    “Your heartbeat says otherwise—” James leaned forward, narrowing his dark and hateful eyes, “Human.”

    “James!” Kara screamed.

    With that, Lena drew her sabre with one long swipe out from the skinny scabbard at her side and quickly positioned herself away from James, far enough to establish her point-in-line with room for an equal lunge, if needed.

    Kara scrambled to her feet and screamed again, “James, don’t!”

    Too filled with contempt to listen, James brought himself directly towards the end of Lena’s sabre and then did the unthinkable— he reached out and grabbed it. Blade digging straight into his palm and blood dripping down the length of his forearm, he grinned…

    “Cute.”

    An odd sort of resonance quickly traveled through the steel of Lena’s sword, delivering a vibrational shock violent enough to break the bones in her hand— had she not loosened her grip and dropped it at the first sign of pain. Lena looked back up and realized that James had the power to manipulate matter in some strange way, likely upon a molecular level. Before she knew it, Kara was running straight for him and tackling him to the ground. They fell atop one another with swinging punches, righting themselves up and onto their knees just to knock each other back down again. Lena was too horrified to do anything other than watch. It was like witnessing fallen gods clawing at one another to gain higher ground, scraping their way back towards their former glory. The ground itself, though, continued to fall out from underneath them at several places, each time where James pressed the side of Kara’s head down as if he meant to bury it.

    Stricken with a vengeance, Lena ran to swipe a long cut into the flesh of James’ shoulder, and he howled a second later, rolling down into the dirt and teetering onto his forehead. He grit his teeth past another enraged bellow and then flung his eyes back towards Lena…

    “You monsters think you can just do whatever you want and get away with it!” He shouted, eyes red and glistening with tears, “Kill my baby sister, and take away everything that was supposed to be mine! You stole everything from me!”

    He slammed a fist into the earth, and the dirt beneath Lena’s feet began to vibrate into microscopic particles of dust again. Lena jumped to find more stable ground, but James was upon her quickly.

    What occurred next was simply too much for her to keep up with. Lena could no longer tell where she ended and Kara began— two hearts beating in a single, uncertain rhythm.

    Kara had thrown herself between them as James’ hand had come down to strike her. He latched on to Kara’s arm instead and then growled with his ever-growing frustration of being unable to do as he pleased.

    “When do you ever get out of the way?” He seethed angrily at her.

    With one squeeze of his hand just beneath Kara’s wrist, there went a horrible -snap- sound and a painful cry out from Kara’s mouth.

    “Agh—!” Kara dropped to her knees and began to groan pitifully over her arm.

    Something deep inside of Lena’s psyche twisted at that very moment, perverting her gentler self into something far more wild. Anathema to her very nature like rust is to iron, Lena felt crazed enough to do something she never thought possible…

    She would kill this man for what he had done.

    James charged straight for her again, and that reactive part of Lena’s subconscious —now flung so far away from freeze— was geared solely towards fight. Lena squared her feet and then reared back to kick him right in the middle of his torso, launching him just far enough to stumble backwards and down into the largest of his sinkholes. He scrambled for a moment at its edge, and then he dropped.

    “No—!” Kara lunged for him.

    Lena ran to grab a hold of Kara as she slid further towards the crumbling edge of the split apart earth. By some miracle, Kara had managed to catch James by a hand and was struggling to pull him out.

    “Kara, what are you doing—?!”

    “Help me!” She begged. With her injured arm tucked closely to her side, there was nowhere to anchor herself should they both fall in.

    James only swatted Lena’s hand away whenever she acquiesced and offered it down towards him. “Get away from me…” He bit back at her, still bitter and hateful even in the most unfortunate of situations.

    “James,” Kara began to cry, “James, please… just come home.”

    “There’s no home there. Not with him.”

    “Let me help you, asshole!”

    James shook his head and flitted his eyes towards Kara’s failing grip.

    Kara sobbed, “No—”

    “I’m gonna kill him, Kara. And then… I’m gonna kill you.”

    “Please!”

    With one last sad look in his eyes, James squeezed his hand again, and Kara began to whimper angrily past the pain it caused her just to hold on to him. It was as if Lena could hear the sounds of the vibrations he was emitting; the excruciating collision of atoms, rising at an exponential rate. Kara held on as long as she could before the pain in her fingerbones became too much. She let go with a wounded gasp, and down he went.

    James fell into the pitted deep of the earth without so much as a cry, defeated yet unafraid. After that, there was only the gut-wrenching sobs of Kara clutching her broken arm to her chest.

    Lena acted quickly and fought to unfold Kara where she lied writhing back and forth upon the ground. “Let me see- let me see it!”

    “It hurts—!”

    “It’s broken, Kara. I’m sure that it hurts- you need to give it to me so that I can set it. Quickly.”

    “God, I forgot how much it hurts—”

    “Dammit, do you want to be stuck with a crooked arm for the rest of eternity?!” Lena pulled at her again and watched as Kara stilled for a moment just to shake her head “no”. For whatever reason, it was so much easier now to exude calmness under pressure whenever Kara was the one depending on her. If Lena didn’t act right away, then Kara’s accelerated healing would cripple her for life. Lena took a deep breath and did her best to hold Kara still, “I’m sorry…”

    Woefully, Kara extended her arm and held her breath with a face twisted up in agony. It took all of Lena’s strength to pull the bones in Kara’s arm straight again with one hard yank— a move that drew such an aching, gravelly scream from Kara that it startled a distant committee of vultures into an equally noisy flight. Lena felt that she would never forgive herself for being the cause of it.

    Kara then sat up and did something which broke Lena’s heart further…

    She wiped her eyes, pulled in a deep breath, swallowed hard, and then set her face with a stony expression. “Okay,” Kara said, her chin giving out one last awful quiver, “…we can keep going now.”

    Lena sat there on her crossed legs staring at Kara yet again, befuddled and amazed for how much perseverance one single person could possess.

    She’ll never give up…

    Later that night, when the dust had finally settled and Kara had stumbled her last for the day, Lena realized something. Kara was true to her nature in the way that she helped those in need, even the ones who didn’t want it. She did so because she hadn’t been able to help the one person she loved most in the world and seemed to be settled on making up for it for as long as she felt doomed to walk the earth. A truly selfless act, containing more humanity than Lena had ever witnessed in any one human being. She realized that humanity lied with Kara, someone whose inner-most oath was to protect everyone she could even to the detriment of her own well-being. So, Lena endeavored to make an oath of her very own…

    She would protect Kara. By any means necessary.

    Chapter End Notes

    Thanks for reading <3

    Chapter 13: There’s Honor in Eagle’s Feathers

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Thirteen: There’s Honor in Eagle’s Feathers

     

    Kara took in another deep breath. Their small campfire crackled and spit orange embers into the evening air, glowing a warm rust color against the lids of her eyes the longer that she laid there.

    It was cold once the sun went down over the foothills of the Sonoran Desert.

    Having not eaten for a matter of days now, Kara was unable to regulate her body temperature on her own. Admittedly, she had been in similar situations before —at least a handful of times— where she’d been run so ragged, starved, and nearly turned rock solid, but no amount of her attempts to convince Lena that she wouldn’t die right away seemed to do the trick. Kara would simply sleep for a long time, and if left without anything to eat for much longer than that, only then might she die. But of course, it was hard to say with a full amount of certainty either way— she had never actually done herself the favor of dying before.

    “Come here?” Kara asked, opening her eyes again. It was as if she could hear Lena’s frantic inner turmoil, whispered like small cries for help along the cool, Sonoran breeze. She wished for her to be still. To unburden her of all her fears.

    Lena was hunched forward where she sat with her legs crossed, head in her hands like always. She internalized so much of her stress— though when she didn’t, it seemed to come out of her in waves. Kara knew that Lena was worried beyond belief. It was clear that she blamed herself for their current predicament. Kara tried telling her that it wasn’t her fault— that fighting James was a necessary evil.

    Kara tried lifting her head. “Please?”

    Lena sucked the back of her teeth with a quick and pitiable -tsk- and then leaned away from her boulder that was shaped like a cat’s head. At least that’s what it looked like whenever Kara could stop her vision from doubling. Lena had sat herself there several times in between her anxious pacing. She came over and then shrugged off her bartered flannel to drape it over Kara’s bare shoulders. “Should I make the fire bigger?” She asked.

    “No,” Kara replied tiredly, “We don’t wanna attract any trouble.”

    Lena scooted herself longways then to snuggle right up against Kara’s front. “That’s if anyone else is foolish enough to trek all this way,” She sighed almost resentfully, “…to the absolute middle of nowhere.”

    “We’ll be outta here in no time. I just need to sleep some more.”

    “You should have eaten, Kara.” Lena reared back to look Kara in the eyes, “I can’t find any more prickly pears- and the saguaros around here are all too tall for me to reach.”

    “How far did you go?”

    “While you were sleeping earlier? At least half an hour in equidistant concentric patterns.”

    “Please don’t do that…” Kara nuzzled her face closer to Lena’s neck and breathed her in there, feeling the faint edges of her slumber approaching once again.

    Lena seemed to melt with the gesture and rested a hand at Kara’s chin, “I don’t have a choice- you can’t walk anymore. I have to do something—”

    “You don’t have to do anything.”

    “Yes,” Lena began to cry quietly, “I do.”

    “It’s okay, I just need a few more minutes.”

    “Alright.”

    Kara had begun to notice how Lena’s smiles were becoming less frequent, rarely adorning her cherubic dimple.

    “How does your arm feel?” Lena asked.

    Kara looked down and saw no noticeable difference. The sickening bent angle just beneath her wrist was no more, and she was glad for it. Walking around with a wonky arm for the rest of her days was definitely an unfavorable way to spend eternity, she had to admit. It was lucky that Lena was there, and that she had acted so quickly. Rebreaking her arm would have sucked— if they had found another way to actually do so, that is. Kara had prior run-ins with battering rams that did less damage… but the worst part wasn’t the break itself. It was the fact that James had done it; The only person in the world who could actually harm her.

    “It’s fine.” Kara replied, “Back to feeling like nothing.”

    “Do you mean to say that your nerve endings feel numb? Is that normal?”

    “Ever the scientist…” Kara sighed as she sat down rather clumsily, toppling over a few large rocks just a few feet away.

    “You weren’t exactly what I’d call ‘forthcoming’ during our first few visits. The vagaries of your moods usually decided if you told me the truth, or simply half of it.” Lena finally came over to sit down beside Kara and dug around the bottom of their supply bag— fashioned from another one of Kara’s shirt sleeves. Lena flinched suddenly with something akin to the word “ouch” hissed between her teeth, and then paused a moment to produce a fat, purplish cactus pear, pinched carefully between her thumb and forefinger. “Missed one…” She said with glimmer of disappointment as she began to scratch at the invisible thorn inside of her palm.

    “Here.” Kara took up the small fruit and rubbed it clean with her thumb. Impenetrable skin sure had its benefits. “How many do we have left?”

    Lena frowned. “That’s it.”

    “Alright.” Kara pinched the fruit in half to share with Lena. They chewed gently around their small mouthfuls of impossibly hard seeds and then got rid of them discretely, both just a shade too shy to turn their heads and spit.

    “I’ll say it.” Lena huffed once she dusted her hands across her pant legs.

    “What?”

    “I despise the desert.”

    Kara hacked a laugh. “Whole lotta nothing, huh?”

    “So much nothing.”

    “Have you been noticing all the large bite marks on everything we happen to pass by?”

    Lena nodded begrudgingly, “Yes… and the hoof prints.”

    “Pretty sure we’re right behind a herd of mustangs.” Kara said with a sour tone, “They go where the food is, and when they’ve picked an area clean, they move on.”

    “So, they’re eating everything before we can even get to it…” Lena replied with an ounce or so more of her frustration. Kara had begun to shiver now with the setting sun, and Lena swiveled her head around in search of something along the hilly ground. “I’ll make a fire.”

    “A small one, please. No leaves or dried grass, just wood.” Kara laid back with a hard -fwump- that rumbled the earth and then rolled onto her side.

    Lena stood up and began to gather small kindling pieces into the crook of her arm. “I didn’t realize it could get so hot during the day- I mean, of course I did- but it’s quite another thing to experience it.”

    Kara yawned, “Oh, this is nothing.”

    “Really?” Lena stopped, dropping a small, thorny mesquite branch with her surprise. She remarked out loud with her timely discovery of its long bean pods attached and then tossed them gleefully to Kara. She still seemed to be perfectly tickled about Kara having called them the “candy bar of the desert”.

    “Yeah, when I was a kid, the summers could get close to a hundred and twenty degrees.” Kara chewed quietly on the longish, sun-dried bean. It would at least mimic a feeling of fullness. They were sweet, sometimes nutty. When Lena had stared with her eyebrow raised for long enough, Kara thought to clarify, “Around forty-nine degrees Celsius.”

    “I thought you were kidding.”

    “What? I was raised on Fahrenheit. Bad habit, I guess.” Kara joked.

    “Not that—” Lena blustered, “Well, yes, actually, but forty-nine Celsius?! That’s inhospitable- practically unlivable. How on earth did you survive?”

    “There’s this great invention, actually, called central air-conditioning. I don’t know if you’ve heard of it, but—”

    “Oh, stop.” Lena laughed with a gentle toss a log piece towards Kara’s abdomen and then startled at the sound of Kara’s sharp inhale, as if she’d knocked the very breath out of her. “Are you—” She blinked, amazed, “Kara, did that hurt you?”

    Kara shook her head and set her eyes to gazing at Lena once again. She was so beautiful, even when her face was pinched together with concern. “Not really.”

    The gears turning behind Lena’s eyes were mesmerizing. “You feel pain only in a weakened state.” She said matter-of-factly, and with just a hint of remorse, as well.

    “I feel lots of things…”

    Lena frowned and came back to crouch alongside her. She brushed her hand gently across Kara’s cheek, “Do you?”

    Kara nodded. “When I want to.”

    Kara shifted slightly and fluttered her eyes open for a second or two, noticing the dying embers of their fire and the unburnt ends of each log smoldering in the wind. She realized that her arm was slung around Lena’s waist, and she leaned away suddenly, but Lena was quick to stop her and to drag her arm back around. She crooked it in front of her chest and then held tightly on to it there, furling her hand neatly inside of Kara’s where it rested over her beating heart. Kara felt strong enough to lift her head at least, but the mere idea of standing up and walking so soon made her feel immediately dizzy.

    “Is this okay?”

    “Shh, it’s fine.” Lena whispered sleepily, “You’re perfect.”

    “I’m sorry.”

    “Aren’t you the one always telling me to stop apologizing?”

    Kara looked around and familiarized herself with their surroundings yet again. She felt that she’d only closed her eyes for a moment or two, but the distressed sound of Lena’s voice painted a vivid picture. The gentle slope of their desert valley was the perfect, shady overhang to stop and rest. Kara didn’t have the heart to say it out loud, but she knew that if they were to stop here, then she might not have the strength to take them further. Not until she could sleep some more. Her eyes felt glazed over to the point where she could hardly see straight.

    “I can’t take another day of not asking.” Lena tossed their small bag down near an oddly shaped boulder and then sat with a displeased huff, “I want to know about James.”

    “James…” Kara sighed after finally taking a knee, “…is not my favorite subject.”

    “I understand that he was probably somebody who meant a great deal to you at some point- but he tried to kill me, Kara. I want to know why.”

    Kara grumbled lowly to herself.

    “What did he do?” 

    “It’s more a question of who he is, really.”

    “Oh? Why?”

    “It’s a lot to explain.” Kara replied with a sullen voice, “I know there’s tons of tall tales out there surrounding remnants- that we’re dangerous, or crazy, or that we harbor an intense hatred of all humans…”

    “Well,” Lena shrugged, “I know you well enough to know that they’re not all true.”

    Kara pulled her lips into a thin, apologetic line and hummed, “Mm, they’re actually half-true.”

    “Half…?”

    “The male half, to be specific.”

    “Is that why there’s more recorded evidence of female and non-binary remnants?” Lena asked, giving an idle pinch at her own chin, “I always thought that it had something to with the vaccine itself. Perhaps from a compatibility standpoint or some sort of—”

    “No. Nothing like that.”

    “Then… what is it?”

    “What I’m about to say- I know it’ll sound like such a generic and watered down version of why they’re pretty much non-existent now, but it’s the truth. After all of us realized that we were gonna be here for a very, very long time,” Kara sighed, “The stigmas began to set in, and when those stigmas turned into full blown fears, everything went downhill from there. Basically, there’re barely any remnant men left because they all went mad. A vast majority of them, anyway. They just couldn’t handle being in a world that didn’t want them in it.”

    “And everyone else could?”

    Kara raised an agreeable eyebrow, “Easier getting along when you’ve already been treated like second-class citizens for so many hundreds of years. There’s a reason why non-binary, trans, and female remnants of color hold positions of power wherever we feel safest.”

    Lena had difficulty stringing together a single sentence after that. Kara figured it was quite like admitting to a lifelong worshipper of God that their “god” was, in fact, not real. In reality, it was that singular, earth-shattering revelation which marked the horrible beginning of the end. The Deterioration period at the start of the 22nd century was the bloodiest cataclysm in recorded human history and had gone hand in hand with the third world war, and then the Turbulentivirus, and then the destruction of civilized society right down to its very infrastructures. Aided by a virus which attacked the amygdala, a widespread anger over everything and nothing was humanity’s biggest downfall. Not only had humans brought about their own demise, but they had left monsters in their wake.

    “So…” Lena finally licked her lips in an effort to speak, “He just went insane after so many years?”

    Kara nodded. “To be fair, it was harder for him than most. His sister Kelly didn’t survive the first trial of the vaccine, and he was never the same after that. We, um… we went to school together.”

    “Do you think he’s dead?”

    “No…” Kara shook her head, “Not when he still has something to do.”

    “You mean killing you?”

    “…Not just me.”

     

     

    “Kara…”

    The perpetual feeling of falling was comforting. Being stuck in that brief lull between consciousness and unconsciousness didn’t feel worrisome at all. Everything just felt so heavy, and she was so, so tired…

    “Kara!”

    Like being raked backwards and into a vacuum, Kara felt herself shifting from side to side. She would wake soon if it didn’t stop.

    “Kara- dammit, open your eyes!”

    “Lena?” Her voice seemed to travel like a faraway echo. Kara fluttered her eyes open at last and blinked towards the glowing silhouette in front of her. There was that warm amber halo again, draped ever so gracefully around Lena’s head. “Lena…” She pulled in a deep breath and tried sitting up again. It made the world spin. “What’s wrong?”

    “Your breathing slowed down so much, I- I didn’t know what to do.” There were tears streaming down Lena’s face. Kara could see them glistening brightly from the flickering flames of their nearby fire.

    “Crying?” Kara reached up to thumb lightly at one of Lena’s cheeks, wiping the salty track clean from her natural, rosy blush. “Don’t cry…”

    “You haven’t moved in over a day- I can't watch this happen to you! Tell me what to do!”

    “You have to keep going.”

    “No—”

    “Just keep heading southeast until you find more railroad tracks.”

    “No!” She shook her head furiously, “No, anything but that. I won’t leave you.”

    “Please, Lena.”

    “Kara, you’ll die!” Lena sobbed as she bowed to rest her forehead upon Kara’s, “Please don’t fall back asleep. Don’t leave me.”

    Kara’s eyes began to droop heavily once more. If not for the sounds of approaching footsteps, then she would have closed them altogether— and who knows if she might have awoken again? The sense of urgency was there, but the feeling of succumbing to her depleted state was too strong to deny.

    Lena scrambled to reach for her sword and then scraped back across the dirt to unsheathe it, pointing it towards a small group that was headed their way. Kara’s heart began to pound, and a renewed sense of vigor thrust its way back into her limbs. She wobbled upright onto her hands and knees, and then fell flat onto her chest again, coughing as the clay dust there worked its way into her lungs.

    “Who’s there?!” Lena shouted, “Don’t come any closer!”

    Kara had a worse time seeing in the dark than she did through her brief lapses of consciousness throughout the day. Lena was in danger, and she couldn’t lift herself off of her goddamn face—

    “I said stay back!” Lena cried out again, but then she stilled for a moment, and her breaths slowly began to even out with a returning sense of calmness. Kara slid her face the other way in the dirt and squinted to see more clearly. “W-will you help us?” Lena begged, “Please- we need food.”

    Two figures broke off from the group and stepped into the firelight.

    It was a man and a woman, possibly father and daughter judging by their similar features. Kara could tell by the expressions of concern upon their tanned faces that they meant them no harm. A wave of relief came over her at once, though she had not the strength to tell Lena who they were. The older man wore a pristine eagle feather pointed skyward near the crown of his head, and his younger female companion displayed many necklaces of precious turquoise and shells around her slender neck. The tribeswoman smiled as she drew nearer to the fire, and Lena dropped her sword to clasp her hands together, bowing all the way forward to prostrate herself…

    “Please help us…”

    Without a word, the Apache maiden took herself over to a sprawling patch of bare cactus and scraped a beaded stick against its large, oval-shaped pads to clear it of its spines. Then, with her smile ever apparent upon her face, she took the desert flora into her hand and closed her eyes.

    Kara heard the sounds of Lena’s gasped “thank yous” as her vision eventually began to strobe into darkness again. She was unable to focus past the clumsy drooping of her eyelids but could see well enough that the cactus had flushed with a vibrant green color all of a sudden, no longer sickly and brown and picked clean of its fruit. Pears began to burst forth along the tops of every pad, growing thick with hues of pinks, and reds, and purples. One last blink of her eyes and they were shut for good. Kara finally slumped the rest of the way flat and lied on her stomach with a final breath, no longer afraid that they were under any real threat. She knew that the Chiricahua would keep Lena safe.

    That was all she could ask for, anyway.

    Her only regret was not kissing her dark-haired savior one last time.

    Chapter End Notes

    A warm nod to Cellars at Marble Falls. My countertop is full of prickly pears at the moment so they frequently occupy my mind with their deliciousness. There's still more of the story to come, have no fear, but if you're lost, then going back and re-reading may prove useful at this point. There will be more answers as the second and third acts progress. This one's the most intricate thing I've ever written. I'm sorry it’s not very good. I've been feeling a bit down on myself lately. Again.
    Thanks for reading.

    Chapter 14: The Price of Stone

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Fourteen: The Price of Stone

     

    “I wasn’t out that long.” Kara scoffed.

    “Three days, Kara.” Lena replied with not so much as a pause in her step. “You are not as indestructible as you think.”

    “It sorta sounds like you’re mad still…”

    “That’s because I am.”

    “I promise,” Kara scratched at the back of her neck, teasing her fingers through her loose and wavy hair, “…no more hero stuff.”

    What could she say to make it right? Her stubbornness often abounded in desperate situations, and even though it came from a good place, it had nearly gotten the both of them killed. Kara should have known that Lena would never have left her behind. Not for any reason, and not in a million years.

    “I- I’m sorry I left you alone like that.”

    Lena barely looked back as she ducked beneath the low hanging branch of a hardy oak. Her voice was as calm as the overgrown dell, “Thankfully, Sonsee-Array was perfectly acceptable company while you were busy regaining your strength.”

    Kara nearly tripped over a split stump with her surprise.

    The piles of firewood around this edge of the village were plenty, stacked high and bordering a greenbelt of large, sweeping live oak trees. The secluded wood valley was home to the Chiricahua tribe, trade neighbors of the Union and their every-once-in-a-while ally for the right cause. While it was true that Kara had been well enough for at least a day or so and that they could depart the Apache whenever they pleased, they had ultimately decided to stay long enough to fortify their supplies, and to make friends where friends could be made. Lena was fast at the latter, but this came as no surprise to Kara. She was all the friendliness in the world bound within human form, but it was hearing her speak so fondly of someone else —or anyone else, for that matter— that threw Kara for a loop.

    “You and Sonsee-Array, huh?”

    “Yes.”

    “Was, um—” Kara cleared her throat, “Was, by chance, her dad around? Maybe?”

    Lena finally tossed her dark ponytail over her shoulder as she spun around. The previous umbrage which sat squarely upon her face soon faded, leaving her expression a shade more curious. Kara felt almost starved for that innocent dimple beset in Lena’s cheek; the one that greeted her every morning right smack in the middle of a smile. “We were left quite unsupervised, if that’s what you’re asking.” Lena replied.

    “I just—” Kara stopped in her tracks. She batted the leafy end of a tree limb away from her face and squinted hard in Lena’s direction, “Hang on, you’re trying to make me jealous, aren’t you?”

    “Oh, I don’t know. Is it working?”

    “Yes.” Kara answered without missing a beat. Her enthusiastic nodding earned her a smile, and then a laugh.

    “Come on….” Lena sighed once her giggling subsided. The sound of it fell over Kara’s head like a gentle rain, and everything suddenly seemed a little bit brighter. The waxy green of oak tree leaves, the rays of the sun, even the clouds. A cool wind swept through it all, and Kara was able to relax at last. Lena pointed with a toss of her head towards a tall tipi with wonderful smelling smoke billowing out from its top, “We’ve been invited to dinner.”

    Kara suddenly understood the purpose of their short trek through the woods. “With Sonsee-Array…”

    “Yes, with Sonsee-Array, and the rest of her family.”

    “Ah,” Kara clapped her hands together, “Well that’s great, then! What are we waiting for?” Without another word, she breezed right past Lena with a quick peck to her raven-haired temple and then trampled over the brush around to the front of the tipi.

    She would be forgiven for stolen kisses, no doubt, but not for the dread which she had foisted upon her companion… Not so readily, anyway. Kara decided that she would just have to earn her way back towards more of those cheek-dimpled smiles. One was enough for now, she supposed, at least until she could figure out the best way to apologize. Surely that would come with more kisses, since she now had so many of them to give. A brief stint with her own mortality had —in the most remarkable way— stomped all over her guarded perspective on life. Those were the ungainly boot prints she welcomed…

    …because they walked in a straight line to Lena.

    The point of it all.

     

     

    “What was that you said about the young boy on his hunt the other day? How did you put it—” Lena snapped her fingers beside her head in search of the words, “…like a ‘dog with a bone’?”

    “Yeah, I guess that was it. Why?” Kara replied, hiking the strap of their newly acquired rucksack over her shoulder.

    Lena admired the small row of beaded bracelets that Kara had been gifted with upon their exodus from the small Apache village. There were many bright blues there to match her eyes, and Lena suddenly found herself staring once again. It had been such a difficult façade to keep— her feigned anger. She had been upset with Kara for all of an afternoon and was then quickly swept up by her thick-layered charm again.

    “I’m just wondering what it means?” She asked.

    Kara shrugged a bit more comfortably that time, “It means ‘determined’, but in a relentless sort of way. Some people find it annoying.”

    “Do you?”

    “No.”

    “He looked up to you, you know.”

    Kara offered her hand in order to help Lena down off the small ledge in front of them. Their path ahead was clear. The battered calderas and mesas of old day New Mexico had given way to the semi-arid, brush-covered plains of what used to be southwest Texas, but at least they could see what was in front of them, as well as what was behind them. A small herd of deer pinned their antlers in their direction once they heard the snapping of dried twigs, and the lot of them quickly dispersed with graceful leaps and bounds. Lena took up Kara’s outstretched hand with a polite “thank you” and then hopped down, continuing on with their steady pace.

    “How do you mean?” Kara finally asked after another quiet moment of walking.

    “I’m sorry?”

    “About Ka-e-te-nay…”

    Lena felt a smirk play upon her lips. She knew that Kara was playing dumb after all, remembering the boy’s name so fondly had all but given her away. “Well, he was constantly asking for you, for one thing.”

    “He was a sweet kid.”

    “Is that all?”

    “Is that all what?”

    “Is that all you have to say?” Lena replied with a breathy laugh. She couldn’t understand why Kara was so reserved whenever it came to children. She was gentle as a rule with most things, but it was as if her whole personality shifted around the innocence of youth.

    Kara tossed her hands up, “I dunno- kids are just… so…” She gestured vaguely in search of the right response but came up with nothing.

    “You’re afraid for them.” Lena guessed.

    “Terrified is a better word.” Kara sighed and then waved over her shoulder, “But he’s got a big family, and an even bigger tribe. Trust me, they’ll all be here long after people like you and me are gone.”

    “That’s comforting, I suppose… When you put it that way, at least.”

    Kara seemed to want to change the subject with an awkward clearing of her throat, “I once met a Cherokee chief in full reg—” Then she stopped whenever a high-pitched screech came from somewhere around the tops of several trees. She motioned for Lena, “…Wait a second.”

    Lena stiffened immediately. “What’s wrong?”

    Kara began to jump up and down with a renewed sense of vigor and started waving eagerly towards a large bird that was circling overhead. A raptor of some kind with bright red tailfeathers.

    “Peck!” Kara shouted at last, “Down here! Peck—!”

    Lena crossed her arms, flabbergasted yet again. She had witnessed Kara attempt stranger things before, but this topped the list.

    Handsome, kind, funny, and strange…

    So very strange.

    “Do you… know that bird?” Lena asked.

    “Yeah.” Kara replied with a smile from ear to ear.

    It suddenly occurred to Lena that she had never seen Kara so happy. Apart from discovering her sister still lived and breathed, of course, but were those tears in her eyes? Lena looked back up towards the sky and watched as the hawk began to fly away from them with some manner of haste. “Oh, what’s it doing? Is it leaving—?”

    “Don’t worry.” Kara subconsciously took up Lena’s hand with a long sigh, and Lena’s heart soared like that of a similar winged beast. “He saw us.”

     

     

    “That many in one place?”

    “Yeah, so, there’s Sara and Ava, like I said- which you’ll never find one without the other, and then Nyssa who likes to pretend that she hates everything but weirdly refuses to leave. Then there’s Mick, and Cait—”

    “Kara,” Lena chuckled, “You should really try breathing in between sentences.”

    Kara nodded, her sunbeam of a smile still ever present, “Sorry. I’m just excited for you to meet everyone.”

    “Did you really mean to wait all this time to tell me about them?”

    “I… well, yeah.”

    Lena chanced the small brush of her pinky finger against Kara’s where they walked side by side, “I understand.”

    “They’re a lot to take in at once, I’ll admit. I probably should have done more to prepare you…”

    “It’s alright. You want to protect them- I don’t blame you.”

    Lena had been listening to Kara talk non-stop for at least half an hour since their feathered friend had disappeared. There was something about it all that unsettled her, though. Perhaps it was the fact that Lena’s situation was now the exact opposite of when she had first met Kara. She was the one with no discernable future now… No home, and no plan for tomorrow other than the grander sense of living… Kara knew exactly what it was she was returning to. A strict sense of familiarity. Meanwhile, it was all Lena could do just to weather one day at a time, but if she closed her eyes and did her best to imagine a future for herself, it wasn’t any wonder that all she could see was Kara. She appeared in every frame. Every scenario. Every beginning, and every end.

    Kara skipped forward a bit and then turned to continue her happy rambling, walking backwards in front of Lena now with more excitable steps, “Have you ever been on a motorcycle?”

    “…A what?”

    “It’s this super old two-wheeled vehicle with a combustion engine—”

    “Sounds dangerous.”

    “Yeah, but that’s why it’s fantastic.”

    Lena smiled, “I guess you’ll have to show me, then. Won’t you?”

    “Mhm.” Kara gave a turn upon her bootheel to face forward again, “I’ll have to take you straight to the council first, but after that I can show you around. You’ll meet another remnant named Amaya, and…” Kara gave a sudden quirk of her chin, and then promptly stopped walking.

    “Kara?”

    “Just a sec.” Kara kneeled down to place a hand flat upon the ground and then waved for Lena to allow her some space. “Would you mind standing over there, please?”

    Lena looked to her right and then took a few steps as asked. “Here?”

    “Mmm, further.”

    “…Alright.”

    “Maybe a little bit more?”

    Lena sighed as she took one last long stride with her right leg. She knew better than to question the more benign requests now. There was always a purpose behind Kara’s every syllable. Lena waited a moment while Kara seemed to bounce her attention between their position and a billowing cloud of dust in the distance. Lena squinted to look more closely at it…

    Curious.

    Wait, is it moving?

    Shielding her eyes from the sun helped her to see more clearly as the disturbance gained on them. Lena gasped.

    “Do me a favor and stay right there, okay?” Kara dragged a foot behind herself in order to brace her stance.

    “Kara, what is that—”

    The sudden, screaming -zip- of wind and the debris that came with it hit Lena like a tidal wave. Never had she seen anything move so fast so close to the ground before. When the dust finally settled, the sounds of laughter rose over a heap of limbs. A man scrambled onto his knees and then leaned himself over Kara, shaking her excitedly by the shoulders—

    “I knew it was you! I can’t believe you’re alive- wait- yes, I can!” He cheered, “It’s really you!”

    Kara chuckled, “Took you long enough—”

    “Wait until the others find out!” Thrilled and stunned all at the same time, he cradled his head in his hands as they stood up beside one another, “Should I go tell Sara?” In a flash, he was behind Kara and then in front of her again, assessing her from head to toe, “We looked for you for months- Are you alright? Where have you been?”

    “Barry,” Kara gestured towards Lena, “This is Lena.”

    His eyes zipped towards Lena then with a similar speed.

    “Lena, meet Barry.”

    As soon as Lena could blink, the astoundingly energetic man named Barry was standing right in front of her and shaking her hand vigorously with more of his enthusiastic greeting, “Hi. Nice to meet you. Everyone calls me the Flash—”

    Kara shook her head, “Nobody calls him that.”

    Barry ignored her, “Wow. You’re kinda pale. Spend a lot of time inside?”

    “Barry!”

    “You’re human, right?”

    “I…” Lena flustered all of a sudden, surprised that he could pin her down so easily, “Am I that obvious?”

    “Eh, you just look like you’ve never seen a speedster before.”

    Lena would have supplied him with some witty retort or other if he hadn’t all but taken her breath away once he shot over to Kara again. She, at least, seemed very pleased with herself to have introduced Lena to the first of her friends.

    “I’ll just go get Sara—” Barry said one second and then was gone the next. Somehow, he managed to move from one place to another so quickly that his voice held more of a presence than his actual body did. He had simply vanished. Lena was amazed.

    “Barry!” Kara shouted.

    The sound of him traveling back at such an incalculable speed had come with the low boom of what Lena could only assume was the sound barrier. Her chest grew tight in an effort to keep up— his attention span was apparently just as swift as he was. Once Barry was stationary long enough for Lena to get a better look at him, she saw that he seemed quite plain. The only peculiarity about his state of dress were the augmented soles upon each of his shoes. Other than that, he wore a button-down shirt with slim fitting pants. Lena supposed that anything looser might tend to come flying off of him at such speeds. His hazel eyes complemented the sepia brown of his hair, which surprisingly enough, kept a permanent “windswept” shape even whilst standing still. How ordinary… Lena thought. Except that she knew he was anything but.

    “Walk with us a while?” Kara asked.

    Barry guffawed as if he had been asked to cut off a limb. “Walk?!” He blustered, “You don’t wanna get back as soon as possible? Oh, geez, I just realized…”

    “Huh?”

    “Cat’s gonna freak.”

    A moment later, the unmistakable shriek of a raptor called in from above with a red-feathered dive. Kara lifted the perch of her hand skyward, seemingly to await his landing like that of a dear friend. The bird flapped his wingspan nearly a meter long with an agreeable nature and then latched onto Kara’s fist, immediately scanning the harsh, drought-ridden landscape for threats. The red-tailed hawk was so much larger up close, Lena noticed. His pale yellow eyes bore a promise, one that said, “I am capable of inflicting great pain, should you cause it.”

    Once Kara was done cooing sweetly at her friend the predator bird, she moved her gaze back towards Barry. “There’s actually some stuff I need to catch you up on first… Might soften the blow.”

    “Oh, boy.” Barry sat his hands upon his hips with that, “Well, I hate to say it, but you couldn’t have showed up at a worse time. Nyssa’s on the warpath again.”

    “What else is new?” Kara replied with a full-hearted laugh.

    Lena wasn’t sure if it was Barry’s apparent clumsiness or the look of shock that ran through him at the sound of Kara’s laughter, but he did something then that seemed even more strange than running at supersonic speeds…

    He tripped and fell flat on his face.

     

     

    It was a small, one-doored, one-windowed pueblo that was built into a massive cliffside, but to say that Lena was shocked at the amount of people emerging from it would be the understatement of the century.

    They poured out from the poorly hinged door one at a time right upon one another’s heels to wrap their arms around Kara and to cheer for her return. Lena was beginning to think that the inside of the structure was not as small as it appeared to be from the outside… She figured that it would serve her well to expect the unexpected from now on, especially where Kara was concerned. So many of them took Kara up by her arms and wrapped their own around the back of her neck; it was truly a sight to witness so much love and devotion being siphoned into one person. No wonder Kara wanted to get back so badly, Lena realized. Her entire world was right here, just waiting for her.

    After a few more joyful pats on her back, the crowd suddenly parted, and its emotional clamor soon hushed— whereupon the hurried footsteps of someone much smaller came rushing forward.

    A young girl with strawberry blonde pigtails threw herself around Kara’s waist and began to cry, bouncing up and down onto her toes as if she were unable contain her rejoicing. Lena watched as Kara dropped to her knees and hushed the girl’s crying with small kisses to each of her fine, baby-haired temples. It was clear that this child meant a great deal, but Lena was aghast at the fact that Kara had not so much as mentioned her.

    Not even once.

    Does… Kara have a daughter?

    That spiraling vortex of doubt and curiosity gained speed. Quickly, too. If it wasn’t for the chopping of air and downward gusts that screamed over the sound of turbine engine noise, Lena would have been lost to it completely. Her heart had begun to pound so intensely that she feared it might explode. Lena shielded her eyes to the wind as she watched two blondes exit the helicopter. They sprinted eagerly towards them beneath the mechanical whine of the spinning props. The crowd had finally dwindled to a few stragglers making their way back through the ramshackle door. Some of them eyed Lena with looks of mistrust, but most of them paid her little to no mind at all.

    Once the pilot and her partner arrived, Kara stood up to greet them. Lena remained silent. Why was it that she felt so anxious now, as if she were spying on something she was not yet privileged to witness? One of the women had the fiercest facial features Lena had ever beheld. Sharp cheekbones, a strong cleft chin, and a brow so severe one would think a simple look could kill, but the other woman held an expression that appeared as soft as silk. A smooth ivory complexion over the most politician-like smile. A quiet danger resided there beneath her somewhat icy exterior… and Lena knew that should she fear at least one of them, it would be her— without question. The fact that she didn’t know why, either, made her all the more nervous.

    “It is you…” The assumingly softer of the two said. The bun of her hair held fast even after running beneath the strong wind from the nearby chopper. “Kara, we never stopped looking.”

    Kara dragged a thumb beneath her eye and then waved her off, “We need to gather everyone as soon as possible- but first, there’s somebody I need you need to meet.”

    The way each of them turned to face Lena at that moment made her heart drop like the Kjeragbolten. It lay split apart upon the ground, same as the boulder.

    “Everyone, this is Lena.” Kara gave a gentle squeeze to the shoulder of the child still clinging to her leg, “I wouldn’t have made it back here without her.”

    “Hello.”

    “Welcome.”

    “Lena,” Kara gave a tuck of her chin downwards, “…this is Nat, and her mothers Sara and Ava.”

    It was all Lena could do to utter a breathy “H-hi…” in response.

    The woman named Sara stepped forward to hug Kara and then ran her fingers through the long flaxen hair that reached nearly to her waist, “This grew fast.”

    “It does that, remember?”

    Lena couldn’t help but to notice that they all seemed so familiar with one another. She wasn’t sure which of her feelings were currently fighting their way to the surface… Confusion? Guilt? Untethered jealousy? She swallowed hard. Why did her heart feel like it was breaking? She should have been happy. Kara was finally reunited with her family— but as for her own? Lost. Presumably forever. Lena was beginning to feel lightheaded with the ever-constant racing of her heart. Her mouth was dry, and she wished to speak, but she hadn’t even the first clue as to what she might say.

    “Alright,” Sara went on after a few patient blinks of her eyes, “Ava and I will take the bird around to find J’onn. We’ll meet you inside.”

    Her partner Ava finally reached out to hug Kara, sighing there upon her shoulder with an immense sort of relief. “We’re glad you’re home.” She said.

    “Me too.”

    Sara motioned for Nat to follow right behind as they turned towards the helicopter again. “Wanna take a ride?”

    Nat’s shimmering, braided pigtails swung back and forth for a moment, seemingly stuck between whether to leave or to stay.

    “Pick a mom, kid.”

    With a sure nod, Nat clutched at Kara’s leg again.

    Sara smiled. “Okay- but just so you know, Kara’s gonna be stuck with the adults for a while until we can all talk. I’ll make sure you guys get some time to catch up as soon as possible, okay?”

    “…Okay.”

    Once Sara and Ava were climbed inside of the helicopter again, they pulled away from the ground with an intimidatingly sheer angle and set off into the clear blue sky once again. Sara proved to be quite the pilot at first glance.

    Everything after that was a blur.

    Lena barely managed a full sentence whenever Kara ushered her inside the smooth clay angles of the small pueblo. It was a single room with makeshift furniture and two large doors at the back. Odd… Lena thought, don’t those doors face a cliff?

    When Kara went to pull them apart at their ramshackle hinges, the sound of open air graced Lena’s ears once again. It was a chambered echo, bringing with it the sounds of life. The world had opened up again, complete with bird song and foot traffic coming from down below. There went Peck flying overhead, another shrill screech into the air to welcome them in. Where had he come from? Inside was a vast expanse of square-topped pueblo roofs, stacked far and wide along the steep slope of the stonework stairs they stood upon. Boxes and boxes of homes built one at a time in an almost maze-like design. It was dizzying to stand there and stare at it all, quite like trying to find the “right side up” of an ancient Escher painting.

    The domiciles that were lit with lanterns upon every inch of the hollow cavern stared back at Lena, making her feel so very small and insignificant amongst its grander scheme. It was an underground city with one immense tree in its very center; a wood giant so colossal that it made the redwoods back in the Grove of Titans seem like saplings. A tree so large simply didn’t exist in nature… not to Lena’s knowledge, anyway… but there it was. What available light that was shed inside the earthen city walls filtered down through the grand tree’s magnificent crown, where just beneath was a large lake. It surrounded the massive trunk and went on for at least a kilometer or more in each direction. The water glowed, refracting the sunlight by day and likely lighting the entire city by night with some bioluminescent characteristic or other.

    Lena stood still, breathless amidst so much hidden glory.

    She felt Kara place a hand at her lower back, anchoring her once again to this unbelievable reality. Spots began to strobe around the edges of Lena’s vision, and she began to feel faint. The skin of her cheeks tingled, and she tried rolling her lips into her mouth to feel them better, but like the rest of her body, they were so very numb.

    “Welcome to the Infinite.” Kara said.

    With one last, desperate flutter of her eyelids, Lena’s knees finally decided to give out. The feeling of being wrapped up inside of Kara’s arms after that was as close to any “afterlife” she knew she would ever get…

    It was nice there.

     

     

    “Blood pressure seems to be returning to normal… All things considered.”

    Kara’s voice was thick with worry, “Considering what? What does that mean?”

    The clattering of steel examination trays was unmistakable. Kara was a veritable “bull in a china shop”, just as she had claimed to be. Lena had wondered why on earth a shop would house only porcelain wares in the first place… but she understood the metaphor better now.

    The first voice came back, calm but concerned. “Considering the fact that I can count on one hand how many human patients I’ve ever had. What were you thinking- bringing her here?”

    “I’ll explain everything later. Is she okay?”

    Lena reached into thin air once she sat up, blinking herself completely awake. She tried reacquainting herself with the events that had come before. She remembered Kara’s hand catching the back of her head on her way down, and then nothing after that…

    “I’m fine—” Lena took a deep breath as she looked around. She had been placed on a bed with clean white sheets, and with that, quickly came to the realization that it was some sort of clinic. The -whir- of a generator coming from somewhere unseen seemed to supply the place with electricity. Lena found herself staring again. She had almost mistaken it all for her lab, as it was so similar right down to its very cabinetry. Funny, she had never stopped to consider whether or not metahumans had as much access to the field of medical science as she did. “What happened?” She asked, pulling locks of her mussed hair over her shoulder.

    “It appears you suffered an acute vasovagal response.” A woman with small, innocent features and shiny brunette hair replied, “Your vitals are quickly returning to normal, so no need to fret.” She held a look of unbridled kindness upon her face once she looked up from the monitor she stood at. “I’m Caitlin.”

    “Lena.”

    Caitlin reached to pinch lightly at Lena’s chin, “Would you look straight ahead for me?”

    Lena flinched at the remarkably cold feeling of Caitlin’s fingertips as they directed her gaze.

    “Sorry…” She apologized.

    Lena frowned, “It’s alright, but I can assure you- I’m not concussed.”

    “Consider me not so easily assured, then.” Caitlin replied with an endearing smile, “Otherwise I wouldn’t be doing my job, now would I?”

    Lena felt Kara out from the corner of her eyes as she rounded the end of the table with an impatient hiss, “I said I was careful.” Then she crossed her arms in Lena’s direction and huffed, “Caitlin’s our doctor. She asks lots of questions like you do.”

    “Oh?”

    “I hope that’s alright.” Caitlin replied.

    Kara bragged, “Lena here is something of a doctor herself.”

    “Is that so?”

    Lena smirked once Caitlin was satisfied with the dilation of her pupils. “I’m a scientist- well, a geneticist, actually.”

    “Well, Miss Geneticist-Actually, I’d love to hear how you—”

    Another voice from beyond the clinic door suddenly came crashing in. “Kara—?”  Someone was trying to enter more quickly than they could scrape at the long handle of the door. It came open a second later and knocked into the metal corner of a nearby cabinet, rocking its various tools around with tinny -clink- sounds. It jarred Lena where she sat with her legs still hanging off the edge of the examination bed.

    “You…” A fiery woman with harsh eyes said as she burst inside. She looked dead ahead at Kara, hands shaking on either side of her long, flowing maroon dress.

    “Cat—”

    “You!”

    “Let me explain—”

    “I can’t believe you!” The woman named Cat sputtered, fanning her graying, short brown hair as she shook her head, “You disappear for months and have us all out of our minds with worry—”  

    Kara held her hands out in surrender as she began to take slow steps towards the upset woman, “It’s alright…”

    “How can you say that?! Oh, that’s just like you, isn’t it? Blundering through the world with no regard as to how your actions might affect anyone else. I almost didn’t believe it when I heard you had just magically shown up after so long- and that you weren’t dead, and oh- the best part- yes, that you came back with a human, no less!” She finished with an irate gesture towards Lena.

    Lena wished for once to be metahuman like the rest of them.

    To render herself invisible, and to stay that way.
