    “Cat…” Kara tried again.

    “No!” Cat took a step back once Kara was within reach of her. Her hands came flailing forward to land at Kara’s shoulders; a weak attempt to push her back. “Not knowing what had happened to you killed me, Kara. It killed me.”

    “Cat, stop.” Kara finally lashed out to wrap her arms around the other woman, who gave but a single, infuriated squeak and then began to cry. She wept quietly against the side of Kara’s neck, and then, much to Lena’s surprise, took Kara by the face and kissed her back and forth from one cheek to the other, tears streaming down to her chin all the while.

    Before long, and just as quickly and abruptly as she had arrived, the woman soon wrenched herself free of Kara’s arms with another frustrated huff and then turned to make her exit. The awkward silence that she left in her wake seemed so loud. Lena was bewildered, to say the least. It was as if a tornado had flung itself inside the small clinic for those few moments. Lena could only guess at what each of the teary-eyed woman’s kisses meant… But the more she tried to work it out, the more it made her feel sick. Lena was beginning to understand that she truly knew very little about the woman she had fallen so stupendously for. The woman whom she had left an entire life behind for.

    Even more appalling than that, Lena still never doubted her.

    She would follow Kara anywhere; of this she was certain.

    Caitlin alleviated the admittedly tense quietude before long. “Just like Cat to make everything about herself.” She vexed half-heartedly.

    “It’s not like that…” Kara shook her head, “Okay, it’s sorta like that. Sorry.”

    “Don’t be. You deal with her more than I do- and besides, that’s probably the first time she’s ever willingly stepped foot inside my clinic.”

    Kara was flush with a rose color whenever she looked back at Lena. Surely, she possessed some sort of explanation, but Lena thought not to rush her towards it. To compound so many questions with answers she might not like to hear… No, that would be a mistake.

    Apart from the many things that Lena had never stopped to consider about Kara’s homelife, there was also the tiny question of whether or not that Kara held anyone near to her heart. In a romantic manner of speaking, of course. Lena had just assumed that there was no one worth mentioning, what with the way that Kara had spoken about relationships and her mild disdain to come anywhere near them. Even the very idea of them made her sad— this Lena had seen with her own eyes.

    She was so confused.

    “I…” Lena blinked, “I should like some air, I think.”

    Kara nodded. “Right- no, of course.” She looked over her shoulder at Caitlin, “Good?”

    “Good.” Caitlin replied.

    “Thank you.”

    “Just remember, Kara…” The sound of Caitlin’s voice trailed after them, “Slowly.”

    Kara took Lena’s hand into her own with a small squeeze as they exited the clinic, not bothering to look back, “I know.”

    Once Lena had regained her bearings, she was stunned once again at the craftsmanship that went into each of the painstakingly built pueblos, with their mud and stone facades and flat, boxy rooftops. Some were stacked five tiers high where others were just a single level, with round wooden posts denoting the fronts of each. There were people ambling happily down the narrow lanes between them all, and Lena couldn’t help but to feel that it had all been there for a very long time— much longer than she could possibly guess.

    “Everything’s going to change now, isn’t it?” She asked, pulling at Kara’s hand and shuffling her feet to a stop. She needed another minute. Everything was quickly catching up with her again, and her breaths began to fall feathery and thin upon her lips.

    “Yeah…” Kara’s voice was soft as she came to a halt in the road. She brought herself to stand right in front of Lena then and bowed forward slightly, resting their foreheads together with a slow sigh through her nose. “Everything but us.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Thanks for reading <3

    Chapter 15: Weightless

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Fifteen: Weightless

     

    “Are you sure you’re up for this?”

    Lena felt a chill race beneath her skin, the memory of her first Infinite council meeting still sitting so freshly upon her mind.

    “I am. I want to help.”

    She sat atop the flat, dusty rooftop of Kara’s earthen abode, staring out past the rest of the city and towards its stupendous centerpiece. The grand tree in the middle of it all was that of a hardy oak, said to have survived thousands of years within its subterranean environs. Truly, it was an idyllic place to live so peacefully and free from all harm compared to the surface… Sunlight shone perfectly there from where it poured in through the underground city’s ceiling, an opening just large enough to swallow the leafy crown of the massive oak’s outermost branches. The rest of the cavernous stretch was lit from the very large body of water that lie valleyed beneath the tree, its bright blue bioluminescence glowing against every shadowy corner and leaving nothing to pure darkness. Kara had affectionately referred to it as the “Shimmering Sea” a time or two during all of her patient explaining. An homage to Jules Verne, if Lena had to guess. Another deep breath went in and out through her nose, and she hugged her knees tighter where she sat, content just to stare and to remember the evening that had come before. To see such a magnificent tree from a distance was one thing, but to step inside of it was another entirely.

    “This is incredible, look how big it is in here.”

    Lena turned in place several times to get a true feeling for just how large the hollowed-out trunk of the Millennium tree was. A grand name for a tree so old, to be sure. It was astonishing to know how well it had thrived under such conditions as well, knowing now that it only stood upon the shell of its former glory. A living fossil. She spied a large flag draped from a tall beam once she looked up. The faded words upon its fabric read “Union Pacific Railroad” where they fit squarely inside of a shield. It was old and frayed from years of battle, but as long as it hung there, Lena assumed it was a source of inspiration for whoever looked upon it.

    She was just about ready to hurl the bulk of her questions in Kara’s direction when an ill-timed clearing of someone’s throat stopped her in her tracks. Lena stumbled slightly as she twisted around to face a small group, some of them bearing familiar faces from just an hour or so before. There was Sara, Ava, and Cat, as well as another whom she had yet to meet.

    Someone else soon joined them, stepping silently beneath the yellow glow of hanging lamplight. It illuminated the dark leather of their hood, but not their face. They slipped a quiver and bow from their back and placed it with a quiet reverence upon a large wooden table. Chairs bespeckled the table’s outer edge, much like that of Arthurian legend. In comparison, all of Lena’s favorite fairy tales were put quite to shame here.

    “I see you have your pigeon back.” The hooded figure said as they drew further into the light.

    Peck flapped gleefully where he scuttled to stay upright on Kara’s shoulder, and Kara nodded. “Yep. Thanks for looking out for him.”

    Finally, the hood came down. It was a woman with dark hair, and even darker eyes. “Believe me, it was not by choice. He was particularly annoying in your absence.”

    Lena noted inwardly that many of the women here bore stern faces, and that it was likely they were born fighters— if their host of chosen weapons were any indication. Sara walked around with a bo-staff made from a polished, white metal which collapsed into a pair of batons, but her counterpart, Ava, seemed to be just as pleased to remain unarmed. Lena felt an immense suspicion in that regard… Either Ava was a strict pacifist, or she was more dangerous than the rest of them combined, even without a weapon. This hooded woman seemed to be quite proud, though, and appeared to handle her bow as if it were an extension of herself. The subtle second nature was easy enough to recognize… Lena took similar care whilst handling her sabre.

    “Well, someone has to be, I guess.” Kara shrugged and then turned to stage-whisper towards Lena, “This is Nyssa, don’t take anything she says seriously.”

    “Poor words of advice, I’m afraid.” Nyssa replied, “Since I was granted a temporary seat on your council.”

    “What?!” Kara threw her arms up with a huff. Peck, having his feathers ruffled from such an abrupt exclamation, took a large leap from Kara’s shoulder and then sought to glide silently out through the exit. “Since when?” She balked again.

    The severe-looking woman named Nyssa raised a notably perturbed eyebrow, “I did just mention your absence, did I not?”

    “Give it a rest, Nyssa.” Ava chimed in from the other side of the table. Her voice was firm, ringing of a warning. It was clear that she harbored a protective side. Lena wondered again what her role was in all of it…

    Nyssa spun on her heel just to jab a finger across the large table, “I’ll take orders from you when I am dead and—”

    “Alright, stop!” It was Sara that time to stretch her arms out between the two squabbling women, a gesture which Lena guessed she made somewhat often. Sara went to smooth back her sandy blonde hair and exhaled impatiently, “We didn’t have any other suitable option, Kara, and trust me- I’ve regretted it ever since.”

    “You do not mean that.” Nyssa said rather flippantly, but with an almost endearing roll of her eyes. There was something between the three of them —Sara, Ava and Nyssa— that Lena couldn’t quite place. The tension was palpable, if not obvious.

    It was right about then that a tall figure stepped out from another tucked away shadow. Plainly dressed and with his hands clasped behind his back as he walked towards the center of all the commotion, Kara started towards him immediately.

    “J’onn…” She said, running to wrap her arms around him like a dear friend.

    Lena noticed how much of Kara’s weight had been placed onto him, to which he stood and hugged Kara back as if she weighed nothing at all. The familiar feeling of embracing her own father in the same hopelessly devoted way whenever she was a child flashed to the forefront of Lena’s mind, and she was suddenly sad again. It came back in crashing waves every spare minute that her mind was free of thought; the earth-shattering realization, the despair, the anger…

    It was exhausting.

    “Lena, this is J’onn,” Kara then waved towards the last person standing idly by with their arms folded, “…and this is Amaya. They run the place.”

    “Hello.” Lena nodded.

    “Where would you like to start?” The woman named Amaya asked, though she had pointed her question directly at Kara instead as if Lena wasn’t even there.

    “Um,” Kara came up to the table and locked her elbows, resting some of her weight upon her knuckles there, “First of all, I need some assurances that Lena will be protected.”

    J’onn marbled his dark-skinned brow with an adamant expression and replied, “All those in need are welcome here- even humans. No harm shall come to her, you have my word.”

    Nyssa scoffed quietly through the straight arrow of her nose. “Still might be best not to walk alone at night, love.”

    Lena remembered just how unsettled she had felt after that, especially whenever J’onn laid his eyes on her. It was as though he could stare straight through her. After everything, Lena felt that she knew less than when she did upon her arrival… For every answer she gained, two more questions lay in its place. It was the main reason for her absconding herself on top of Kara’s sound and uncluttered roof. She was still reeling; everything made so much more sense and yet, it didn’t all at the same time.

    “Lena agreed to help me escape, and she risked everything to come here with me.”

    “I’m sorry, but it does beg the question… Why?”

    She also couldn’t stop thinking about Amaya’s unbridled harshness. It was the most efficient way to learn the truth, surely enough, but an unbiased outlook couldn’t be so pure and truly devoid of fault… could it?

    Amaya was intent to determine Lena’s hand in it all. A skilled interrogator, she was. The beautifully stoic woman had gone from the strictest sense of ignoring Lena’s very existence to addressing her directly, and Lena felt that the ground beneath her feet could have opened up and swallowed her whole without her even knowing. Amaya’s very presence was bewitching in the way that she seemed to take up more space than anyone but was no larger or smaller than any of the people who stood on her council. Lena attributed her unflappable personality to the fact that she was very likely a remnant. Kara was right, as per usual… they did tend to stand out. That, and the distrustful nature of remnants was infamous, known far and wide. Lena knew then and there that she would only need to convince this one woman, and then hopefully, the rest would follow.

    “I know you’re looking for the sort of truth that someone like me would be hard pressed to admit,” Lena replied, feeling all eyes upon her now, “So instead of struggling to convince you- which I do very much wish to do, I’ll tell you everything. Who I am, where I came from, what I did… All of it.”

    Amaya raised an ochre eyebrow at that. “Very well.”

    “I also want to make one more thing very clear.”

    “Go on…”

    “Kara didn’t persuade me to do anything. That’s not why I’m here.” Lena looked timidly towards the blonde who merely smiled in agreement, “She only supplied me with the right questions, and with the courage I needed to go in search of answers. I found them.” She clasped her hands and took in a deep breath, “I’m here because I need your help. Without Kara, I would still be interviewing metahumans and exploiting their powers- and to what end, I truly don’t know- but whatever’s happening back in Oregon is wrong. I know that now. The facility I worked at houses metahumans against their will, and I’m terrified of what they might be doing to them while we all stand here and deliberate. Please, if there is any way that you can help them, then I would implore you to at least consider it.”

    Amaya looked over at J’onn. He gave a sure and simple nod back at her, almost as if he knew that Lena was telling the truth. It made Lena’s skin crawl. How could one person make her feel so naked and inconsequential but still appear to be so kind and understanding at the same time?

    “I will hear what you have to say, and then our council will reconvene privately to discuss the matter further. Thank you for your honesty.”

    Kara crossed her arms and chimed in again with a sad tone, “There’s something else…” She cleared her throat with a readied tone, “Alex is alive.”

    The look of disbelief was plain to see around the table. Ava and Sara both stood there with their mouths held slightly agape, fully speechless. It was the sound of Cat’s voice which eventually cut in over the deafening silence…

    “Are we to assume that she was the one to tell you this?” Cat asked with an obvious darting of her hand towards Lena, “The same person who claims to have held you prisoner?”

    Kara shook her head. “No, I—” She huffed a tired breath, “Look, I get that it’s a shock… but I wouldn’t be standing here saying it if I didn’t believe it myself.”

    “So, you saw her with your own eyes, then?”

    “Sort of- not exactly.”

    “Then how do you really know?”

    “Because I know my sister!” Kara twisted her gaze seemingly to beseech one of the only other remnants in the room, “Amaya…”

    Cat continued, “Did it ever occur to you that whatever they gassed you with so many times messed with your head?”

    Lena raised a hand, “It’s an incapacitating agent, actually. Completely harmless.”

    Cat answered quickly with a branded level of snark, “I’m sure.”

    Kara tried defending herself once again, “You know as well as I do that remnants can sense one another.”

    “Yes, but it could have been anyone!”

    Lena felt Kara move in front of her at that point and gestured with her eyes down towards the sabre at her side. Knowing that this might happen, Lena agreed to hand it over without complaint. Kara then turned towards the council again and laid the sword down upon the table.

    “She’s alive. See for yourself.”

    Out of everyone, Lena was most surprised to see Sara shuffling around the scattered chairs to make her way to the other side of the table. She laid her hands upon the sword without hesitation and a revelation seemed to come over her once she thumbed over the hilt’s insignia. She spent a single, quiet sob with it and then placed it back down.

    What she did next surprised everyone.

    Sara made straight for Lena, and with her hands held out, she went to take her around either side of her head, palms flat against her temples.

    “Sara—” Kara raised her voice, “Sara, wait!”

    Instantly flung out of the Millennium tree’s hollow trunk, Lena felt as if she had been rocked by a clap of soundless thunder. Her mind was suddenly not her own. Painted there behind her eyes were images of painfully familiar corridor walls, as well as the doors that came with them.

    Kara’s cell.

    Lena’s bedroom.

    Her father’s office.

    The more Lena fought to get out from beneath Sara’s influence, the deeper she sank into it. Her first day at the compound in Sisters, Oregon… Crying beneath the smooth, violet light of her jellyfish tank…

    Twists and turns they took around the grand mezzanine of Lena’s memory. Sara was relentlessly in search of something. At last, the breakneck speed at which those memories came to a halt outside of a smoldering forge stunned Lena once again. Sara had found was she was looking for.

    It was the blacksmith who handed Lena her very first sabre. A gift from her father… It had been so sharp that day that she had accidentally slashed her first instructor across the hand. Her training would prove to be much more grueling after that.

    The blacksmith never smiled. Not even once. Why had Lena not noticed such a dour expression before? The soot covered woman had reddish-brown hair and kept her comings and goings as busy as possible, as if she wanted more than anything to make each of her interactions with Lena as fast and uninterrupted as possible. It was strange to relive the moment, even if doing so was not of her own choosing.

    Time quickly sped up again. They stopped at the slap across Lena’s face, and then again at the sound of a waterfall. The breathy kisses at the base of Lena’s neck… The brittle crunch of pinecones beneath her knees…

    When Sara released her, Lena fluttered her eyes open to the tune of birdsong and felt dizzier than what she ever considered humanly possible— almost like the entire organ of her brain had been flipped upside down inside of her skull and then trampled upon. She felt Kara’s arms beneath her own, catching her as her knees went out from beneath her a second time. All that was left after that was the utterly destroyed sound of Sara’s voice, riddled with tears…

    “I can’t believe it…” She panted, “It’s really Alex.”

    The sound of knuckles rapping against the wooden trapdoor of Kara’s roof quickly thrust Lena back to the present. She made sure to wipe beneath her eyes before being seen, unsure if there were still tears there or not. Lena wasn’t convinced that she would ever become fully adjusted to such a prolonged state of shock, though, hearing the welcome sound of Kara’s voice soothed her nerves to a dull, static roar.

    “You okay?” She asked, poking her head through.

    Lena shrugged as she crossed her legs. “Still don’t know how to answer that, unfortunately.”

    “That’s alright.” Kara replied, taking one careful step up and onto the roof and then walking over to sit with Lena. She seemed comfortable enough striding upon the solid structure of her home— fearing very little that it might collapse under her weight. It was a good place to escape, the roof. Kara acted as though she had done the very same at least a thousand times.

    “So, what do you think?”

    Lena looked at her, finding solace there in the grateful smile upon Kara’s face. She hadn’t done so much in front of anyone else still, and that was a comfort to Lena, for some reason… Kara’s smiles still belonged to her. For now, anyway.

    “What do I think about…?”

    Kara gestured vaguely. She meant the Infinite, of course. “It’s incredible.” Lena shrugged again, “I would have never imagined a place like this to even exist.”

    “I need it to be kept secret.”

    “Yes, of course. Kara—” Lena sighed and then scooted on her hands and hips to face Kara, “I just want you to know, I would never do anything to threaten your safety here. When I left Oregon with you, I left all of it.”

    Kara smiled again and took one of Lena’s hands into her own. She rubbed her thumb idly back and forth and then, for whatever reason, her smile slowly faded. It seemed like it pained her to do so, but she let go of Lena’s hand after a moment, choosing just to keep her own within the space of her lap. That awful, guilty -pang- behind Lena’s chest leapt forward again, kicking hard at the middle of her heart and causing her to blurt unexpectedly.

    “Is Cat—” Lena pursed her lips, knowing it was too late to turn back, “Is she special to you? Or, I mean, was she at one time or another?”

    To be honest, Lena hadn’t been able to stop thinking about the whirlwind encounter inside of Cailtin’s ER. That, as well as the umbrage that Cat had displayed towards her. It sparked of jealousy, or some other manner of hurt feelings. Lena just wanted to have it figured out. She hoped that it would eventually stop plaguing her if she could just wrap her head around the meaning of it.

    Kara crossed her legs longways where they sat facing the Millennium tree and sighed. The chirping of birds all the way towards the center of the city was dwindling with the setting of the sun, where the last few glimmers of light were slowly drawing to a close. It looked as if the tree’s grand crown had been set ablaze. It was profoundly beautiful, Lena thought.

    “It’s complicated.” Kara replied.

    “So, yes?”

    Kara grimaced, “I’d like to sit here and tell you ‘no’, but I’m not sure if that would be a lie or not. All I know is that we were close, and now we’re not.”

    “That’s it?”

    “She fell in love with me.”

    Lena felt short of breath. The admission was like ripping off a bandage. “But… you didn’t feel the same?”

    Kara gave a sullen shake of her head “no” in reply.

    “I see.”

    “I knew her parents.” Kara dropped her head back, dragging the gloriously gold ends of her hair against the smooth clay of the rooftop, “And her grandparents, too. I won’t deny that there were feelings between us. We shared a bond that I don’t think I could even put into words, but she…” Kara brought her head upright then and closed her eyes with a hint of disappointment, “She wanted things that I couldn’t give her.”

    Lena was almost afraid to ask. “Like…?”

    “A home. Children. White picket fence, all of it.”

    What did a picket fence have to do with anything? Lena sat with that for a while. Something washed the back of her tongue before long, quite like the taste of bile. “Did you ever—”

    “No,” Kara shook her head preemptively, “No, we were never together. We may have shared some moments over the years, but they were nothing more than a handful of stolen kisses. I took too long to tell her how I felt about it. That’s the truth.”

    Lena nodded. “It just seemed like you two shared a pretty rich history. I’ll be honest, it made me feel like I was being turned inside out- seeing you like that with someone else. Is that selfish of me?”

    “I don’t think so.”

    The way that Kara kept looking at her was almost more painful than anything. The genuine expression of want apparent in her eyes, and the way she had to stop herself from reaching for Lena’s hand again… Lena knew that they were just torturing themselves at this point. If Kara had been willing to pass up a life with someone who loved her so deeply, then what chance did Lena —a human— truly stand? The sacrifices Kara had made…

    A fate worse than death, I think.

    How lonely that must be…

    “I don’t want you to make me any more promises, Kara.” Lena confessed past the awful quivering of her lip. It hurt so much just to hold fast to the “right thing”, just as Kara did. She was so much stronger, though, Lena had to admit. She had so many more years of practice…

    …and Lena was still so new to everything. Especially loss.

    “What do you mean?” Kara asked.

    Peck came flying over to land at the end of a wooden viga that extended out past the top of the roof. He chose to sit in silence with them, admiring the amber glow of fading sunlight which crept further and further away.

    Lena raised her knees to her chest again and hugged her arms around them, needing to feel safe again amidst so much unspoken agony. “You did everything you could to keep me safe, and to get us here in one piece… After your next council meeting, you’ll have fulfilled the last of your promises. I’d just like you to not make any more of them. You don’t owe me anything.”

    “But I do.” Kara blinked.

    “No, you don’t.”

    “I—”

    “Kara, please,” Lena felt her eyes welling up with tears again, “I can’t know what it’s like to love you, and then to lose you. We’re agreed on that, aren’t we?” She hung her head, hating the way that it felt to be so painfully honest, “I can’t become another version of Cat.”

    Lena had never seen such a look of hurt upon Kara’s face. That, in itself, was the worst part.

    Kara frowned with a nod, seemingly settled upon a decision, “Fine.”

    “Fine?”

    “I won’t make you any more promises.”

     

     

    It was a cough that rattled in their chest.

    The fact that the child was also three years old by the time they had first seen the surface of the world was not lost on Lena, either.

    The Infinite provided an oasis of sorts from the cruelty of the world above, but it was awe-inspiring nonetheless to witness the faces of young children looking upon a bright blue sky as if it were still their first time seeing it. It would never get old. Daytrips to the outside world required vigilant supervision. Kara had volunteered to join that morning, so, of course, the offer for Lena to come along as well came shortly thereafter. Mick had sort of grunted it, rather than asking her outright. Either Kara had berated him into it, or no one there seemed to trust her very much still.

    No one besides Caitlin, that is.

    The child coughed again, to which that time went a small, static -zap- along with it. The air in the clinic seemed to buzz with all of the teary-eyed, raspy hacking. Lena had reached for the stethoscope when it suddenly shocked her, and she almost dropped it with her surprise. She looked towards the three-year-old on the table then with a dumbfounded expression and finally noticed every hair upon their head standing on end.

    “They don’t understand what’s happening- and why it’s so difficult for me to hold them whenever they can’t stop coughing…” The child’s mother said. She seemed just as frazzled, hair mussed by the same static electricity.

    Lena was fascinated. Caitlin had called for her earlier that day to come and sit in on the exam, possibly in the hopes that Lena might have an opinion on what ailed the child. Lena prepared herself for yet another unintentional shock as she motioned closer with the bell of the stethoscope…

    “May I?”

    The mother currently at her wits-end gave a look to Caitlin, who then prompted Lena to go ahead. Lena carefully placed the tool with its listening diaphragm down upon the child’s upper back and listened for the “snaps” and “crackles” that she assumed to be allergy-induced electrokinesis. She was amazed to hear the phenomena so superbly inside such a small metahuman… Powers typically had a way of presenting themselves around a more pubescent age, with some outliers being “late bloomers”, of course, but in the event of illness or life-threatening circumstances, some children could present far sooner than others. These patients tended to have more of a handle on their abilities by the time they arrived at adulthood, granted that their powers were of a gentler nature. That was not always the case, Lena had found. Sometimes, the danger could never be fully reined in.

    “Their symptoms began when?” Lena looked up to question the mother.

    “Um. Right after the thaw, I guess.”

     “And I assume you experienced the whole of your pregnancy during the freeze here?”

    “Yes… They’ve only ever known the cold.”

    Lena nodded. “The climate, I’m afraid, may be lending a hand towards your child’s coughing.” She turned to speak with Caitlin, “Is it always this warm and dry outside of winter?”

    “Pretty much.” Caitlin agreed, “So, you’ve landed on environmental allergies?”

    “Was… that incorrect?”

    Caitlin shook her head with a more-or-less satisfied expression, “No. I concur.”

    The child’s mother still seemed to possess the need to express her concern, “I’ve bathed them a few times in the shimmering waters, which seemed to help for a while. What else should I be doing?”

    “I would suggest you continue that if they’re agreeable enough, or for when the coughing becomes too much. We can work on a more long-term solution in the meantime.”

    “What kind of mother can’t hold their sick child?” She lamented further, “They blew out the fuse box on the side of the house the other day. Sparks went everywhere—”

    Caitlin reached forward to console the woman, “Everything will be fine. We’ll get straight to work on it.”

    “Might I offer a suggestion?” Lena added, “Have you thought about bringing the water to them? To have it inside the house, I mean, and to put it into the air.”

    “How?”

    “Well, I could,” Lena shrugged, “…build a humidifier, of sorts. If the climate here exacerbates their allergies, then it might be prudent to battle the climate on its own turf. Wherever possible, of course, like a bedroom, or playroom. Something on a smaller scale might do, I could maybe draw something up for you?”

    “You would do that?”

    Lena felt herself smile again. Those had been so few and far between lately. “I would be happy to help.”

    The child gave one last cough that expelled more like a sneeze, and the lights inside the clinic flickered momentarily with their effort. They began to cry again, and reached with flapping hands for their mother, uttering puled words like “no more” and “go home” to illustrate their physical discomfort.

    Once they were both gone from the clinic, Lena was busying her hands with whatever she could find in lieu of Caitlin’s assessment of her. She knew that was why she had been asked there that day. To be sized up in real time, without any bias or preconceived notions that a human might know any more or any less about metahuman physiology. Caitlin was perpetually kind, to say the least. Lena felt that she could breathe easily around her.

    “Thank you for the assistance.” Caitlin mentioned while washing her hands in a nearby sink.

    “I hardly assisted,” Lena replied, “But I’m glad to have been invited back.”

    “Yeah?”

    Lena nodded and then leaned gently against the polished steel surface of a countertop, “There are a lot of ‘unknowns’ for me, going forward. I’m not quite sure where I fit anymore… if I fit anywhere at all.”

    “I can imagine how that would be hard.” Caitlin replied, drying her hands with a small, white cloth. “You gave up everything to come here? Just like that? I’m sorry if my asking sounds so banal- I’m just not sure of another way to ask.”

    “It’s alright. I did, yes.”

    “For Kara?”

    “Erm—” Lena swallowed dryly, flustered by another very pointed question, “Well, it was the right thing to do. To leave, I mean.”

    “What was the deal there? Where you came from? I’ll be honest, there’s a lot of talk that comes in and out of this door, but I almost never get the full picture.”

    Lena pursed her lips with a frown and then looked down at the smooth, grayish tile of the floor.

    “Too much?” Caitlin asked.

    Lena gave a shy tilt of her head, “Sort of. I’m still coming to terms with everything, like I said.”

    “I’m sorry—”

    “You don’t have to apol—”

    “You’re just unlike any other human I’ve ever met.”

    Lena reared her head back with that, though a bit more than she had intended to. She didn’t mean to look so taken aback by the statement, it was just that both of them seemed to possess the same kind of anxious rambling, the kind which blurted inner thoughts into being outer thoughts. Caitlin recovered quickly, and with much more grace than if Lena were to do so.

    “You must have met some rather unsavory ones, I assume.”

    “Yeah. Actually.” Caitlin went on, “But I know they’re not all bad. I’m just trying to figure you out, I guess. That’s just the scientist in me.”

    Lena nodded in agreement, “I can understand that. I had a far different upbringing than most, I suppose. I was… sheltered.”

    “Ah.”

    “Privileged.”

    “Mm.”

    “And spoiled rotten, to be perfectly honest.”

    “Really?” Caitlin finally teased another smile across her face, “I would have never guessed.”

    Lena gave a playful narrowing of her eyes in return, “I can’t tell if that’s sarcasm or not.”

    They laughed for a while, right up until there was a knock at the clinic’s door. Caitlin quickly ducked behind a tall, rolling cabinet and hushed a finger over her lips towards Lena, whispering desperately, “If that’s Mick, tell him I’ve stepped out!”

    “Mick? Why?”

    The door creaked open upon its hinges, and Kara’s voice came floating inside, “Hello…? Caitlin?”

    Caitlin withered from her icy posture immediately, heaving a cold breath of relief. “Here—” She came out with a wave, “Just inventorying- um- supplies, and stuff. Totally not hiding.”

    “Mhm.” Kara hummed a deadpan note, “Mick’s still on daycare duty.”

    Lena’s confusion fell breathily upon her lips, “I’m lost.”

    Kara smiled and jabbed a finger towards the doctor, “Boyfriend.”

    “He is not my boyfriend.” Caitlin shot back with a huff, giving a similarly adamant swish of her white coat for good measure.

    “Yeah, might mention that to him the next time he comes ‘round with a bouquet of snowdrops.”

    “You’re never going to forget that, are you?”

    “Nope.”

    Caitlin sighed with her head in her hands, “Awesome.”

    Kara veered her eyes delightfully back towards Lena and tossed her head in the direction of the door as if to say, “let’s go”. It made her heart leap with joy. The damned thing, it had absolutely no will power of its own. It was up to Lena’s head to keep everything in check, and even that was fuzzy more than half of the time. Lena wanted so desperately to spend every available minute she had with Kara, but also to never see her again for how much it hurt just to be around her. Lena craved what she couldn’t have, and she didn’t have the first clue as to how to rid herself of the feeling. Not with Kara being so close at hand. Peace and quiet was what she would need soon… and to cry again, possibly.

    “Walk you home?” Kara asked, that charming smile of hers wiping all sense of caution from Lena’s mind.

    Lena nodded wordlessly. She waved a small goodbye to Caitlin and was hand in hand with Kara before she knew it. Neither of them pulled away like they had done a time or two earlier that day, as if they had brushed up against fire. They were both wary to trespass the very clear line they had drawn between them, but their bodies, apparently, had agreed to no such thing. It seemed harmless enough, to walk and hold hands at the same time.

    “How was your afternoon?” Lena finally asked.

    “Good. Wanted to hang out with Nat for a bit before the council meeting tonight, so I dropped by.”

    “That reminds me. There’s something else which I’ve been meaning to ask—”

    “Natina is my goddaughter.”

    “…oh.”

    “I’m ashamed to admit it, but I didn’t exactly want to be at first- the responsibility seemed so overwhelming.”

    “That’s understandable, considering everything.”

    “If anything happened to her parents, she wouldn’t have anyone. I mean,” Kara looked out towards the rest of the city, “A village, maybe, but someone who could truly look after her? I realized I couldn’t say no. Better to be there for her, just in case.”

    “I can tell she loves you very much.”

    Kara smiled, “She’s amazing. And so much like her moms- it’s mind blowing.”

    They strolled silently again for a while until a turn in the road opened up towards the long, sloping bank of the Shimmering Sea. Leggy cranes both gray and white stood at the water’s edge, drinking and preening comfortably without a worry in the world. Kara had mentioned once that it was easy for the elegant waterfowl to drop down inside of the Infinite, but far more difficult for them to find their way back out again— thus creating the weekly “Crane Walk”. It was an occasion for the community of metahumans to come together on a chosen morning and to escort the birds back aboveground. There were many things like this that generated good will between neighbors, Lena noticed. They truly were a family here, albeit a very large one.

    The sounds of children playing echoed off the tall domed ceiling, their laughter skipping like smooth rocks along the water’s glassy surface. Kara finally came to a stop in the patchy grass and swung their clasped hands back and forth as if she were enjoying herself.

    “I love it here.” She said with a happy sigh.

    Lena could see why. It was a paradise, pure and simple. “I believe you. It’s perfect.”

    “Do you wanna get in?”

    “What—?” Lena flustered for a moment, “You mean in the water?”

    “Sure. It’s not actually that deep.”

    Lena chuckled beneath her breath after taking another look around, “…But with our clothes on?”

    “They’ll dry.”

    “Mmm…” Lena considered it.

    Kara toed out of her boots and then pinched the legs of her jeans at the knee as she tread slowly into the water, sinking all the way down until she was at least waist deep. “C’mon,” She waved eagerly back towards the shoreline, “It’s like nothing you’ve ever felt before. You gotta at least try it.”

    Lena sighed with her hands upon her hips. The water did shimmer brightly on its own, bioluminescent and almost ethereal with the way it rippled around the end of Kara’s soaked shirt. She decided just to go ahead and humor her, knowing it was far more likely she might tame an actual bull than to refuse this one. Lena stepped out of her shoes and winced at the sudden coldness of the water once it gained all the way up her bare thighs. Her shorts were an ideal choice for the warmer weather, even though she was still getting used to showing so much of her skin to complete strangers. She had found Kara’s eyes glued to her legs every once in a while since she had worn them and decided that they weren’t so bad after all.

    Like always, Kara was right.

    The water was unlike anything she had ever experienced. It felt almost weightless where she was submerged within it, each step taking her deeper and deeper. Lena was watching her toes sink into the bright copper sand at the bottom and was amazed to see through it all so clearly. It glowed with a brilliant effervescence around her alabastrine skin. Slowly, Lena began to feel her stress melt away. She watched and watched, right until she ran into Kara with an “oop” sound out from her mouth.

    Lena laughed as she pawed at the tops of Kara’s shoulders, “Sorry.”

    “It’s great, right?”

    “It’s…” Lena paused, “I don’t understand how this exists.”

    “You said that about me, too. Remember?”

    “That’s because you’re incredible—” Lena suddenly clapped her mouth shut, afraid to say anymore. She didn’t have to. Kara understood full well what she had meant.

    I love you.

    Kara drew herself forward and took Lena up by the small of her back, bringing them to stand closely together inside the gently rippling water. Lena rolled her lips into her mouth to hide an unruly whimper. She wanted so badly just to pour herself over Kara like she had done before beneath all of the sequoias, but they had decided to cork that bottle. Hadn’t they?

    She had, at least.

    Still, there went that pressure building up inside of her, threatening to bubble up and spill over at any minute.

    Dammit.

    Oh, this feels like dying.

    “Kara…” Lena placed a hand flat at Kara’s clavicle, pushing her away.

    “I wanted to ask you something.” Kara forged her way ahead; despite the space that Lena was trying to instill between them.

    Lena stopped. “What?”

    “After it’s all over- after we bring my sister home and help whoever else that needs us, I was wondering if you might stay here. If you wanted to, of course.”

    “Kara.” Lena said again, a bit more patience to her tone this time.

    “I won’t lie to you, Lena. It’s hard enough knowing that I can’t be with you- but I also know now that I can’t be without you.”

    “…Kara.”

    “I want you to stay.” Kara begged, dropping her brow forward to rest against Lena’s, “I would do anything for you to stay.”

    Lena rolled her head back and forth. “Why are you doing this?”

    “I can’t help it.”

    With one last frown, Kara bowed the rest of the way to press her face into Lena’s neck. She gave a small sniffle there, and then —to add to Lena’s heartbreak— she wet a few tears, unable to keep them to herself any longer. Lena shushed her for a moment and fingered through her long, golden tresses, knowing that there was no answer in the world worth sharing at that point. It was futile; she knew it in her heart.

    Staying would hurt them both, but leaving might all but destroy them.

    Chapter End Notes

    Been a couple of weeks since I've updated. I'm on a new OCD medication that's made me feel kind of sluggish and uninspired, but the constant ass-kicking inside my head has dramatically waned. I can't figure out if I'm too tired to be mean to myself, or if it's actually working. The nausea and loss of appetite isn't great either but that should all go away after a few more weeks, I'm told. I'm hoping to post some steamy chapters soon, because they're coming up-- don't you worry. LOL
