    ACT III

     

    Chapter Twenty: Wherefore Art Thou, My God?

     

    Kara pushed at the handlebars of her motorcycle, walking it towards the top of the incline outside of the large hatch doors. The steel entryway folded closed again behind them with that automatic whine it always made, the ringing -pop- from their tired hydraulic cylinders striking Kara with a feeling of loss already— should that be the last time she would ever hear it?

    Would she ever come back home?

    “Is everything alright?” Lena asked as she strolled alongside. She was eyeing the gremlin bell hanging from the bike’s slightly tarnished frame— an object to “scoop” evil spirits up from the road, trapping them inside and thusly protecting the rider. It had been a gift from Cat several years ago. Kara hadn’t any reason to remove it.

    Kara nodded, “Yeah, it’s just…” She looked over her shoulder back at the cactus riddled hillside and then kicked the stand down for the bike, “I’m just realizing how much I’ll miss my books.”

    Lena smiled with a well-timed tilt of her head. The moment smacked of the first time they had both left a place they shared together, both of them knew it. Kara’s dark-haired passenger was quick to ease her hesitation, “You’ll come back to them.”

    “Yeah?”

    “Mhm,” Lena tucked her chin with a sure nod, “A guarantee.”

    The realization that those were only words born of wishful thinking wasn’t lost on Kara; even so, the determination in Lena’s eyes seemed to calm her restless spirit just enough. Kara hadn’t slept a wink the night before. It was all she could do just to watch Lena breathe throughout each of her fretful dreams. She was constantly surprising her in these little ways; proving that she was so much stronger than anyone ever gave her credit for. Lena simply wouldn’t admit that she was scared. Kara would do well not to even bring it up. The lamp of Lena’s courage would have to shine brightly for the both of them.

    Kara walked over and thumbed at the smooth hilt of Lena’s sabre, “Are you prepared to use this?”

    “I—” Lena stammered for a moment and then seemed to leap over her unsubtle dithering all at once, “For whatever is necessary, yes. I am.”

    “We managed to slip past a lot of very real threats on our way towards Union territory, but you have to understand that they’re waiting for us, just on the other side of the Colorado.”

    “I understand.”

    Kara narrowed her eyes, but just to get one more good look at the features on Lena’s face. Such beautiful eyes she had, one barely green and one barely blue. Not a freckle in sight other than the few around her neck and collarbones— gifts sent straight from angel’s lips. Lena gave a small bounce forward and onto her toes…

    “I mean it, Kara. I’m ready to—”

    The sound of a thundering four-beat-gait came upon them all of a sudden, quieting Lena’s fervent insisting. There were two of them, in fact. Galloping hoofbeats from a pair of horses. Their riders came into view from around the hill and slowed upon their approach, kicking up a cloud of red clay dust in their wake.

    Sara pulled at the reins of her dappled mount to turn him around in small circles, easing his eagerness to bolt into a gallop again. “Aw,” She said, “…thought we’d have some catching up to do. Oh, well.”

    “See,” Ava replied with a careful lean out of her saddle, “I told you we didn’t need to take the flyer.”

    Kara sputtered for a split-second, “What—” Then she threw her hands up in in confusion, “What are you guys doing here?”

    “We’re coming with you.” Sara replied matter-of-factly, still reining in her rather proud steed.

    “No… No, you’re not.”

    Ava reached to pat at the neck of her white-maned palomino, “You know it’s pointless arguing with her.”

    “Now, just hang on a second—” Kara smacked the sides of her legs with a huff, “You can’t just drop everything and come with us.”

    “And why not?”

    “Because- because Nat?! You want her to have some kind of a family, right? And also, who the hell told you we were leaving?”

    Kara’s mind instinctively wheeled back to J’onn, but then she set her eyes upon Lena. Maybe the expression upon her face landed a bit too harshly; Lena immediately placed her hands skyward in defense of her supposed actions…

    “As I said, there’s no such thing as a secret in the Infinite.”

    “You didn’t…”

    “Kara, they only want to help.”

    “But Nat—!”

    Ava responded then with a tone she reserved only for the battlefield; such a firm and unyielding sort of presence it held. “Don’t you yell at her.”

    Lena merely gave a silent nod of “thanks” in reply.

    Kara decided just to reel her mouth shut again at that point. She didn’t want to run the risk of smacking herself upside the head should the volume of her voice go unchecked. Granted, she’d been made to do more humiliating things against her own will in the past, like hold her breath until the edges of her vision began to spot and turn white— some of her more “heated” arguments with Cat had required Ava’s rather unique way of intervening.

    “You really thought we’d be fine with letting you go alone?” Sara hacked a laugh, “You wouldn’t make it two days without us.”

    Kara clenched her teeth. Why did Sara always have to be so smug? She did it to ruffle Kara’s feathers, obviously, either to prepare her for the worst or to set her up for success. It didn’t matter which in the end. Sara knew exactly how to press Kara’s buttons, but all of the right ones, of course. Even when Kara didn’t want her to.

    “Made it just fine for nearly two months on the road without—”

    “Remind me,” Sara cut her off again, “Weren’t you practically half dead by the time you stumbled the rest of the way here?”

    “Stop it!”

    “No, you stop it!”

    “Turn around and go back, Sara- I meant what I said, Natina needs at least one living mother.”

    Sara shook her head, “She’ll have three when we get back.”

    “Would you just listen?!” Kara saw Ava dropping her brow into her palm, rubbing lightly there at her temples to ease her disappointment. She’d seen worse spats, after all. “We need people here to protect everyone else. Wait… damn.” Kara bellowed a sigh, “Cat was right. Don’t… don’t tell her that I said that.”

    “But so were you. Don’t you see that?” Sara replied, “You told me once that peace can only exist when there are people willing to wage war for it. That’s why we’re here, to go to war with you.”

    Lena finally spoke up again, “Kara, people sleep peaceably in their beds at night only because rough men stand ready to do violence on their behalf…”

    “Please, babe, don’t quote George Orwell to me right now.” Kara replied.

    “Suit yourself. And for the record, I didn’t ask them to come.”

    “But you knew they would if they caught wind of it.”

    “Well. I’m not going to apologize, if that’s what you’re after.”

    “I don’t want—” Kara sighed at last and then dropped her hands to hang at her sides again, “That’s not what I want.”

    “I’m glad.”

    “I’ll go along with it, for now, I guess.  But… that doesn’t mean I have to like it.”

    “Good.” Sara interjected with a roll of her eyes before leading her horse towards the beaming horizon, “Now she gets it.”

    Kara grumbled and shook her head, “Alright, what’s that supposed to mean?”

    “Nothing… Except for, you know, the fact that you’re not actually the boss of us…”

    “Hey!” Kara shot back, “Why don’t you just—” Suddenly, she stumbled forward and fell onto her hands and knees, shocked for the way that her left foot had mysteriously tripped over her right. The ground had quaked upon her impact, and she brought a fist down with it to mark her frustration. Kara eventually dragged her head back up and then stood again, growling beneath her breath, “Dammit, Ava.”

    Ava simply smiled as she led her horse around, “One more thing… Be nice to my wife.”

    “Fine.”

    “Great! Shall we get a move on, then?”

    “I guess, but don’t come crying to me when none of us make it back!” Kara shouted on the tips of her toes towards the pair of fair-haired riders. She landed with a huff and then turned back to Lena, who wore a pensive, almost guilty sort of look now.

    “Please don’t be cross with me?” She said.

    “I’m not. I just… I can’t bear the thought of something terrible happening to the people I love. I mean, what if something happens to—”

    “Stop that…” Lena pressed a finger to Kara’s lips and then hushed her all the way quiet with a kiss. “You just have to trust that everything will be okay.”

    “You mean, you want me to have faith?”

    “Isn’t that what that is?”

    Kara shrugged, “Pretty much. Yeah.”

    “Then yes, have faith in them. Have faith in me.”

    Kara swept the seat of her motorcycle of its remaining dust and then gave it a small pat, “In you?” She smiled as she helped Lena to climb on, “Easy. You’re what faith is made of, beautiful.”

     

     

    —Two Weeks Later—

     

    “I’m sorry, my love.”

    Kara frowned, tapping the road-weary frame of her motorcycle one last time with the steel toe of her boot. “It’s okay.”

    “Are you sure?” Lena asked, rubbing small circles against Kara’s back.

    “No. But, it’s just a machine.”

    “What about this one—?” Sara shouted from where her head was craned beneath the hood of an old junkyard flatbed. She turned out of the truck’s engine bay to toss a small object towards Kara, who caught it with a gleam of hope in her eye. It soon disappeared, and her disappointed frown returned.

    “Too short.” Kara tossed the spark plug away, right along with any hope of fixing her bike. The plug was rusted shut at its gap, anyway. There was no chance of it supplying any kind of electricity. Not in this century. And besides that, she had no real way of knowing what the issue even was to begin with. The bike simply wouldn’t start again. It was long-since tired, and now, it was apparently done.

    Sara continued her mechanic-laced grumbling and stomped back over towards the first ancient contraption that she’d picked apart in search of an answer. “Why do they all have to look the same, anyway? ‘Spark plugs’… How can you tell the difference?” She asked as she dove up to her elbows in engine parts again. Spare pieces came flying over her shoulders, tossed directly behind her without any care as to where they might land.

    “I just can, that’s all.”

    “Pitiful design, anyway, these engines. I mean, what idiot looked at millions-of-years-old dinosaur soup and thought, ‘hey, that’s a good idea for—” Sara stopped as soon as Ava cleared her throat. Her head came back up, a hint of rust over her left eye, and she laughed apologetically through her teeth, “…Um, sorry.”

    Kara couldn’t stand to look at her cherished chopper any longer. She dropped her eyes to the ground and turned away, ready to make for the pass up ahead on foot. “Thanks for trying.”

    “Yeah. No problem.” Sara replied as she dusted her hands.

    Ava soon took to disrobing the horses of all their tack and saddles, and one by one clapped them away with loud stomps and whistles. Surprised, Lena immediately went to protest…

    “What are you doing?” She called after Ava, “Wait!”

    Kara caught Lena by the waist just as she went to bolt after the escaping equines, “They have better chances of surviving on their own from here on out, just relax.”

    “But—!”

    “The canyon path is too treacherous up ahead. It’s too eroded away and steep in some places, trust me.” Kara hugged Lena as she continued to wrestle herself free, “Let ‘em go, Lena.”

    “But… Kara.”

    Sara rolled her sleeves down and glanced back at the horses running with the wind, “Don’t worry, Gary’ll probably make it all the way back to Laredo. He’s not as dumb as he looks.”

    Lena ran still, blinking idly to herself, “What kind of name is Gary for a horse?”

    “…I have no idea, actually.”

    Deciding to let loose of Lena’s waist now, Kara hoisted her own saddlebag back over her shoulder and then nudged her chin towards the pass, “C’mon, we should take the trail north into the canyon. Once we’re clear of the outskirts we can find a place to camp inside where there’s more cover.”

    Sara seemed to agree. At first, anyway. “Any reason why you’re still taking us around the buttelands?” She asked, staring intensely at Kara. Kara shirked her head away in an instant. She wouldn’t hold still long enough for Sara to get a clear picture of what was in her head. She’d have to piece it together from the fading wisps of her memories as they trailed out of her mind. “Alright,” Apparently, Sara caught on just as she always did, “What are you hiding?”

    Kara shook her head furiously, needing to take herself away from the subject as quickly as possible— lest her mind land on the hard gnashing of teeth, and the sounds of a near-century-long grudge. “Nothing. You’re just nosy.”

    “Call me nosy all you want, but it’s not me who’s trying to hurl every other memory on top of the one she doesn’t wanna admit to. You’re giving me a damn migraine over here—”

    “Then mind your own business?” Kara shrugged.

    Traveling for two solid weeks together in an effort to avoid the Sonoran had taken enough convincing, to be sure. Kara had explained that she simply wasn’t so eager to enter the desert again, not after her last unfortunate trek through it. The tall, red flash of a butte and its surrounding plateau burst to the forefront of Kara’s mind again, yet entirely against her will. It was like the more she tried not to think about him, the more he fought to make himself known. A bewildering scream of anger echoed between Kara’s ears at that point, and a face twisted up with vengeance appeared behind the lids of her eyes. Squeezing them shut hadn’t helped at all.

    “Jimmy—”

    “Don’t call me that!”

    Sara reared her head back, almost as if she’d been punched. “Ow- What… who…?” Seemingly out of patience, she took several steps towards Kara and went to swipe at her hand in order to see for herself. Kara dodged her completely, but was now forced to at least admit that there was something she was hiding. Sara drew her pale blue eyes into daggers at once, “What’s going on with you?”

    “It’s not something I wanna talk about!” Kara protested.

    “If it’s that loud inside of your head, Kara, then you need to!”

    “No!”

    Sara swiped at Kara again, earning her a word of caution from her wife standing nearby and at the ready. “Sara, let it go sweetie.”

    “Not until she tells me who—”

    Kara dodged again, “Quit it! I’m warning you!”

    “Oh, yeah?”

    “Yeah!”

    It was Lena’s voice that time to thrust a wedge between them, “Oh…” She glowered, “That’s it.”

    Before Kara realized it, Lena had snatched her up by the wrist and went to simultaneously grab at Sara’s shoulder with her other hand. She had forced herself in between them like a conduit, jolting stiffly at the string of chaotic memories being channeled through her. Kara had no choice in the matter, pull like a vacuum out of her, they did… Screaming, aching.

    “…we’re not supposed to be like this!”

    “Like what?”

    “Wrong!”

    Kara grit her teeth hard at the way James’ voice raked through her mind, clawing over it as if to tear it clean from one incorporeal end to the other. It was the way he towered over Lena, one move from killing her in an instant.

    “…Human.”

    “James!”

    The ring of Lena’s sabre blade lashed out next, making Kara wince.

     “James, don’t!”

    His oversized hand was around Kara’s forearm again before she knew it. The pain… Oh, it was just as horrible as she remembered…

    “Agh—!” The -snap- from the bones in Kara’s arm dropped her to her knees, knocking Lena off balance and breaking the connection altogether.

    As soon as Kara’s eyes were open, she fumbled for her injured arm, realizing seconds later that she was not, in fact, injured at all. It had only felt that way. Such was a regrettable (though sometimes useful) trait of Sara’s mental ability; to inflict the pain again without suffering the original damage.

    Sara stumbled backwards and then blinked her own eyes open, stunned. “Was that…” She jawed openly for a moment, “Was that a remnant? I thought… Wait, you said that all the men of your kind were dead. You- you lied to us?”

    “No, no I only thought he was—” Kara couldn’t help the way the pain had so quickly translated itself into anger. Her brow gathered tightly as she swiveled back towards Lena, “Whose side are you on?!”

    Lena was just as taken aback as Kara expected her to be. Seeing it, though, hurt worse than any bone break Kara had ever suffered. Human or remnant.

    “Yours, Kara.” Lena replied with a wounded tone.

    “I… wait. I didn’t mean- I’m sorry.” Kara wanted to kick herself. Lena wouldn’t so much as look at her now. She turned to walk out of the lone junk lot and towards the winding trail, and Kara felt her heart all but leap out after her, “Lena?”

    But instead of Kara’s voice being the thing to make her stop…

    …it was one new face after another, slowly descending upon her like a pack of hungry wolves.

    Half a dozen at first, then half a dozen more, all of them rising from behind broken highway pedestals and decayed building remains. There weren’t many ruins to hide amongst this far out from civilization, so Kara was shocked to see how many of them had snuck their way around to gather so closely together.

    Even worse, they had guns.

    Kara hated guns.

    “Woah—” Ava was the first to remark at their uninvited guests.

    -whip-

    -slock-

    Sara’s staffed stance was a thing of beauty. Her wife, of course, went to throw her arm in front, urging Sara to hold off. There was an accompanying -tink- from the pinky ring on Lena’s sword hand as it went to the handle of her sabre.

    “No—” Kara called out amidst the growing tension, “No, no… Let’s everyone just calm down, there’s no need for—”

    As soon as a figure to her left raised the muzzle of their gun, Kara wheeled her back to them and subconsciously threw herself around Lena, shielding her from gunfire.

    Much to everyone’s surprise, they didn’t so much as get the chance to squeeze the trigger.

    -pop-

    -thwip-

    -thock-

    A pained shout rang out into the mid-morning air, and Kara threw her eyes over her shoulder, afraid of what she might find…

    Nyssa was nocking another arrow from the sleek quiver at her back and drawing her bow arm for another shot.

    “No—!” Kara yelled suddenly. She didn’t wish for anyone to die. Rightfully so, that was usually where her mind went whenever Nyssa decided to make an appearance. “Nyssa- don’t!”

    Everything after that was a blur…. All except for the not-so-hidden nucleus of their group, who remained a monolithic presence amongst so many moving parts. It all happened so fast…

    “Ack—!"

    “Ungh!”

    -thunk-

    -thud-

    -fwump-

    All who were standing opposite their small group were now lying upon the ground, unconscious, seemingly ragdolled onto their limbs as if their strings had been cut altogether. Each of them had taken blows of some kind, but either by their own hand or from the person standing directly beside them.

    Slowly, Kara’s eyes made their way over to Ava, whose hair appeared windblown, and cheeks tinged with a rose color as if she had exerted herself to a great extent. After that, Nyssa began her irate shouting to be released. As per usual.

    “Agh- let go of me- witch!”

    Sara placed her hand at Ava’s cheek, and then, like the cool crash of a clear blue waterfall, the hardness in Ava’s eyes began to fade. Nyssa shook herself free of Ava’s influence and scowled angrily, almost shrugging herself off as if she’d been covered with a foul-smelling blanket.

    “Did I not tell you that I would kill you should you ever do that again—”

    Sara was quick to interject, like always. “What are you doing out here, Nyssa?”

    Nyssa washed the contempt clean from her face, yet almost too fast to be deemed “sane” by any means. “Why, saving you… beloved.”

    “He’s alive, if anyone cares to know.” Lena said from the nearby heap of downed raiders. Kara had spun around so quickly at the sound of her voice that she’d nearly fallen over. Lena wrenched the arrow tip out of the man’s chest and grimaced at calm flow of blood which came forth. Astonishingly enough, it was a shallow wound. How very unlike Nyssa to miss… Impossible, actually. Lena began to wave them over for aid, “Rags, a shirt, somebody—”

    “Oh, just leave him, dear. What’s one less vagabond in the world?”

    Sara dropped her head into her hands, and Kara would have done the very same— if not for the swift rush of wind and the untimely skidding of feet. Everyone stopped and looked towards the sudden vortex of swirling canyon dust, wherein its middle, stood none other than Barry.

    He blinked happily as he coughed and swiped the dirt and dust from his eyes, “Dang,” He said with a -snap- of his fingers, “Missed it.”

    Everyone shouted at him together, surprised and unsurprised all at the same time…

    “Barry!”

    Barry Allen, the Fastest Man on Earth, stood there with his mouth held slightly agape and with one hand raised in question while the other pointed at the very obvious bad guys lying face-down on the ground, “Wait, why are we yelling at me?!”

     

     

    Never was there a more disorderly hive of remarkably skilled individuals. They bumbled around each other like clumsy bees, helping and not helping where and when they could.

    Nyssa avoided gathering wood to stoke their fire in an effort to hover around Sara, who avoided Nyssa in turn like the deadly wasp that she was. At least all was forgiven now, or so Kara hoped. Having to explain James after doing her best to keep him secret was a rather long walk for a short drink of water. She hated admitting it; the fact that he hated her.

    All she ever did was love him like a brother.

    Her closest confidant… turned enemy.

    “James… James- why does that name sound so familiar?”

    “Wasn’t he one of the founders?”

    Barry was always one to remember the details, even if he wasn’t exactly supposed to know them in the first place. He was a skilled investigator, to say the least, never missing the drop of a name under any circumstance. Bit of a history buff, as well. He held a great interest in learning as much about the Infinite as possible.

    “But why does he hate J’onn so much? And you, for that matter?”

    Sara was enthralled, having moved well past her hurt feelings. Kara had no choice but to guard the rest of her life’s biggest secret…

    “You know I hate it when you say that.”

    Chapter Twenty-One: The Right Reasons

     

    Sunrise over the Napa Valley was beautiful.

    Lena had listened to Kara speak about it at length, about how the sky seemed to light up with a fire at the break of each day and how it spilled out over the flat valley floor like liquid gold. A pan of untapped wealth. The promise of tomorrow.

    Even though they had walked for days in their northerly direction, Lena felt that they were still so far off from their destination. Truthfully, she longed for the switchbacked mountainside where she and Kara had made their way south the first time. She could almost smell the sweet resins and trampled pollens of their wildflowered trail. She longed to go there again, where their footsteps met the loud crash of waterfalls and the feathery beats of songbird wings met the still canyon air… Lena’s sharp gasps as she leapt over narrow gaps… Kara’s low hums beside her ear…

    “Oh…!” Lena’s arms windmilled forward at the sight of Peck diving straight for the ground in front of her feet. Her idle daydreaming was starting to send the wrong impression to the rest of her party— that she was clumsy. She wasn’t. Little did they know that it was all she could do just to retain her sanity.

    With a -squawk- and a miss, the red-tailed raptor scuttled across the path in front of them in search of his prey. A field mouse just small enough to slip through his talons had made quite the display of running rings around him before zipping through the long rows of unkempt vines. Lena felt herself smile and allowed Peck the space to continue. How was it that a bird was so much easier to understand than people were? At least his intentions were laid bare with the laser focus of his eyes. There was another confused squawk and then several more sharp scratches against the sandy loam, eventually marking his defeat. Peck had been leaping at anything that moved lately; his constant state of high alert was continuously on the rise the father they pushed north. Lena wondered if he could feel the apprehension in his traveling companions, or perhaps just her own…

    “He seems restless, don’t you think?” She asked with a small gesture.

    Kara shrugged, “Nah, he’s just got a bad case of fomo.”

    “…I’m sorry?”

    “Fear of missing out.”

    Lena huffed a laugh through her nose and then shook her head. “I’m not sure that’s a thing.”

    “Believe me,” Kara reached out to take Lena by the hand as they walked, “…it’s totally a thing.”

    A wayward grapevine snagged at Lena’s borrowed shawl, pulling it neatly from the tops of her shoulders. Barry leaned down behind her and grabbed it up from the ground, offering it out to her with a humble smile.

    “Here.”

    “Thank you…”

They would be through the open floor of grapevines and into the hills on the other side soon. At least then they would be that much closer to all of the towering pines that awaited them— even if they were still more than a week away. Everything about their journey reminded Lena of her time spent with Kara; those first few heart-pounding, fledgling feelings that screamed of uncertainty. Getting to know Kara had made the trip seem much shorter the first time around. The months had felt more like weeks, whereas the weeks now felt more like months.

    Lena laughed as Peck hopped energetically to find his way around some of the vines, still longing after his breakfast, no doubt. The foliage was thick with leafy green, and the wood was heavy from all the mottled blue grape clusters. Lena’s hands were still sticky from all their greedy pillaging. The stark difference of the life she had before was on full display in a place like this; Lena had never seen so much fruit before. Admittedly, most of her meals had come hand-delivered from a cafeteria type of environment— where a lot of which was processed for longevity and rather bland whenever it came to taste.

    Ava popped her head up from the next row over, a flax-colored beacon over the verdant leaves, “Kara, I think we should double back for the hill.”

    “Mm-mm, this way’s faster.” Kara replied with a shake of her head, “Straight through.”

    The glossy sheen of black wings swooped low overhead, making Ava bob out of sight. An odd thing for any bird to do, Lena thought. Ava shook it off and continued, “We’re sitting ducks once we’re out of this field. You really want to risk getting caught out in the open just to save an hour?”

    “Oof!”

A tingling feeling beset her lips almost immediately where the tiny blood vessels just beneath screamed for oxygen, but once the spasm in her diaphragm subsided, she drew in a single ragged breath and coughed hard. The temporary loss of feeling in her lips and cheeks made it difficult at first to speak again…

    “Bitch.”

    “Do you plan on taking a nap, then?”

    “What? No—”

    “Then get up.”

    Dammit.

    I am so sick of this.

    Lena’s ears continued to ring over the sound of Kara’s displeased shouting. She had made clear her feelings on their sparring— but hadn’t gone so far as to step in. Not once. At least there was no doubt in Lena’s mind that Kara was always watching from afar, but that only added to her already wounded sense of pride. She knew it was a mistake to lead with her emotions, but still she somehow ended up inside the same bitter pitfall every time. It was safe to say, at this point, that Lena was outmatched. Vastly, so.

    As soon as she was able to, Lena rocked upward and onto her elbows just to feel the hard sole of Nyssa’s boot shoving her back down again. This time, the look upon Nyssa’s face was different. Gone was that smug, victorious smirk. She no longer seemed to be enjoying herself. Perfect, Lena thought… I’m apparently so bad at this that I’ve bored her now.  

    The unfazed assassin leaned over, both eyes dark with what Lena could only describe as disappointment, and then she frowned as if she pitied her. Lena knew that she did. It was plain for all to see. How could she ever compare?

    But, why would she ever want to?

    “I will admit… It is almost cruel.”

    Lena winced as the sharp edge of a small rock rolled beneath the bony point of her elbow. Both of her hands went to the hard sole of Nyssa’s boot so that she could speak again, “That’s rich. I thought you liked being cruel.”

    “Do not pretend to know me.”

    “Trust me, I know more about you then I care to, but I don’t need to know you in order to beat you.”

    “Yes, you do.”

    Lena stopped struggling, “Yeah? And why is that?”

    "If you know yourself but not your enemy, then for every victory you gain you will also suffer a defeat."

    “Hah,” Lena hacked a laugh and then shoved Nyssa’s boot off from her chest entirely, “Said like a true- wait…” She panted a breath of surprise as she dusted the brittle detritus of leaves from the backs of her arms and sat up, “Did you just quote The Art of War?”

    Nyssa merely folded her arms and gave a silent shrug in response.

    “You’ve read Sun Tzu?”

    “Perhaps. And now, a question for you…” Nyssa gave a few long stretches of her neck as if she were sore from all of her effort, “Would you care to know your biggest failing?”

    “Excuse me?”

    Lena jumped slightly at the sound of logs clapping together. Ruby had abandoned her game of tag and was now stacking firewood nearby. She was laying them against a smattering of sheared boulders and pretending not to eavesdrop. Never had Lena met a more curious child. That is, of course, with the exception of herself. Ruby was sure to face a life of trial and error, but her unwavering dedication to the things in which she set her mind to would undoubtedly steer her in the right direction. All she would need is a helping hand every now and then. Lena grumbled for another moment to herself and then deigned to hear Nyssa out.

    “Alright, fine. Tell me.” Lena scraped the dirt from her knees and then stood up with a single, unsteady wobble. Being knocked onto her ass at least a dozen times before dinner had a strange way of unsettling her, body and spirit.

    “You expect your opponents to fight with the same stalwart standards as you do. I am sorry to be the bearer of bad news—”

    Lena scoffed, “No, you’re not.”

    “…but the ‘fairness’ you seek has all but left this world.”

    “You want me to fight dirty. Like you. Is that it?”

    Nyssa lunged forward and gripped Lena by the shoulders. A look of excitement had spread across her face, “Yes!”

    Their charged back-and-forth had now become oddly reminiscent of the discussions Lena had once had with her father. The subtle truth of it rang loud between her ears. Why did the world wish to shape her into something she simply wasn’t? Though she knew it wasn’t any fault of her own that she was gentle-hearted, the very fine line that everyone wished for her to cross was a step too far. Toying with her father’s tight-lipped guards was one thing… but to seek the faults of another and then to exploit them for the sole purpose of winning?

    No.

    That wasn’t her at all.

    “I…” Lena shook her head, “I wouldn’t even know how.”

    “That’s a lie.”

    “Okay, fine- then maybe I don’t want to!”

    Nyssa frowned again. “Then, you will die.”

    “Ugh!” Lena slapped her hands against her thighs, “You can’t tell me that the only way I’ll survive out here is to destroy each and every person who crosses me.”

    “That is… a rather harsh way of looking at it.”

    “You know what? I’m never going to figure you out.”

    Finally, Nyssa smiled. Full of mirth and tooth. “On this, we can agree.”

    Frustrated beyond belief, Lena dragged her tired feet over towards a fallen tree trunk. It was large enough to climb into should she wish to carve a hole and escape from the world. It was a feeling that she carried more and more often, now. Surely, the Grove of Titans would offer her some sort of solace, assuming she lived long enough to even make it there a second time. Lena wiggled both of her hips onto a flat surface suitable for sitting and then palmed over the ancient bark with a sad kind of admiration. “I’m not built for this…” She hung her head, “I- I can’t do this.”

    It was times like this when she remembered the benefits of her daydreaming. Every other thought in her head was of her father. The hard lines of disappointment beset in his brow, and of her fencing instructor, and his eagerness to prove each and every one of her moves wrong. She saw Leslie’s face every now and then as well; The hard clench of rage that sat at the back of her jaw, and the callous disregard for life she held just so that she might win a single peacemaker.

    Lena sighed. How transparent was she, really? It was as if Leslie had sniffed her out and used her duelist’s code of honor against her. Was that what her moral compass was? A failing?

    “But you should.” Nyssa softened her stance and went to lean against the trunk beside Lena, “And I will tell you why.”

    “Oh, please do.” Lena replied with a roll of her eyes, not daring so much as to lift her head.

    “Unlike most people, you fight for the right reasons, but you cannot continue to expect your opponents to show you the same kinds of latitudes that you would give them. If you’re worried that would only make you like them in the end, then allow me to put your mind at ease.”

    Lena reared her head back with a simultaneous raise of an eyebrow. “What’s happening right now? Are you seriously being nice to me?”

    “Only because I grow tired of seeing you lose so spectacularly.”

    “Annnd there it is.”

    Nyssa turned to face Lena and furrowed her brow with that branded look of seriousness she so often kept. “Out here, most of your battles will be with people who only fight for themselves. This is what sets you apart. While I find your bleeding heart to be doubly nauseating, it is, in fact, the only one I would ever trust to rebuild the world. This place needs people like you, don’t you see? People sleep peaceably in their beds at night only because rough men stand ready to do violence on their behalf.”

    Lena laughed. “Okay, I’ll admit I can see you reading Sun Tzu, but Orwell? Who are you, and what have you done with the annoying broody assassin? Weren’t you literally saying the complete opposite before we left the Infinite? What changed your mind?”

    “You did.” Nyssa gave a flippant shrug.

    “I don’t know if I believe that…” Lena sighed, “But anyway, I suppose you consider me one of those ‘rough men’, then?”

    “I do.” Nyssa’s eyes fell to the ground where she toed around the springy leaves of a fern. “Along with my beloved… and Kara. And even though you might not have known Alex, she was just the same- albeit a bit more difficult to read.”

    “Coming from you, that sounds like a compliment.”

    “It was.”

    Lena nodded and then left her head to hang again. She fingered lightly around her ear where she tucked some of her long hair behind and then resolved just to sit idly in their shared silence. Surprisingly enough, it was a comfortable one. It was the first time that they weren’t at each other’s throats for more than three odd minutes at a time. Unfortunately, the feeling soon faded into awkwardness, and much like it always did, Lena’s mouth set upon breaking the silence with the first thing that came to mind—

    “Speaking of reading, where would someone like you even get your hands on a book?”

    “I steal them from Kara whenever the mood strikes me.”

    “I see. Small word of advice?”

    “Hm?”

    “Best not let Kara find that out. Ever.”

    Nyssa blinked for a moment and then yanked her head upwards as if she had heard something close by. There was a flash of her hand as it went to her hip and then again as it flung outwards, a streak of silver cutting through thin air. The abrupt -thock- sound her dagger made as it struck the skinny trunk of a standing tree nearly made Lena leap, if not for the surprised -squeak- that came directly after. Time seemed to stand still for a second or two as Ruby leaned out from behind the tree, pale faced and wide eyed. She cleared her throat nervously…

    “Y-you guys have books?”

    With that, all of the tension went out of the air. Lena relaxed again and felt herself truly laugh for the first time in days. Despite the ever-looming reminder of what still lay ahead of them, it felt good just to be in the moment. Nyssa had surprised her— which wouldn’t have been so unusual, had it not been for the fact that it was a welcome one.

    If someone so dour and morose could be so uncharacteristically optimistic —even for the most fleeting of moments— then maybe everything she had said was true after all. Maybe the world was still worth fighting for, just as long as there were those still willing to bleed for it.

    Not for themselves.

    But for others.

    Chapter End Notes

    Thanks for reading <3

    Chapter 22: At Your Mercy

    Chapter Notes

    TW: graphic violence

    Chapter Twenty-Two: At Your Mercy

     

    Compared to the Infinite, the forest of the Pacific Northwest was the loudest place on earth.

    Lena missed her quiet creature comforts. Her cup of ginger tea, a warm blanket, one of Kara’s many books… Nary but the scrape and scratch of armadillo claws…

    Here beneath the great pine canopy of western hemlocks, firs, and cedars, the world was awash with noise. The towering redwoods had begun to grow few in number as the days went by, and the gentle erosion of hillsides swept up and then down, plunging them deeper into sapling thickets and densely grouped evergreens. Where there was so much shelter, there was also life. Lena could easily recall the forest floor of old-world Oregon being so uproarious during her first voyage away from the compound, but had it somehow gotten louder in her absence?

    “But I can help!”

    With a huff, Lena scooted closer to adjust the way her cheek laid upon Kara’s shoulder. She had been awake for hours.

    “You’ve already come so far, Ruby, we just want to take you someplace safe—”

    “No! You just want me out of the way!”

    There was a small pinch of pain to the middle of her palm. It was enough to shake her mind free of the day before. Unbeknownst to her, her fist had been clenched so tightly atop Kara’s stomach that her fingernails had begun to dig in. The fact that she had to place so much awareness into something as simple as unclenching her fist over and over again was shocking— but still there was the hurt sound of Ruby’s voice again, pacing back and forth inside of her mind like a caged animal.

    “Please, I- I don’t wanna go.”

    She had tried both anger and bargaining, but her begging seemed to plague Lena the most.

    “I wanna stay with you…!”

    Lena held her eyes shut as tightly as she could and forced the regret to subside to something of a dull roar. It worked for a minute or two, and then her mind would reel again with the distress of it all. She eventually sat up and pinched at the bridge of her nose, defeated. Lena worried that she might not sleep again until after everything was said and done. Whenever that might be. Then again, they might just all die and solve the problem of sleep indefinitely.

    The sound of Kara’s mild snoring caused a smile to spread across Lena’s face. Always the light in her darkness, she was. Guiding her back home safely. Lena brushed some of Kara’s tousled golden locks and then bent down to leave a kiss at her temple, to which Kara returned the gesture with an abrupt -snort- as she turned over. Lena smiled again. She supposed that two hundred years of practice towards sleeping with the element of danger on the horizon was why Kara always rested so soundly… She claimed to love sleeping, whenever she wasn’t being tormented by her nightmarish dreams, that is. It simply passed the time. Lena was envious of that fact, in particular. She would toss and turn and cling to Kara the whole night through, right up until the sun decided to grace the earth with its warm presence again. Lena would be alone with her thoughts, but also quite literally at the mercy of them.

    And that of mercy, they seemed to have none.

    Something scurrying just a few trees over snapped Lena’s head around and then back towards the rest of their camp. The glowing embers of their fire had been stoked with kindling and was starting to smoke again. There was movement nearby. Lena squinted into the darkness and saw Nyssa draping her cloak around Sara’s shoulders, who stared endlessly into the dancing flames as if she were stuck there, thinking. Lena didn’t have to guess. It was obvious. Sara was skilled at discerning the pain of others… but quite lousy at hiding her own.

    Deciding just to alight herself onto her feet again, Lena crossed the small camp and brushed the ground clean of pine needles where she might sit across from the two former soulmates. As per usual, Nyssa’s softer side was on full display whenever she was around Sara— but much more so whenever they were alone. Lena saw glimpses of it here and there. Unintentionally, of course. But she was always amazed at their uniformity. The effortless way in which they moved around one another, finishing one thought and forming another without ever having to share a single look. It was like the two of them could speak a language with one another that only they understood, but by never having to say a word at all.

    “Can’t sleep either?” Lena asked quietly as she reached for a stick to poke at the fire.

    Sara smirked tiredly. “Who can sleep when there’s so much damn noise?”

    “Kara can, apparently…” Lena twisted around slightly and then peered into the thick of the forest, “It’s not actually so bad once you get used to it, I think. The owls, on the other hand—” Then she stopped as soon as she found Sara’s glowering eyes pointed directly at her, “…Oh.”

    “Yeah.” Sara agreed.

    It was hard to remember the extent of Sara’s abilities at times. The more recent and painful the memory, the louder and more unavoidable it was.  

    “Sorry.” Lena flashed her teeth with a sheepish apology, “I… can’t exactly help it.”

    “That’s the problem. Neither can I.”

    Nyssa came back with the braided lanyard that was once fixed upon the pommel of her scimitar. She placed one end between her knees and began to unravel each red strand with a kind of reverent patience that she reserved only for her weapons. She poured Sara’s tea with the same kind of patience. To Lena, there was no discernable difference. The respect was inherent. Nyssa frowned, “Might I suggest stuffing down some of those painful memories and locking them away…” She gestured with the flat of her hand, moving it downward, “…deep inside?”

    “Why?” Lena raised an eyebrow, “Is that what you would do?”

    “Yes.”

    “Seriously? You’re probably the world’s most accomplished killer, no offense—”

    “None taken.”

    “…and you mean to tell me that you don’t have any terrible memories?”

    “If I did,” Nyssa responded without taking her eyes from the braid in her lap, “I would keep them to myself.”

    “It’s not that easy!”

    “If you say so.”

    Lena tossed her hands into the air with a huff and then squared her hips in Sara’s direction again. “You don’t get anything from this one?” She asked, pointing at Nyssa. “Nothing at all?”

    Sara shook her head. “Nope.”

    Lena sat there, bewildered, but it didn’t take long for that faraway stare in Sara’s eyes to return. Lena felt guilty again. For all of it. But especially for Ava. “Sara, I’m… sorry. I didn’t mean for—”

    “It’s not your fault.” Sara lifted her sad gaze, “Someone had to go back with her.”

    “Why could it not be you, then?” Nyssa grumbled to herself, still hard focused upon her task.

    Seemingly done with the conversation, Sara reached over and pulled Nyssa’s dagger from its sheath. With one swift movement, she cut a lock of her vibrant blonde hair and then tossed both it and the dagger down at Nyssa’s feet. “Because I’m the only one who can fly us all out of here.”

    Without another word, Sara shoved Nyssa’s cloak from the tops of her shoulders and then stomped furiously into the wooded area just beyond their small campsite clearing. Nyssa seemed mostly unfazed by it, or maybe it was that she had plenty of practice with coming in second place and no longer let it affect her. Lena knew that it must have at some point. A love like that doesn’t just sit on a shelf and watch as the world goes by. Nyssa would follow Sara to the very end of it all, and then all the way back again. She leaned down and pinched the lock of Sara’s hair between her fingers, combing it free of its wild and wavy nature and then went to place it betwixt the strands of her scimitar braid.

    “What’s that you’re doing?” Lena asked, confused.

    “It is for luck.”

    “Mm. Hard to believe you need any of that.”

    “Something else we agree on.” Nyssa explained as she began to wind the gold color of Sara’s hair into the blood red of the braid in her lap, “But it is not for me, so much as it is for Sara.”

    “I don’t understand…”

    “It is something sacred between us. She does not wish me to die.”

    Lena nodded, “Because she loves you still?”

    Instead of answering, Nyssa sat up straight and then paused for a second, alerted to something nearby. Her eyes narrowed, as if she were listening to the world breathe. The dagger at her feet slipped into her hand and vanished a heartbeat later, sailing across the clearing— only to be caught by someone else an instant later, its tip poised a single inch from their nose.

    “What has it been? Two weeks?” Nyssa asked in a slightly begrudged tone before going back to her scimitar braid.

    Much to Lena’s surprise, J’onn stepped out from the shadows and thumbed over the smooth Damascus steel of Nyssa’s dagger. “Four.”

    “Damn.”

    “What…” Lena buffeted a breath loosely between her lips, “Where did you- Wait, what’s going on?”

    Nyssa answered with a harrumph, “I had noticed we were being followed for a while. I just didn’t know by whom.”

    J’onn folded his arms proudly across his chest. “Actually, Kara’s hawk was the first to discover me.”

    Nyssa gave an extra firm yank to the knot of her braid and then sent the most incredulous look back at J’onn, “Impossible.” When J’onn remained silent in the way of an answer, Nyssa stood up and waved for dagger back. He lobbed it handle first over the fire and then watched as Nyssa took it, along with herself, in the same direction that Sara had left in, muttering disdainfully on her way out, “Stupid pigeon.”

    Lena couldn’t believe how stealthily J’onn had been trailing after them, and for so long, as well. He came over to sit beside Lena at the fire and pulled at the sleeves of his dark green jacket, quieting himself to the small crackles and pops of the embers in front of them. She was still so shocked that he was there to begin with…

    “Why are you here, J’onn?” Lena asked with rapid blinks of her eyes, “I mean, why didn’t you just come with us in the first place?”

    “I’m afraid it’s a bit more complicated than that.”

    “How?”

    “I had to be sure of something, first. There is a lot to explain, but for the longest time, my presence on this planet had to remain a secret. Nothing was more important.”

    “Let me guess… Because of people like my father?”

    J’onn pursed his lips. Something in that question seemed to unsettle him greatly. He turned to sit face to face with Lena and then folded his hands into his lap. “How much do you truly know about him?”

    “About my father?”

    “Mm.”

    “I…” Lena buffeted another loose breath, “He raised me on his own after my mother died. He’s a scientist, a geneticist- like me. Ever since I can remember, we’ve been traveling the world in search of keys to unlocking mankind’s greatest potential—”

    “His words?”

    “Yes, though, my efforts were a bit more shortsighted than that. Also his words. Why?”

    J’onn pulled at his chin in a distressed manner. Something obviously weighed heavily on his mind. “I could not risk Sara learning the truth of my thoughts. Not yet. It is why I spent so much time just out of reach. I have been watching, and waiting, and hoping beyond hope that I would turn out to be wrong…”

    “Just,” Lena shifted nervously and reached out for J’onn’s hand, begging him, “Tell me what’s wrong. Please.”

    “He is not who you think he is.”

     

     

    Much to Lena’s chagrin, it was all just as she had left it.

    Fortified spires with their radiant solar lanterns aglow. Stone and steel rampart walls. Lena supposed that the inside of the compound was still as it was when she had escaped with Kara, as well. She couldn’t believe they were going back, and yet, she could… Only because she knew that she had to. There were lives depending on her. On all of them.

    -fwip-

    -pop-

    Nyssa quickly ducked down behind the stationary hover-canvasser and then heaved a long-held breath. She shook her head, indicating nothing short of bad news. Kara ambled closer on her knees and took a peek around the end of the vehicle’s steel cage to look once more towards the large southern gate.

    “So?”

    “So…” Nyssa adjusted the taut string of her bow across her chest, “This is suicide. Clearly.”

    Kara rolled her eyes with a huff, “How many?”

    “Too many.” Nyssa’s dagger-like stare went to Lena in an instant, “I thought you said the night shift was a skeleton crew?”

    Lena steadied herself with a hand at Kara’s knee, stammering for a moment. Kara then nodded to her with a smile, and Lena finally took a stab at an answer. “They’re supposed to be. I mean, unless they’re expecting a transport, which—”

    To their left, Sara clutched tightly at Nyssa’s elbow all of a sudden as if she’d been stricken with a chill up her spine. “Incoming…” Everyone shifted their attention to Sara’s wild-eyed line of sight and watched as her breath caught in her throat the longer she listened, horrified. “Children- they’re scared. Oh my G—” Sara ducked suddenly, motioning for the rest of the party to do the same, “Shh- get down!”

    The sounds of sniffled crying approached along with the steady hum of a moving hover-canvasser. If the rest of them had any question as to what the cages were for on the rear of the large transport vehicles, then they surely had their answer now. No less than a dozen seekers walked alongside, trampling over the blowing brush as their cargo moved ahead and towards the gate.

    “If only Ava were here…” Sara whispered sadly.

    Lena’s heart plummeted. Surely it would take one of Kara’s much-talked-about miracles for her traveling companions to not regard her with a burning sense of hatred after all of this. They had gone their entire lives believing that humans were of a specific sort of evil, and that they should fear them above all else. There was so much proof of it here.

    Evil in abundance.

    Once the transport was far enough ahead, Kara leaned upward again to peer through the darkness. She tucked her chin with an affirmative gesture and then whispered back towards the group, “Alright, new plan. We wait for the next—”

    -fwip-

    “…or, not. Dammit Nyssa!”

    Lena waited breathlessly for the sound of gunfire, but none came. No sharp -thwip- of arrows, no bloodcurdling screams…

    -pop-

    -thud-

    The black helmet and flak jacket of a seeker hit the pine-needled ground in front of them, gurgling the last threads of his consciousness with a hand held out for aid, and then he slumped onto his face. Nyssa jostled the man’s shoulder with the toe of her boot for a moment, inspecting him from head to toe. “Speedster—” She tossed her chin towards Barry who was still crouched at the rear of their group, “These are about your size, no?”

    “I- buhh,” Barry fumbled forward slightly and then shrugged, “Sure. I guess. Why?”

    Nyssa merely gave an impatient -tsk- from the backs of her teeth in reply.

    “Oh! Um. Okay- yeah!”

    “Think you can slip in with them and find us another way in?” Kara asked.

    “On it!”

    A whirlwind of leaves and pinecones swirled upwards from the ground all of a sudden, leaving a damp cloud of dust and one nearly naked seeker in its wake. Barry took off for the convoy in the same breath, knocking Lena sideways and onto her hips as the air pushed around her. Kara hoisted her back up and onto her knees, and then took an extra moment to gaze upon Lena’s face, almost as if she were committing her to memory. The moment didn’t bode well, in Lena’s eyes. If Kara had doubts at all about their mission, then they were presumably in more trouble than she had considered.

    The seeker began to stir slightly with another pained gurgle out from his mouth, which didn’t take long for Nyssa to stomp at the base of his neck, rendering him unconscious once again. Kara’s orders to the rest of the group were “not to kill unless absolutely necessary”. Which, in other words, meant “absolutely no killing at all”. That was how Lena understood it, anyway. Nyssa abhorred the idea at first, but complied whenever Sara threatened to not speak to her for the next year or more. In hindsight, having Ava along for the endgame of their mission would have drastically cut down their chances of defeat, but she was far to the southeast of them by now with Ruby. The Infinite would keep her safe. Lena simply couldn’t stomach the thought of Ruby suffering the same fate as the rest of the captured metahumans… She would rather Ruby hate her for the rest of her life, just so long as she had a life left to live and to hate whomever she liked. Lena would have to be satisfied with that. It was all that mattered.

    They waited quietly in the brush, listening to the low, rolling booms of approaching thunder. The air was damp, thick with the foreboding storm that was headed their way. Whether it was her sense of fear, or the static hanging amongst them that stood Lena’s hair on end, she couldn’t tell. Maybe it was both. She silently began racking her brain for a better way. A different way. Some other way that they could get in and out of the compound safely... but with so many scared metahumans in tow, Lena knew that casualties were somewhat of an inevitability.

    Kara sighed and turned to face her one last time, smiling yet again. “You okay?”

    “Mhm.” Lena nodded as she rolled her lips into her mouth. She dared not to speak the truth of what she felt, which was terror. Agonizing, unrelenting terror.

    “Last chance to stay behind—”

    Lena shook her head to stop Kara in her tracks, “I won’t.”

    “Lena—”

    “And- you,” She added a defiant shrug, with just a dash of self-deprecating whimsy upon her breath, “…you can’t make me, so, there.”

    Kara’s smile only grew wider. She nodded and then took one of Lena’s hands to squeeze lightly into her own. “I know.”

    “Besides, I’m the only one who knows their way around the place. But… Kara?”

    “Yeah?”

    “What if she doesn’t…” Lena cleared her throat nervously, “What if she won’t come with us?”

    “She will.”

    “How do you know?”

    “Because I’ll drag her back kicking and screaming if she doesn’t. I’m not giving her a choice.”

    Lena sat back on her haunches again and pondered the thought. None of it felt right. She wasn’t sure exactly what bothered her more… Her father, the madman. Kara’s sister, the turncoat. Ruby’s mother, the victim. The odds against them were insurmountable. Lena scoffed inwardly at the realization that Nyssa was bound to be right yet again.

    It was suicide.

    Plain and simple.

     

     

    One “impossible” task down.

    Lena fiddled with the plasma bank of her rifle, wishing she could just put it down— or better yet, bury it somewhere no one could ever use it again.

    Barry had made fast work of neutralizing the entire guard post at the compound’s south gate, but it was, unfortunately, at the expense of allowing the transport of meta children to enter the main concourse for processing. Not only was he smart, but he was also apparently no stranger to making hard kinds of decisions. It angered him having to do so, that much was made clear. Whenever the slender speedster was upset, he tended to voice his frustrations by way of shouting at the universe, head craned towards the sky and eyes rimmed red, but the fact that he was unable to do so here inside enemy lines only caused him to turn that anger inwards. He boiled underneath, and said nothing. To Lena, that was somehow scarier.

    The reinforced vest that hung on her shoulders was heavy. Carrying on with the disguise of their stolen seeker garb, she strained her neck back and forth as they paced in their tight formation from one checkpoint to another. Nyssa had nudged her hard with her elbow once to keep her from fidgeting, as if it would give them away if she continued. Lena apologized beneath their breath and kept her eyes low to the ground as they passed another pair of sentries in the middle of a heated debate. Their uniforms only made it easy to move about the compound undetected if they were to play the part, which was confident, and bold, much like humans were. Humans who lived their entire lives under the misguided belief that they were safe from all harm, that is. There was an arrogance to it, Lena realized. Little did they know that they were outnumbered three to one across the globe, but still had the gall to landgrab, and murder, and kidnap, and…

    …Experiment.

    Lena swallowed past the thick lump in her throat once the main tower came into view. The ground beneath her ill-fitting boots held a steady incline all the way there, and her thighs were burning from all of their effort, but they were close now. She gave a low “psst” towards the group and nudged the top of her helmet towards a supply shed, rocking it loosely over the tops of her ears. They gathered beneath a spread of cargo netting, keeping each of their heads on a swivel for signs of any movement around them. Thankfully, the upper tier of the compound was kept to a skeleton crew, just as she had said, though, she hadn’t any idea why the seekers had increased their captured transports to run well into the night. It nauseated her to know that it was all at her father’s behest; The man she hardly even knew.

    “Okay,” Lena tucked further out of sight, ushering the group to come closer, “If we’re still agreed to try and collect Alex first, then we’ll need to follow this path straight into the tank yard. The forge is on the other side.”

    “And everyone else…?” Sara asked.

    Lena sighed. “Across the compound, far on the west side behind several rows of chained fencing.”

    “So, we have to choose?”

    “We won’t have another chance. I don’t see any other—”

    Kara shook her head, “We’re getting everyone out- including Alex.”

    Nyssa chimed in once again, ever the voice of pessimism, “Yes, but should she raise the alarm here,” She turned and struck her arm out to the west, “How do you expect us to get all the way over there? We have to cross a veritable sea of enemies before we can even—”

    “I’m aware! Nyssa!” Kara seethed through her teeth.

    “You are allowing your desire to see your sister again to cloud your judgement—”

    “Then stay here!” Kara worked to hush the volume of her voice, bordering belligerence, “If you can’t contribute anything positive to—”

    Sara spread her arms between the two of them then, pushing a small amount of space between each. Nyssa was the only one who moved. Shoving Kara was futile; she was rooted in her purpose like the steep side of a mountain. “Stop!” Sara huffed, batting some of her fallen hair away from her eyes. She took the bulky helmet from her head altogether and then tucked it beneath her arm, “Nyssa’s right.”

    “I can’t believe you’re taking her side…”

    “I’m not taking—”

    All of a sudden, Kara’s head spun around at the sound of footsteps rounding the other end of the supply shed. Her helmet came off as well. Whoever it was came close, close enough to walk right on top of them had there not been a solid wall of concrete separating them, and then they began to move farther away. The timed metallic -clank- in their gait gave Lena pause. Kara followed the cargo netting towards the opposite end of the shed, fighting all the while to peek through it in an effort to follow the person. Then, she stepped outside and into the open, hands falling to her sides and rifle clattering against the ground with an awkward -clack-.

    “…Alex?”

    Lena waited, her breath held in anticipation. She could see through a small, ripped square of the cargo net that the person on the other side was alone. They wore an augmented brace around one of their legs.

    “Alex,” Kara’s voice cracked, “It’s me- Kara…”

    Lena squinted hard to see the other person’s face. They wore a look of confusion, or perhaps they were just stunned. There was, however, nothing upon their expression that denoted familiarity. The person standing there did not seem to know Kara from —as Kara would say— Eve.

    Too much time seemed to pass in the space of mere seconds. Nobody said anything.

    The dull glow of lantern light poured across the fortified plateau which they stood upon. Everything seemed to hang in the balance. Lena could see almost to the far rampart wall and its heavily armed guard towers from so high above…

    Sara and Nyssa were the first to step out from the shadow of the supply shed, leaving Barry to grab at Lena’s arm, forcing her to stay hidden. At times, he could be just as cautious as he was smart.

    Sara’s jaw traveled up and down for a few seconds in search of words. “Oh, my God… Alex.” Time and time again, she landed on the same weighty expression that Kara so often did. Only in moments of great emotional upheaval were those words used. Lena shivered.

    “Alex,” She stepped closer, “What- what are you doing here? You’re… alive!”

    “Hey!” A voice came from behind them, “This area’s off limits! Return to your—”

    “…Shit.” Barry muttered beneath his breath, and then he was gone in a “flash”, just as he had boasted the day that Lena had met him.

    “Oomph!”

    “Ach—"

    “Aughh!”

    Three well-timed -thumps- of unconscious bodies hitting the hard ground one after the other made Lena jolt with surprise. She screeched and then clapped her hands over her mouth, frightened. Had there been time enough to think of what to do next, Lena would have opted just to grab Alex and run, but they wouldn’t so much as get the chance.

    The trio of sentries had actually been two pairs.

    The last of them was trailing shortly behind, puffing a plume of cigarette smoke into the air. Lena could feel her heart pounding all the way in her throat as they noticed the lump of bodies lying just up ahead. The lone sentry froze for a split-second, hesitant on whether to hip-fire their sidearm or to call for help. Lena saw the red glow of an emergency lockdown button on the wall to the right. They saw it too.

    Barry was fast, but was he fast enough?

    “Barry!” She dashed out and into the open. All bets were off. “Don’t let them hit that button!”

    The time it took to process a single thought was, unfortunately, too long. A fraction of a second was too much to ask for. The cigarette fell from the sentry’s mouth with how fast they darted for the side of the nearby building, where their hand slapped hard at the button. It blared an immediate siren with a simultaneous flare sent up into the night’s sky, and Lena’s shoulders fell with a defeated breath. Barry caught up to them only too little too late, and landed hard against the ground, hard enough to knock the sentry out cold. Back on his feet, he spun around and panicked, “Now what?!”

    Lena ran to Kara and pulled at her arm, knowing exactly what they had to do next.

    Run.

    “We have to go- Kara!”

    But… she wouldn’t move. Gone was the decisive “tank” Lena knew and love. There in her place was a lost little girl, floored by the sight of the sister whom she thought was dead.

    “Kara! The entire compound is about to converge on that flare,” She pointed high into the sky with her other hand, “We don’t have any more time! We have to go!”

    “I can’t leave her again…” Kara mumbled sullenly. She looked as if she hadn’t blinked once since setting her eyes upon her sister. Alex had begun to backpedal away from them all, and looked to be seconds away from turning and bolting into a flat out run. “I…” Kara shook her head, “I can’t leave her again.”

    “Kara, come on!” Lena pulled hard, this time with the help of Sara and Nyssa both pushing hard at her back. Still, she refused to budge.

    “Alex…”

    What had sounded like the crash of a sudden rainfall became more like the running bootsteps of armed guards, and many of them. Too many to count. Lena had to act quickly. If Kara wouldn’t, then she would have to choose. Lena grit her teeth with a panicky growl and then called out to Barry, nodding her head towards Alex, “Grab her! Let’s go!”

    The first wave of sentries that came upon them were welcomed by a row of Nyssa’s ephemeral portals. She grabbed one and then popped out of sight, only to return and grab another. Their screams eventually came from up above, which was where —much to Lena’s horror— they only grew louder. It was the harsh way each of them landed on the hard ground again, lopsidedly onto their arms or legs. There went the sickening -crack- with the breaking of their bones. Sara was still shoving Kara in the opposite direction with all of her might, but to no avail. She soon took to hitting her with closed fists, screaming at her to “move, dammit!”

    Barry, meanwhile, had chosen to approach Alex with his hands held out and at the ready, seemingly in the hopes that she might come with them willingly. Alex, on the other hand, looked as if she had no intention of coming willingly. For every step that Barry took, she took an equal step back. She was shaking her head as they spoke.

    Lena felt helpless. The plasma rifle hanging at her side seemed so much heavier now as she took it into her hands. She wished more than anything not to have to use it. They were overwhelmed before they even knew it.

    “Barry, now!” Lena shouted.

    But it was another voice which drowned out her own.

    Someone overlooking the barricade back towards the concourse had shone a spotlight directly on Alex, and there from a loudspeaker, came forth a single name, spoken as if it were any other occasion. It was chilling, how calm and certain the voice was…

    “Sentinel.”

    Lena stood there and watched as the confusion apparent on Alex’s face turned to disappointment, and then to something else, something entirely unexpected.

    Anger.

    Alex reached down to tap at the knee joint of her brace. A decision had been made. What surrounded her leg instantly whirred to life with several more parts expanding and folding and collapsing into an entire mechanized suit. It hung upon her every limb and moved as one with her. Lena gasped. Was she just another one of her father’s assets, after all? The last of its metal rolled down the length of Alex’s outstretched arm, a row of scaled plates which emanated into a fist. She chugged it up and down once, as if to load it, and then she burst forward on her toes, aiming herself directly at Barry.

    With her fist cocked back and at the ready, Alex struck Barry in the chest hard enough to send him flying over the edge of the barricaded plateau, and down off the side of it entirely. Gone, in yet another flash.

    Sara shrieked after him, devastated, “Barry—!”

    It was as if everyone had forgotten how to move.

    How to breathe.

    How to think.

    Surrounded, Sara dodge-rolled one fired electrostatic charge after another until there were too many to evade… She was hit on her hip and then at her neck, which forced her body to run stiff with a pained groan out from her mouth. Sara landed hard on the ground, seizing along her every inch. Lena watched as Kara’s head finally turned to witness what went on behind her. Her eyes met Nyssa’s.

    “Fear not.”

    The assassin blinked, and then threw herself through one of her portals, leaving naught but a tendrilled black mist where she once stood. Sara wailed after her, calling Nyssa’s name over and over as if her heart had been all but ripped from her chest.

    They were finished.

    Kara turned to face her sister again, “Alex,” She held her hands out as the sentries quickly gained on them, “I know you might hate me for leaving you behind—”

    “You’ve ruined… everything.”

    Lena was surprised to finally hear the sound of Alex’s voice. There was an angry husk to it. Lena knew that she meant every word. She moved to stand behind Kara and placed their backs together to watch as the sentries slowed upon their approach. Their firearms went to aim at them immediately. There were so many.

    Suddenly, Lena felt as if she had lost everything. There were to be no more sunrises. No more books. No more sounds of children playing on a warm afternoon. No more evenings she would place her head on a pillow and feel that she was safe… There would be no more tomorrow.

    This was it.

    Her life would never be her own again.

    “What are you talking about?” The gentle croak of Kara’s voice expressed the weight of her hurt. It was awful to hear. Lena flailed blindly behind herself to latch onto her, to which Kara grabbed her up by the hand. She was shaking. “It’s me, Alex.”

    “Stop calling me that!” Alex shouted, clocking her fist up and then down again as if she meant to dispatch Kara the same as she had Barry. “I don’t care where you know me from, just, stop… calling me that. My name is Smith.” She looked around at the sentries then, her brow instantly furrowed with desperation, “My name is Smith! I don’t know them!”

    Lena felt Kara’s weight suddenly leave her back. She spun around, begging Kara not to tread any closer, “Wait, don’t—”

    “I can help you get out of here.”

    “You’ve killed us all.” Alex, or Smith, spat back.

    “Please… You’re my sister.”

    Before Lena could scream, a hand covered her mouth and pulled her hard in the other direction. The scuffling of her feet against solid ground turned Kara back around, and her own mouth fell open to shout a moment later, but it was over before she knew it— Alex’s gauntlet connected with the back of Kara’s head, crossing her eyes the very instant she disconnected with reality. Lena wretched a heartbroken cry through the fingers muffling her mouth and fought to breathe in through her nose. The large crowd of sentries then seemed to calm themselves as they parted longways down their middle. Lena struggled to free herself from her captor’s grasp, but was kicked to her knees for her trouble.

    “Hold her steady, now.” A voice like her father’s said. Just as she feared, the man himself eventually stepped through the crowd, tapping his cane gleefully alongside with that reliably safe and happy demeanor he so loved to tout. “She will break every bone in your hand, given half the chance.” He hummed a short note then as he prodded Kara’s leg with the end of his cane, “Sister, was it?”

    Alex swiftly disengaged her mech suit and went to fold her hands out in front of herself, meaning to beg for his mercy, “No- no, she’s confused. W-we don’t know each other—”

    “Silence.”

    “Please, sir—”

    “I said be quiet!” Lena’s father made a mad lash towards Alex’s neck, choking her as hard as he could. “The sound of begging,” He pinched his face together, struggling to keep up his strength, “How I detest the sound of begging.”

    At last, his hold over Alex had run its course and he faltered for a moment onto his cane, seemingly drained of energy. Lena was shocked. She hadn’t seen her father in such poor health for a very long time. The unknown illness he battled with seemed to ebb and flow, to her knowledge. He was out of breath for having walked down a corridor one day, and then full of life the next. Alex collapsed onto her knees and coughed violently as she pulled air back into her lungs.

    “Please, don’t,” He mocked, smoothing the faint beginnings of raindrops from the lapels of his two-piece suit, “Why are you doing this? Don’t hurt me—” Then he spat, “It never changes. How boring you all are.”

    The tears in Lena’s eyes began to cloud her vision, so much so that she could hardly see past the gust of air that swept at the jackets of all the armed guards. In the blink of an eye, someone landed feet first in front of them and shook the ground they stood upon. Green was their skin. Their skull, elongated.

    J’onn…

    J’onn, no!

    “Lionel.”

    Startled one moment and then delighted the very next, Lena’s father clapped his hands together in a joyous fashion. “Martian!” He sighed, grinning from ear-to-ear, “Here you are, at long last… Two hundred years!” Lionel laughed victoriously.

    “Let them go.” J’onn said intently.

    “Oh, now, why ever would I do that?”

    “Because I know what you truly want.”

    “Which is…?”

    “Me.”

    Lena finally struggled to spread her jaw far enough to bite the hand holding her back. She thrust her head from their grip and shouted, “Daddy, please don’t—!”

    In a fit of rage, Lionel threw his cane in her direction, missing her by an inch and then stumbling forward without it, “You shut your mouth- you insolent, backstabbing bitch!” He screamed louder than she had ever heard before, “Just like your witch mother!”

    J’onn projected his voice for all to hear, “Let this be the end of it.”

    “No!” Lionel continued to shout, spitting his vengeance with every enraged syllable, “I- will- have, what I want! As is my right!” He waved his arms to the sentries behind him, “Take him!”

    On cue, every sentry within the vicinity fired a single electrostatic charge at J’onn’s body, landing every single shot with painfully precise shocks. J’onn’s eyes shot towards Lena, impressing upon her the importance of what was soon to happen. His voice was in her head like the -clang- of a bell—

    “Trust me.”

    “J’onn! J’onn—!”

    The man she regarded as Kara’s father was now at the mercy of her own. J’onn withstood the first several seconds of the electric shock before falling to one knee, and then he bowed all the way forward limply, unable to outlast so many volts at once. An attack like that on literally anyone else would have disintegrated them down to a cellular level, effectively turning them into a pile of ash. Lena shielded her eyes from the white-hot brightness of it all as the charges continued to rage.

    Lionel shouted one last time, “Take them all!”

    Chapter End Notes

    Thanks for reading <3

    Chapter 23: Dreams—are well—but Waking’s better

    Chapter Notes

    TW: blood, violence, depictions of death

    Chapter Twenty-Three: Dreams—are well—but Waking’s better

     

    Being cradled in her mother’s arms had never felt so good.

    “Lena, darling.”

    “No- not yet, mommy.”

    The sound of her laugh, gently forced through her nose.

    “It’s time to wake up.”

    “But I don’t want to leave…”

    “I know. Daddy’s going to take us somewhere new. Wouldn’t you like to see mountains again?”

    She could remember it all so vividly; the calm of her frozen shore where proud eagles used to soar. There was her seaside tundra set aside vast cliffs… How she longed to traipse through its icy expanse. Eerie whale calls announced the piercing white of daybreak like a ringing in her ears, a haunting reminder of what she had left behind…

    Much to her disappointment, the sound of muffled voices led her back towards the prison of her body. She stilled only long enough to feel that her hands were bound. Her breath caught in her throat. The hammer of her realization had swung hard.

    She couldn’t move.

    “There she is, at last.”

    Her father’s voice. The tranquil insanity of it was unmistakable.

    Lena peered blearily at her surroundings. It was a shadowless view of a tiled operating theater, and there she was, the subject of her father’s ruthless machinations on full display. She was strapped to a surgical bed held at an incline, just enough to interact with face-to-face but also enough to put pressure on her spine. Her father stood before her, his arms folded and grinning wide to see her so bewildered. It seemed to please him very much, her pain, her bewilderment. She had so many questions at once, but the first to fall out from her mouth was, in truth, the furthest from her mind…

    “Where—” Lena licked at the dryness of her lips, desperate to find the sound of her own voice again, “Where is your cane?”

    It was strange to see him without it. Even on his best days he kept it close at hand, always an extension of his body.

    “Would you believe me if I told you that I no longer needed it?” He paused and then shook his head, deciding for himself. His long brown hair tossed back and forth against his neck, carrying it with it a healthy sheen the likes of which Lena had never seen before. He looked as if he had been made anew. Somehow decades younger. “No, perhaps you wouldn’t.” Lionel sighed as he scratched at the coarse scruff of his chin, “But I have faith that you will, in time.”

    Lena began to panic. The horror of her situation was quickly setting in. She pulled at the tight restraints of her wrists and struggled to loosen herself from the bed— as if she had any real means of escape. She knew that she didn’t.

    “You’re a monster.” She seethed at him with a quiet rage.

    The hurt laced upon each syllable was like tearing flesh from bone. This was the man she had trusted her entire life. She was angry with him, of course, but also with herself for ignoring the very clear signs of his true nature. His unhinged temper and his varying stages of illness… His adamant refusal to allow her any real freedom of her own… It was his insisting upon her sword skills and various schooling, the constant drive to improve her intellect, the callous disregard for lives unlike his very own—

    “Monster, visionary…” Her father huffed, stepping a bit closer to shield her from the harsh lighting, “Always an ocean’s worth of overlap there. True genius is often regarded with such contempt. Dippel, Mengele… No matter. I forgive you.”

    “You forgive me?” She felt like screaming. “For what exactly?”

    Lionel made a show of placing his hands over his chest, “For breaking my heart.”

    What else was she to do with her anger, other than cry? The stinging of tears suddenly welling in her eyes forced her to blink them away. She dared not look away from him, scared that he might make a move to strike her as he had done before. Lena truly had no idea of what to expect next. Would he kill her? Torture her?
