The grating sound the bay door made as the bull fought and twisted to free himself was awful, but he eventually came to stand still on the other side again and then pulled his antler back through.

    A polite -knock- came from the door.

    Kara was picking herself back up from where she had slid and crashed into some metal shelving behind them, so Lena took her hands to the chain again and raised the door as far as its bent pieces would go.

    “Hello…?”

    “Lena?”

    Ruby’s voice.

    Lena began to pull hard at the chain again, but the door was beyond operational. She looked back at Smith and begged, “Help- please!”

    Smith was quick to acquiesce with a sure nod. She held her hand flat like a knife and then sliced from left to right, cutting the door effectively in half. Its bottom piece crashed loudly onto the concrete, and Ruby squatted with her hands on her knees just outside. A smile stretched from ear to ear as she stood again to pat the elk on his shoulder, shooing him along, and then she waved for them to come out.

    “That was close, huh?” Ruby grinned.

    “Yeah,” Kara huffed as she wrung some of the water out of her hair, “I woulda won.”

    “Sure you would’ve.”

    “For real!”

    “If you say so.”

    Lena laughed and then spun Ruby around by her shoulders to inspect her for injuries. “You’re alright?”

    “Yup. Ava made all the bad guys take a nap. It was awesome.”

    “…Ava?” Kara gasped just as soon as she saw the blonde in question. She ran nearly fast enough to knock her down, but they held onto each other tightly and laughed away the remainders of their anxiety.

    “Come on,” Ava said, “They’re just around the corner. Nyssa’s working on getting everyone outside- but she can only transport one at a time.” When Kara steeped a growl from deep inside of her chest at the sound of the assassin’s name, Ava was quick to dissuade her from lashing out, “Relax, she came back for us.”

    Nerves steeled, they set off down the flooded path and around the end of the next building to find the observation dormitories nearly emptied. Dozens of women and children milled about between the cargo trucks, and the tell-tale -pops- of Nyssa’s portals were dropping one individual off at a time. Lena immediately went in search. The image of the woman she was looking for was fuzzy, at best. Dark chestnut hair, length well past her shoulders…

    No.

    No.

    No—

    Dammit. Where is she?

    She’d been circling the lot so fast that she nearly stumbled inside one of Nyssa’s ephemeral conduits. It closed just as she had gotten a good look inside… Wait, were those actual stars?

    Someone in front of Lena broke her fall once the portal collapsed, and they balanced against each other so as not to tumble down. Brown eyes stared back at her, mouth held agape with a breath not yet taken… They recognized each other at once, but before Lena could so much as think to speak, the haunting sound of Ruby’s desperate crying drove them apart.

    “Mom…? Mom!”

    Mother and daughter collapsed arm in arm at last, and just like that, the mountain’s worth of blame that lied upon Lena’s shoulders was lifted. It was as if the air had become breathable again. Samantha tore her eyes from the top of her daughter’s head and blinked a fond “thank you” towards Lena with the last of the rain trickling down her rosy cheeks. Lena smiled and shifted her attention towards another of Nyssa’s portals.

    This one carried Sara.

    As soon as the pilot could steady herself on her own two feet again, she wrapped her arms around Nyssa’s neck and sobbed her heartfelt thanks there. Lena realized she had never seen Nyssa so at peace— it was like staring at a completely different person, even if it only lasted for a split-second. Ava ran to them and found Sara turning to greet her with a kiss. They were always anticipating each other’s moves, giving truth to the phrase “home is where the heart is”. Lena looked over at Kara and finally understood everything.

    But the solace was short-lived.

    Sara was suddenly bent over someone lying on the ground and was clutching at their arms, screaming. She was inconsolable. Furious, almost.

    Nyssa approached Kara with a long face and then bowed her head in sorrow, “I’m sorry. I could not wake him.”

    Lena watched in horror as Kara took a knee. It was Barry lying there on his back, battered and bruised almost beyond recognition. A long incision was stitched closed from the back of his head all the way to his ear, and Sara looked back up with bloodshot eyes. Burn marks tracked the entire surface of her neck. Lena realized that she must have been collared many times.

    “I can’t hear any of his memories, Kara.” She sobbed angrily, “Is he even in there?”

    Lena shouldered her way through the small crowd and took up Barry’s wrist to feel for a pulse. “He’s alive, but that’s all I can say for sure right now.”

    “I’ll kill them.” Sara seethed brokenly.

    “Sara…”

    “I’ll kill th—” She stopped all of a sudden. Her eyes went longways past the building as if she were listening intently to something in the air, and then she twisted back around to face Kara again. “J’onn’s waiting for us.”

    “Alright,” Kara clapped her hands together, “Let’s—”

    “Sentinel!”

    Everyone’s head quickly turned in unison…

    All except for Smith’s.

    Lena saw from the corner of her eye the way that Smith’s shoulders fell whenever the name was spoken. It came from a group of seekers slowly emerging from the mist. They seemed to have joined the effort in retaking the compound and had come upon them quickly with their weapons drawn, though, they appeared hesitant to stalk them any further.

    “Sentinel, stand clear!”

    Serendipity, at last. Lena realized that this detachment had to have been too far off in their mission to know of Smith’s betrayal. They still believed her to be on their side.

    Smith turned around with a steady limp and shook her head at them. “No.”

    “Ma’am, there’s been a breach—”

    “I said no.”

    “Sentinel—!”

    “Actually, I think I like ‘Alex’ better.” She said with a sigh.

    Without saying another word, she raised a hand raised towards the sky and then made a fist, seemingly grabbing at thin air but pulling hard as if there were an enormous weight to it. She screamed through the clench of her teeth with all of her effort, and before anyone could understand what was happening, the top floors of the nearby testing annex sheared sideways and then came crashing down beam by beam, cutting off the seekers' only path with a ground-shaking slam. Shattered glass and the ear-splitting screech of twisted metal were nearly enough to bring Lena to her knees. Not often did remnants reveal the absurd scope of their powers, but each and every time that they did, she was shaken to her core.

    Clouds of stone dust and other debris kicked up into the dwindling rain shower, and much to everyone’s surprise, Smith, or… Alex, turned back around and simply dusted her hands with a job well done.

    “So?” She asked, “Are we ready to go, or what?”

    Kara nodded as she scooped Barry into her arms. “Ready.”

    Lena could hardly believe they might make it out in one piece. At last, she had reached the threshold of forever as it stared back at her from the far side of the compound, but the enormity of that “forever” was daunting, to be sure. She felt the same as she did before, but somehow different as well. More invigorated as if renewed after a long and restful sleep. The longevity ahead, and the power that it promised, were almost too terrifying to speak out loud— but she had to find a way to tell Kara. It still seemed so impossible, especially since she didn’t fully understand it herself.

    Ava and Sara kept the group of metahumans knit closely together as they marched onward, their way ahead lit by strobes of muffled lightning in the distance. Several to Lena’s left stopped just beyond a large metal cage whose corner was dug deeply into the mud, and they seemed to satellite around something there on the ground. To Lena’s right, Kara slowed her pace to a near stop. The gutted expression upon her face was unmistakable. A piece of her weary soul was lying there in their midst.

    It was Samantha who looked back, holding a pair of limp reddish-brown wings to her chest. She wept quietly as she asked “who could have done such a thing”— until Lena could no longer stand it. She darted through the crowd and gasped, heartbroken.

    No…

    Peck?

    Misty eyed, Samantha bowed her head and pressed the bridge of her nose to Peck’s beak. In that moment, every metahuman around her seemed to grasp something Lena could not. Silently, they each took a step back, leaving her to face Samantha’s sheer will alone. With an abundance of patience, Ruby’s mother dropped a single tear where Peck’s hooked beak met the soft, sensory plumes of his face, and then she waited until the surrounding air began to fill with static.

    As if drawing on the state of the available nature around them, a pressure began to build. An unseen force, funneling life back into hollow bones…

    Peck’s coppery discs for eyes suddenly burst open, and he collected his wings together with several excited flaps. Snaps of electrified air pulled around each beat. It seemed warning enough for Samantha to place him back on his feet and to scuttle away at the very last second. A bolt of lightning then shot up from the ground and met Peck mid-air. The white-hot flash and the -crack- that made Lena clamp her hands over her ears were suddenly gone— as well as the raptor himself. An echoing war cry was all that was left in his wake.

    The others helped Samantha back to her feet as Kara drew near. “Is- is he…?” She asked with a tremble in her voice.

    Ruby bounced on her toes beside her, unable to contain her glee. “He’s like us, now.”

    “How?”

    “My powers aren’t something I can easily explain, I’m afraid.” Samantha replied, “But we do know that they’re hereditary.” She smiled, placing a hand at her daughter’s cheek.

    Kara’s breath gradually steadied, calmer now. “I… can’t thank you enough.”

    After a few more pats on her back, Lena fell into step beside Kara as they made the final leg of their exodus. She reached to the back of Kara’s neck, rubbing it absently as the joy of their long-awaited future together began to spill over.

    “Let’s go home.”

    Kara smiled as she shifted Barry’s weight in her arms, nodding in agreement. “I love you.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Twenty-six chapters. Six months. One hundred and twenty-thousand plus words. While so vastly different than my usual stories, I can't believe how far we've come here. This one will surely hold a special place in my heart, being the most epic of romances I've written, but I find myself longing for little pastures again, and the steady -clop- of horse hoof. What say we head back to the farm after this one? Please, type in the comments which of my SC stories you would like to see a sequel (or some manner of return to) and I will weigh which is the most feasible. I can begin plotting an outline soon. Up to you. Otherwise, I will ruminate on some new ideas.

    As always, thanks for reading. <3

    Chapter 27: Of Sunshine and Eternity

    Chapter Notes

    Welcome to the last action packed chapter of this story. The next update will be epilogue styled, as per usual. Be sure to catch the chapter end notes, and buckle up!

    TW: blood, violence, major character death, graphic depictions of death

    Chapter Twenty-Seven: Of Sunshine and Eternity

     

    “Stay together, now.”

    Ava’s calm and reassuring tone broke over the pitter patter of watery runoff, forcing Kara’s eyes back towards the long road ahead. The mountain at their backs would soon be that of a distant memory, or so she hoped. The thought of home was still so fresh upon her mind, but there was still the problem of “getting there” to solve. In an ideal world, their plan would have transpired without so much as a single flaw: Pacify what resistance they were met with, grab Alex and any other prisoners they could find, and then fly back out with whatever aircraft Sara could pilot. But of course, in an “ideal” world, none of this would have happened to begin with. Instead, Kara had led her closest friends and family to near-certain death, for whom she feared most of all that one might never recover. Kara’s eyes cast down towards to her bare feet again. Barry was almost weightless in her arms, but her head certainly felt heavy.

    “I’m so sorry…” She whispered.

    He was so skinny.

    Around the last bend appeared those damnable water tanks, brimming with rainwater— Kara’s would-be graveyard, had Lena not defied her father and saved her life for the first of many times.

    From that point on, Kara realized there was little use in shielding the children’s eyes. Tears streamed from every one of them, cutting horrified tracks through the dirt on their cheeks. They clung to their parents’ legs, and sometimes to one another, shrinking away whenever they passed another corner where bodies lay mangled and twisted into revolting shapes.

    Death always had such an ugly way about it, Kara thought. It cared nothing for appearances. If anything, it seemed to revel in its ugliness, displaying itself in the most revolting forms as a silent reminder that, sooner or later, it would claim them all.

    Several who walked in front came to a sudden stop at the sound of voices up ahead. Barked orders and sharp commands, Kara was suddenly on high alert again. A few of the children immediately ran to hide behind Sara and Ava, who tightened their formation with the other metahumans still carrying at least an ounce of bravery in their veins. Kara slipped across the road with Barry in tow, and in the blink of an eye, their caravan had split apart— at least half a dozen on one side, and the rest on the other. They were tucked into the broken entryways and shattered windows of the buildings which lined the lowest tier of the compound, trembling with bated breath and waiting for their next move. Kara gave a dove’s call towards the front of the group, where two courageous manes of gold cautiously leaned out from their hiding place in response.

    “Kara—” Sara’s voice was an anxious hiss upon the wind, “Kara!”

    “What is it?” Kara called back, cradling Barry carefully as she lay him down on a splintered door.

    “We’ll have to fight our way through!”

    “What—”

    “They have J’onn!”

    The precious blood that ran beneath Kara’s skin suddenly turned cold. “I thought you said he was waiting for us?!”

    Sara sputtered for a short moment, racking her brain for some sort of an explanation, “I- I don’t know why he would—”

    “You may as well come out, my dear.” Someone shouted from the far end of the road. It was a dark husk of a voice, the Angel of Death himself.

    Kara felt Lena go rigid beside her. A moment later, Lena’s hand clamped around her own. She was shaking like a leaf. Broken glass crunched under Kara’s feet as she turned and cupped Lena’s face. Those blue-green eyes flicked everywhere, desperate for escape, but Kara knew that there was only one way out. Forward.

    “Hey, hey—” She urged hurriedly, “Breathe for me? Just breathe… Okay?”

    “Mm- hm- okay.”

    “Now listen, I’ll be right beside you. I promise.”

    “No- I can’t face him,” Lena whispered, terrified, “I can’t do it, Kara.”

    “Yes, you can. I believe in you.” Kara folded Lena’s hands inside of her own and held them tightly, “I believe in us.”

    “W-what do we do?”

    Kara bowed forward to press a kiss to Lena’s forehead and sighed, “We stop him. Once and for all.”

    “And what if we can’t?”

    “Think about the people here, Lena. They’re scared, and they’re depending on us to get them out. The way I see it, there isn’t much of a choice. Do it for them, but most of all, do it for you.” Kara caught a sob as it lurched halfway out, “I want to see you free at last. Of him, of all of this…”

    Lena sniffled and sent a stray tear from her eye with a slow blink. She nodded in the affirmative, “Alright.”

    Nyssa came by to kneel at Barry’s side and —with what Kara could only assume was a sign of respect— saw her out of the building with a sure tuck of her chin. Perhaps it was a goodbye, Kara thought. The once disloyal, backstabbing opportunist had changed. What was that they used to say about tigers and stripes? Had they been wrong? There was something about Nyssa now that seemed so certain and so forthright, even inside the smallest of exchanges like that of a simple nod. If anything, it helped to alleviate the guilt weighing heavily against Kara’s chest as she stepped back out and into the light with Lena.

    The clouds had finally split apart, no longer dark and swelling with the threat of rain. A lone ray of sunshine crashed upon the earth where a number of armed sentries stood. J’onn was there, chained to what was left of a dismantled hover-excavator. Its long arm was free of any attachment. Why didn’t he simply phase through the boom that he was bound to? What had they done to him? Or, worse yet, was he there of his own volition? Kara knew that J’onn had given himself up too easily whenever they were first captured… Perhaps his many millennia had softened his once steadfast convictions or riddled his mind with poisonous thoughts of surrender. While Kara was busy contemplating upon their approach, Lena gasped.

    “Kara…” She stammered, “Is- is that—”

    Kara came to a halt there in the mud. She couldn’t believe her eyes.

    “…James?”

    At the end of the road stood the towering visage of her oldest friend. Earth’s last male remnant.

    Kara’s worst nightmare had come true.

    A squad of at least eight surrounded James where he sulked opposite J’onn. His gaze never left the man once fabled as their beloved all-father, his hatred laid plainly at the man’s feet as his fists were clenched in anticipation of retribution.

    Kara noticed the shock collar fastened around his neck and quickened her pace. Despite all the words that had passed between them —and all the damage already done— she still felt a fierce sense of protectiveness towards anyone who had once been dear to her, even if they now loathed her beyond all rhyme or reason.

    James could hate her until the end of time, for all she cared. She wouldn’t let anyone else hurt the people she loved. Never again.

    “James—” Kara hastened herself towards the middle of the road, “James!”

    “That’s far enough, remnant.” Lionel seethed, pistol in hand. His aim was true at the back of James’ head, and he seemed to have no qualms about firing if the need arose. In that split second, Kara wondered whether he actually would— after all, James was the only living being capable of liberating her blood from her body. Just another asset, she assumed. Bargaining might well be their only way out.

    Lena’s voice cracked with sorrow as she called out to her father, “Please- just, let us go.”

    “Did you not think that I would expect yet another betrayal from you, daughter?”

    “Listen to me,” Lena tried deepening her voice, “You don’t have to do this.”

    Kara watched Lena’s expression sink a moment later, as though she had truly believed he might heed her every word.

    Strange…

    If the Keeper had gone through all the trouble of capturing James, then he was far past the point of reason. He already thought he had won. Kara suspected he might be right. She could feel it in her bones.

    Someone here was going to die.

    “How naïve you have always been… One of your many failings, I’m afraid. I should have liked to have had at least one child who didn’t turn out to be an utter disappointment. Oh, well.”

    Lena begged him a second time, “This isn’t right! Rebuilding the world doesn’t have to come at such great cost! We could work together and seek a more peaceful resolution. I- I could—”

    “No! I am done relying on others for what must be done!” Lionel screamed. “I finally have all that I need to save mankind, and I will let nothing else stand in my way! Blood or no blood, I will kill you if you defy me again.”

    Kara couldn’t bear to stand there any longer and do nothing. Worse still, J’onn remained idle where he was chained, unwilling to free himself. She knew her hand would soon be forced, and the thought of it stirred a growing anger inside her.

    Goddammit—

    You asshole!

    J’onn’s eyes flitted to her own in an instant, and Kara’s heart nearly stopped because of it. She knew then that he had truly given up. He wouldn’t be coming home with them.

    “No.” She pleaded with him from her end of the road, “No, please- don’t leave me.”

    “I am so proud of you, Kara.”

    “J’onn!” Kara finally screamed, startling Lena at her side. Without warning, she bolted into a sprint down the road and was met with a burst of gunfire. A bullet grazed the shell of her ear with a sharp -zip- as it tore through the air, followed by a devastating -crack- as two white-hot streaks of lightning leapt from sky and ground alike, colliding somewhere in between.

    More bolts cascaded down the mountainside in a strafing run from a set of bright red tailfeathers. A shrill cry split the air overhead.

    Peck had rejoined the fight.

    His electrifying distraction compelled more aid to move in from behind. Ava darted into view from Kara’s left and quickly struck her hands out towards the end of the road, forcing the armed unit to start firing upon one another with reluctant screams.

    James saw an opportunity there and took it— but too little too late, it would seem. Lionel released the charge from James’ collar with a press of a button and down he went, gripping the mud-slaked road with both hands.

    The scream that came out from his mouth vibrated all but the white, puffy clouds above them.

    Kara clamped her hands over her ears as the road split in two and watched as the tear formed a deep ravine at the base of the mountain. It continued racing up along its steep slope, where slabs of concrete and heavy equipment toppled sideways as the earth opened up like a wide mouth beneath them. They tumbled inside one by one until the volts being delivered to James’ neck finally cut out.

    The earth groaned beneath Kara’s feet. A whisper of relief. Another slab of concrete toppled, missing her by mere inches. She felt the heat from the sparks, smelled the ozone, and tasted the metallic tang in her mouth. Every instinct screamed at her: move, protect, survive.

    Still unsteady, Kara reached for Lena and then Ava, helping them back to their feet. She raised her hands in surrender just as Lionel, red-faced and wild-eyed, trained his pistol on the back of James’ head again. Kara’s eyes darted to J’onn— but still, he refused to act.

    “Enough!” Lionel shouted as he craned his head around to witness himself alone at last, “Martian, compel your boy to bring me that one’s head,” He threw his chin angrily towards Kara, “…or I swear to God, I will shoot everyone here while you watch!”

    J’onn frowned, “I will not.”

    Kara knew that J’onn must have realized the very same as her, that Lena’s father wouldn’t dare to rid himself of his last card to play.

    In a fit of rage, Lionel took the last step forward to jam the barrel of his pistol against James’ dark crown. His thumb pulled the small hammer back, “Last chance.”

    It was in that moment that James’ eyes met J’onn’s. Father and son shared an earth-splitting silence between them, passing one telepathic word after another. A well-weathered argument, no doubt. James shook his head once, and then twice, and then J’onn opened his mouth to beseech him at last…

    “Do it!”

    Kara gasped. James sent a hate-filled look across the ravine towards her and then set his sights back upon J’onn again. He aimed himself not at her, but at the man who had bore him such an exceptionally devastating long life. His only reason for living. The subject of his revenge.

    The bullet readied inside Lionel’s pistol suddenly ruptured, exploding its parts into Lionel’s hand with a loud -bang-. The mistake had been thusly made: Lionel had bridged the gap of physical contact with a remnant who could break anything that he touched. Before Kara could so much as shout, the collar around James’ neck broke in half and he was back on his feet, running three full strides directly at J’onn before landing on top of him with a -slam-. The ground shook again, and time (their true enemy) stood still.

    A horrendous green color aspirated from J’onn’s mouth as he exhaled.

    Stricken with an immediate regret, James unfurled his fist where it protruded directly out of J’onn’s back. Then, with a look of silent despair, he pulled it back through.

    J’onn fought for each and every breath after that, finite though they might be.

    “No—” Kara screamed, “No… no no no!”

    Her entire world shattered once again.

    Even in death, J’onn was seen to be full of love. His eyes stayed fixed upon the man whom he had once called “son”, and he whispered something to him with the last of his strength. Kara felt her chest tearing in two as J’onn’s life seemed to dwindle and watched as his head hung low for the last time. Then, his legs went out from beneath himself, chain pulling taut with his weight held just an inch or so off the ground.  

    Everything after that felt like wading knee-deep through mud, or windmilling through a vacuum.

    Lena tried her best to hold Kara back, and Kara felt her pulling there at her wrists, but apart from that, there seemed to be nothing else keeping her from throwing herself right into the deep, craggy ravine. Why not join her second father in the afterlife, where it was presumably safe? Whatever afterlife was so willing to attach itself to this one, that is. Kara knew that she couldn’t give herself over to it, not while there were still people alive and well who needed her. She wouldn’t leave Lena behind to mourn alone. What a cruel turn of events that would be. No, Kara knew that she would be the one to outlive Lena, not the other way around.

    Kara scraped her hands against the edge of the split earth to keep from falling in and then threw her head back again to scream some more— but not before James, with his arm slicked up to his elbow with J’onn’s green blood, turned to catch Lionel before he could think to run.

    His voice was soul crushing.

    “It’s over now,” James said with a sad stumble, “…finally.”

    With that, he gave a stamp of his foot, and then the mountain began to come down.

    The upper ring collapsed almost immediately. It caved in on itself as its foundations were demolished one by one. Snapped back to reality, Kara backpedaled and scooped Lena into her arms. She ran in the opposite direction towards the unobstructed part of the road and shouted for the rest of their group to follow, but they were soon cut off by a landslide of falling debris as the mountain continued to disassemble itself all the way from its godawful peak.

    James meant to bring an end not only to himself, but to them all. To him, they were all to bid the same impatient farewell, never to have existed in the first place. Kara only wished that she could have saved him from himself. Maybe then J’onn wouldn’t have found the means to bring such a cataclysmic end to all of their problems. He had sacrificed himself in an effort to ensure there would be no more of his progeny sprung upon the world, but it angered Kara amidst the outpouring of her grief… It was still so selfish of him. She should have listened to him the first time he had admitted he was tired. After all, who wouldn’t be after ten thousand years or more of the same horrible cycle? She should have seen it coming.

    My fault—

    Their path of escape now good and cut off, Kara began to spiral.

    It’s always my fault.

    Why must the world always be falling down on top of her? Lena threw her arms around Kara’s neck and held fast there.

    “Do you still believe?” Lena asked.

    That, fortunately enough, was all that Kara needed.

    Yes…

    Yes, I do.

    She began looking around for another way to see them all safely out of the compound until a sudden -pop- from one of Nyssa’s eerie portals began to open up wide, echoing far away and back again, in and out of time itself. Kara had felt her watching her slow descent into panic and assumed she’d taken it upon herself to give them a way out. The portal grew larger in size with each of her hands raised high, and she stirred it wider and wider until a number of them could easily step through… Kara had never seen a more spectacular sight in her entire life; a careening tunnel of pure starlight whereupon the other side lay a smattering of trees.

    “You must go—” Nyssa said at last with her face pinched together in pain, “Hurry!”

    Poor Ruby seemed hesitant at first to step through and into the whirling black mist that was tearing a giant hole through space, but not because she was scared to go in, it seemed… She appeared hesitant only to leave Nyssa behind.

    For such a perceived rapscallion, Nyssa was inherently tenderest towards children. Maybe she saw a bit of herself in them, Kara wondered. The child whom she used to be, if ever there was one.

    Samantha dragged her daughter through without another second to lose, and Kara could almost swear to have heard Peck screeching overhead, a final warning to flee certain doom. Lena ran behind and began to shout for the rest of the metahumans to follow suit, which they did with small, efforted jumps into the swirling void. Kara was quick to collect Barry as the last of them made their way towards their guaranteed safety. With Alex safely inside, she followed Ava’s commanding presence into the undulating black rupture and, for some odd reason, thought to turn around one last time betwixt the starry expanse…

    What she saw there back at the foot of the crumbling mountain broke her heart all over again.

    Sara would not go in.

    “Why’re you just standing there? You can let go now!” Sara begged, pulling at each of Nyssa’s arms.

    “Not until you are safe on the other side.” Nyssa replied.

    “But I don’t understand- why can’t you just come with us?” Sara looked upwards then at the portal’s immense size and seemingly realized that it was all too late, “What—” Her voice began to crack with dread, “What have you done?”

    Nyssa’s face softened, just as it always did whenever she looked upon her beloved. “I love you, Sara Lance.”

    Sara shook her head furiously in reply, “You idiot…”

    “Will you say that you love me in return?”

    “Nyssa—”  

    “I know that you do.”

    “Nyssa.”

    In lieu of any more arguing, Sara pressed herself from head to toe against Nyssa’s frame and then kissed her for as long as her held breath would permit. She split apart from her a moment later with a gasp and then opened her mouth to speak again.

    “Please, come with—”

    -fwip-

    -pop-

    It was as if the mud beneath Kara’s feet had been suddenly swapped for dry land. The smell of pine was fresh on the wind, carrying with it the scent of blood and rainwater.

    Sara’s anguished wailing soon filled the air.  

    Nyssa lied there in a heap at her feet, dead.

    Her skin was bluish with her sad eyes still at half-mast, almost as if she were plucked right out of time whilst staring longingly into Sara’s crystalline irises. Fate, as it were, had decided to lick at its pale fingertips and to pinch at the flame that did burn so brightly within her, snuffing out her life without so much as a second thought.

    Sara was inconsolable and violent for as long as anyone tried to comfort her. Her wife, Ava, knew just to leave her to her outbursts, as there was often nothing else to do in similar situations other than to simply be there for whenever she eventually crash-landed.

    Many of the rescued metahumans shared Sara’s heart-wrenching grief, weeping quietly nearby as the sun climbed higher over the piney forest. The distant, continuous rumble of the collapsing mountain felt almost inconsequential in comparison, fading into the background of their sorrow. Kara hated to direct everyone’s attention once again back to their escape, but she wouldn’t mark Nyssa’s sacrifice to be made in vain with having them all captured a second time. She was sure that the woods that lay ahead of them were still crawling with seekers.

    It took some convincing for Sara to leave Nyssa behind, not to mention a well-placed slug to Kara’s jawbone for the mere mention of it, but they finally agreed to come and collect her body whenever it was safe to do so. However long that would take.

    Sara must have shed a tear for every step she took after that…

    …for they came without end.

     

     

    Lena didn’t know quite what to say.

    There had been so much lost in the face of their freedom. A future without fear, a home without threat… Life was not without its awful parallels, Lena supposed, but what was lost would surely never be forgotten.

    Nyssa.

    J’onn.

    James.

    Her father.

    She thought to run to Kara as they slowed to catch their breath once again in the thick of the pines. She wished to shield her from all that was bad in the world. To protect her, as she had done for others for so, so long… Kara looked back at her just in time for the thought to compel her feet forward, but she stayed put instead. A calmness washed over her once again. Yes, they had faced insurmountable loss, but there stood Kara inside a brilliant beam of sunlight where it filtered down through the treetops, smiling at her just as she had done when they had first met. All of Kara’s hope-filled speeches had been brought to life inside of those dimpled cheeks, that wonderfully strong chin, and those two inimitable blue eyes that had a way of staring directly into her soul— because, according to Kara, Lena had one. The best of souls, as she put it. It was where Kara admitted to placing her faith for their future to come. Little did she know that the future she spoke of would indeed be a long one for them both.

    Around the hollowed-out trunk where Kara had left Barry, movement stirred. Alex waved urgently for Kara to return, and whenever she stepped aside, Lena saw Barry’s head tilt toward the path they’d just come from as if pulled by some invisible force. His eyes fluttered open groggily and his chest heaved with labored breaths, and then, to the amazement of everyone, he wobbled upright and came to stand on his own two feet again.

    Something on the wind tore down from the fallen mountain, whipping through the trees with a severe sense of urgency. It was coming their way, and fast. Barry stiffened, every muscle alert, as if some deep, primal instinct had already sensed the threat. He seemed to recognize it before anyone else.

    “Barry, hey now, wait- wait a minute!” Kara put her hands out to keep him still, but he looked straight past her and into the forest, intent on meeting whatever, or whoever, was there—

    And in a flash, he was gone.

    The sound barrier broke upon his presumed arrival, which gave way to shuddered tree limbs and pounded earth as something, or someone, continued to fight their way closer. Lena could hardly keep her eyes on them once they broke into the clearing. Barry slowed to a normal running gait several times in an effort to keep his balance, but whoever it was opposite him seemed to be no worse for wear. Another speedster, Lena realized, but not just any speedster…

    Please, let it be anyone but—

    Lena shrieked as Barry was thrown sideways into a tree, nearly wrapping around it from the force of the impact, but enough to uproot at least half of it. He slid to the ground with a pained grunt, coughed once or twice, and then slumped forward, unconscious. His opponent sped to a stop at the far tree line and laughed heartily.

    Lena’s father, the relentless evil.

    Oh, no.

    Lionel stared back at them with a wide grin. There didn’t seem to be any doubt in his mind that he would slaughter them all, one by one. The thought of it instilled a sort of finality inside Lena’s sword hand, whose nervous trembling soon stopped. This would be their final stand, she decided. Their day of reckoning. Lionel Luthor would never hurt anyone again for as long as she lived, should she not die at his hand first. The Union at her back, however, was already one step ahead…

    Sara shot an arrow from Nyssa’s bow to pierce Lionel’s sinister grin, to which he slapped out of thin air as if batting away a fly. Alex was not far behind in her own right— where shards of metal burst out of the soft ground and were sent flying across the clearing. A volley of gold, copper and silver. They pelted the trees instead of their intended target, who was still too fast to pin down. He taunted them as he flitted from one position to another…

    “There is nowhere to run!”

    He laughed again.

    “For I am everywhere!”

    Frustrated, Alex bent down with her good knee and searched the earth with the flat of her hand. She smiled just as soon as she found what she was looking for.

    The ground shook again as she clenched her teeth hard, unearthing the long barrel of an old-world tank, still intact. She flung the massive, armored vehicle towards the sound of his voice, and then spun around as she herself was hit. Lionel had landed a glancing blow against her impaired leg, but at such speed, he had broken it entirely. She fell to the ground screaming, and with that, Kara entered the fray.

    Ava ushered their rescued group further into the woods while Sara and Kara fought alongside one another in a practiced rhythm to stop Lionel. Any of them could die at any moment, Lena realized, and she had just been standing there.

    Determined, Lena’s voice traveled up the length of her throat again, no longer tucked away into its hiding place, and she ran to meet the fight head on.

    -boom-

    -crack-

    -hiss-

    “Ach—!”

    -thwip-

    -thud-

    “Watch out!”

    -crash-

    A tree split in half as Kara was launched into it, and half of her body came to be wedged there inside the sheer tensile strength of its fibers. She fought to squeeze her way out, but to no avail.

    “Kara!”

    -smack-

    Sara, having lost her stride, flipped over backwards as she was struck and landed crookedly onto her legs. It forced a shrill whimper out through her mouth whenever she scrambled to stand again. Her staff was far on the other side of the clearing, bent and twisted beyond use. No one had managed so far as to strike a single blow. The futility of it came crashing down upon Lena in waves. Her father would kill all of them except for Kara, whom he would likely torture until he was made as impervious to injury as she was. Only then might he kill her, having been made a “god” at last with his combined stolen abilities.

    Lionel trampled over the green ends of split branches towards Sara as she scuttled away from him. “Get away from me, you bastard!” She screamed.

    Lionel hacked a cold laugh as he picked her up by her slender neck, leaving the toes of her boots dangling in mid-air. Lena ran to lunge at him with her sabre again. She felt that she was left with no other choice than to part his hand clean from his body. And she would do it, too.

    This would end here and now. One way or another.

    -swipe-

    She blinked, and suddenly there was no one standing there before her. Her father’s voice came from behind her just as soon as she blinked again.

    “You are so very much like your mother, dear girl.”

    Lena’s rage mixed with the foul taste of bile that washed over the back of her tongue. Her throat burned again for all of her hatred of him.

    “I know that you killed her.”

    “I see her in your eyes, sometimes. But that vigor for life, I suppose, was beaten one too many times out of her. She hadn’t even the courage to defend herself at the end.”

    “Care to try it again?” Lena seethed at him, gripping her sabre tightly enough to run her knuckles white.

    “What… you?”

    “Aren’t you tired of murdering defenseless people? Best me in a Peacemaker, father. Earn your title as a ‘god’… Unless, of course, you fear that you’re no match for me. I will show you the mercy of a swift death, if that’s the case.”

    Every time he laughed, she felt like screaming. “Is that so?”

    “Do you accept?”

    “You do know the rules of engagement are different out here in the wilds?”

    “I do.”

    “There will be no yielding whenever I defeat you.”

    “Likewise.”

    Lionel scratched at his goatee for a moment, as if to chew on the thought, and then he raised his hand towards the ruined mountain as if to call to it. He waited patiently while something cut through the air towards him from a long distance, which eventually landed squarely inside of his palm. The shining pommel of his cane caught Lena’s eye and sent a jolt of fear up her spine, but only for a second’s worth. She and that cane had unfinished business, to be sure. From it, he drew a long, straight blade. A hidden sword, kept right at his side for all these years.

    “If any of you think to intervene, I will cut you down where you stand!”

    The terrified squeals of the children huddled together steeled Lena’s nerves more than ever. She would see them safely home— a vow she would not abandon. Lena breathed deeply through her nose as she circled the flattened grass opposite her father. The wind pressed lightly against her sweat-streaked skin, tugging at the wide sleeves of her shirt with soft ripples. Every sound was so crisp, every smell so pungent… She wiped the dried mud from her injured hand and readied her sabre in her other, tilting it upright to run in-line with her nose and then back down again at her side as a salute. Her father offered the same simple respect. The man was strict whenever it came to tradition, if anything.

    “En garde.” He said.

    Lena inhaled once more, then nodded, planting her feet in a ready stance. “Allez.”

    Quick to strike, her father assumed his point-in-line, extending his sword to bait Lena’s advance while simultaneously holding off her attack. If he offered even a trace of familial generosity —adhering to the formal rules of fencing— then she would have a real chance at winning. She had seen more duels in her young decades than he had in his illness-stricken centuries.

    Lena paced toward him slowly, eyes fixed, waiting for his first beat to start the match.

    -thwack-

    And so it began.

    His footwork nearly matched her own, but what fueled his skill was pure hubris. It offered him much in the way of feinted attacks and perfect parries but also made vulnerable his returns. Had he been well enough during Lena’s youth to meet her on their ornate piste, then he would have seen firsthand why her instructors often called her “prodigy.” Back and forth they fought over open ground. Lena had to admit before long that she had not dueled with such a fire behind her. Not in a long time, at least. Nyssa would have been proud. The thought made her lunge harder and faster, and soon she was able to draw more openings with each of her father’s attempts to guard.

    -tack-

    -tack-

    -tack-

    -thwip-

    Lionel barely yanked his free hand back, fingers saved by mere inches. To his shock, Lena’s blade had scored a long cut at the base of his thumb. A muffled scream tore through his clenched jaw once he realized that she was playing with him— leaving the same mark upon him as she had every single one of her instructors. In response, he growled angrily and shot out of sight only to re-appear directly in front of Lena with a fierce blow to her face. She barreled backwards with her hand to her cheekbone, narrowly missing two long slashes of his thin sword. He sped behind her after that and shoved her forward onto her hands and knees.

    “Augh—!” Lena cried out as she landed. Her eyes darted for a split-second over towards their group amassed in the trees. Several had moved in unison to hold Ava back from interfering. Lena shook her head quickly, telling her not to risk her own life.

    It has to be me.

    “Stand up- stand up!” Lionel shouted impatiently, “Pathetic!”

    Lena pushed herself up from the ground and then turned around to parry another of her father’s attacks. He would leave her no room to recover, it would seem.

    “How silly of me to think that you would honor tradition, daddy.”

    “Shut your mouth, girl!” He yelled and lunged for her again.

    She didn’t know why she had to rub his nose in it so much… Now she had gone and lost her advantage. Her emotions had undoubtedly colored her vision. She knew that seeing red would lead only to her death. Lena had a decision to make at that point, but knew well enough that her father was at least right about one thing.

    The outside world kept to its own set of rules. Or, rather, a complete lack of them, at times.

    In order to survive, she would have to adapt.

    Their duel continued with haste, fueled by her father’s intense desire to be known far and wide as this great and unbeatable foe, the “savior” of mankind. She would play that hand as long as she could, needing only one more opening to bring all of his madness to an end.

    -thwip-

    -tack-

    -tack-

    -swipe-

    “Ah!" Lena broke her riposte short just as Lionel cut deeply into the flesh of her forearm. A repayment for all of her toying with him, surely enough. The off-white color of her canvas sleeve quickly turned red with her blood, and as soon as she pawed there in a frantic effort to hold the cut together, it was already fusing shut before her very eyes. She was stunned for a moment, long enough for her father to dash in front of her again and to knock her down once more.

    She landed on her back hard enough to force most of the air from her lungs, but she was more concerned with reaching for her sabre which had been knocked loose from her grip.

    Lena barely reached the frayed end of the rope tassel when her father’s muddied shoe stepped firmly onto her wrist. He waved his cane sword before his face, a victorious grin twisting his features. Lena grimaced and clawed at his ankle, but the tip of his sword sank deep into the muscle of her shoulder for her trouble. She screamed and lied flat again, gasping.

    “Amazing, don’t you think?” He yanked his sword free of her shoulder then, watching with glee as the flesh there stitched itself back together. “To be unkillable…” He eyed the bloodied spot on her shirt where he had stabbed her and then laughed, “Well, not so unkillable, it would appear.”

    There it was.

    Her moment of world-upending hesitation, come and gone like a passing breeze.

    “What are you waiting for, hm?” Lena spat at the grass in front of him. That burning sensation in her throat soon would soon feel the cool balm of her vengeance. “Take up my sword, father.”

    Appearing as though he were happy enough just to oblige her, Lionel snapped his fingers and found his daughter’s sabre gracing the inside of his hand.

    “Now,” She seethed at him, “…run yourself through with it.”

    As if it were his very own idea, Lionel took both hands to the curved hilt of Lena’s sabre and then leveled it over his chest. His smile was still spread across his face as he pierced his own heart.

    Lena could hear gasps all the way from the tree line as Lionel dropped to his knees. The slow realization of what he had done came over him like a tidal wave. A look of horror ran across his features, washing them clean of his preconceived victory.

    “Goodbye, daddy.”

    He reached for Lena one last time, seeking to grasp his long-sought legacy. “My…” He whispered painfully, and then he toppled over, as still as stone.

    It took Kara several more tries to pull herself free from the middle of the large evergreen that held her captive, but she shouted for Lena to come away from the clearing lest her father revive himself by some manner of miracle and finish what he started.

    Afraid that he might, Lena reached to feel for his pulse. There were no signs of life still within him, it seemed, not with the way his skin began to shrivel and shrink at all points around his face, along with all of his hair rapidly turning wispy, and white. His true age seemed to snap back through time, shadowing decade over decade of healing and decay, and healing again, until he was naught but sinew and bone. Lena scrambled away from the sight of him and met Kara’s shoulders as soon as she had knelt to pick her up.

    Everything at once came out of her in wretched sobs.

    The father she should have had, the mother she had forgotten, and the brother she never knew, along with who knows how many others…

    The fact that it was all truly over with had struck her harder than she expected. Being held in Kara’s arms felt like floating across calm water— safe, suspended, and weightless. She longed to land on that shore again and again, and to stay there, basking in the warmth of her sun. Lena cried until reality’s weight pressed in, making her feel faint, and before she knew it, night had come. Or perhaps, it was merely sleep. Wherever it was, it rocked her back and forth like the gentle lapping of ocean against hull. The subtle -clack- of train tracks came not long after.

    The chaos had come to an end, and somehow it was all Lena could do just to dream of her immortal jellyfish, Turritopsis Dohrnii.

    How she would drift endlessly through time, just like them, but with Kara at her side.

    Forever.

    Chapter 28: Future Forward

    Chapter Notes

    The final chapter. Thank you all for reading. <3 As per usual, my epilogues are swift. Don't miss the notes at the end.

    Chapter Twenty-Eight: Future Forward

     

    —Six Months Later—

     

    The dahtiye flyer’s photokinetic wings carved a flawless figure-eight through the air, holding Kara’s gaze with an almost mesmerizing sort of trance. The hard, drought-ridden ground was quickly approaching, where microscopic particles of red dirt and clay dust kicked up into the air, sweeping outward once they landed with a smooth -bump-.

    Kara looked back to their pilot, who preemptively nodded in the affirmative that she would wait as long as needed.

    “I’ll just be a minute.”

    “Roger.”

    The wings above Kara’s head stalled midair, their long, light-woven feathers blazing proudly before scattering into particles and vanishing in the blink of an eye. She could see from a distance the tree where her beloved motorcycle was parked. Alex, forever the mechanical genius, had brought the chopper back to life again— on the condition, of course, that she could keep it. Kara had agreed too quickly. She was almost jealous to see how fast her sister had taken to it, but still happier for having her there, nonetheless.  

    “They say anything back, yet?” Kara asked quietly once she was betwixt the long rows of grave markers.

    Alex shook her head. “No. But then again, what would they even say? I still don’t know who they are.” She tucked a length of her light brown hair behind her ear, almost rust colored with highlights now from so much sun. The desert plains of Laredo might as well have been a completely different planet compared to the cloud-covered forests of the Pacific Northwest. “Feels weird…” She went on, “Talking to complete strangers with a forced sense of familiarity.”

    Kara crossed her legs and sat down beside Alex. She eyed the crumbling tablet face of their mother’s headstone and wondered if her handwriting had changed after two hundred years or not. The feeling of running her fingertip there through the stone, carving her mother and father’s names all those years ago, was ghostlike at best. Kara doubted very much that she would ever forget it.

    “You don’t have to, you know.”

    “I was told it would help.”

    “Do you always do what other people tell you?”

    “Erm,” Alex shrugged, “No. Not really.”

    Kara smiled.

    You’ll always be my Alex.

    Whether you know it or not.

    “We’re flying out to rendezvous with the Chiricahua.” Kara explained, “Seems they’ve been sheltering an empath who can’t get a handle on their abilities… Something about making the entire village randomly burst into tears, or whatever. We’ll have our hands full on the way back. Care to join?”

    Alex frowned. “I, well- I don’t know how helpful I could be with this leg, honestly.” She looked down and patted at the side of her knee, dusting the dirt from her faded black denim jeans. Kara understood immediately, but the question still remained…

    “Alex?” Her sister lifted her attention once more, and Kara was glad just to see her respond to the name more readily as of late. “Why haven’t you made another brace?” Kara asked, “I know that you can, but, is there something else stopping you?”

    “I dunno.” Alex replied, “I guess I’m just afraid that it’ll remind me too much of a past that I wanna forget.” She huffed a sudden laugh at that, “It’s ironic. The life I want to remember is as good as gone, but the one that haunts me seems to be around every corner still.”

    “It’ll come back. Some of it, I hope.”

    Alex sighed heavily, “Your lips to God’s ears.”

    “They would be proud of you,” Kara nodded towards the graves in front of them, “Mom, and dad.”

    “D’you really think so? I get the feeling sometimes back at the Infinite that I wasn’t exactly the life of the party…”

    “You were just a little prickly before, that’s all. To be fair, we literally watched the world fall apart for two centuries. I think that’d dim the light in anyone.” Kara folded her hands into her lap, “You carried a lot on your shoulders back then. I tried to help you unburden yourself every once in a while, but I think the damage was already done.”

    “I’d just like to know where I stand most of the time. Still feels like I’m kind of split in half.”

    “Well, as far as I can tell, if you can’t escape the past that still haunts you, and can’t remember the one that you had before, then maybe it’s a good thing that you’ve got a brand new future ahead of you. Maybe the past can just… stay where it is. What do you think?”

    Alex tried and failed to hide the grin that spread from one ear to the other. “Tell me, where did you get all this sage advice?”

    “I’m wise in my old age.”

    “I’ll say. Wait, is that a gray hair?”

    “What—” Kara’s hands immediately began combing through her shimmering flaxen locks, “Oh!” She laughed once the joke caught up with her, “Yep, you got me there.”

    It felt good to chuckle alongside her sister again. Almost as if she’d never gone. Kara knew that Alex’s full memory would likely never return, but she was excited to see what would play out in the years to come. It was a second chance for them both. A blessing in disguise.

    “Come with us.” Kara leaned towards the flyer again, “Let’s make some new memories.”

    “Maybe next time.”

    “You sure?”

    Alex lurched forward to stand and then balanced herself on her good knee, grimacing while she placed her weight on her other. “Yeah. I, um- I think I’ll go work on something new back at the forge.” She glanced down, “Maybe it’s time I did a little less hobbling around.”

    “I’m glad to hear it.” Kara replied with a beam of a smile as her sister turned to leave. “Oh, hey, take care with the throttle on that bike at high speeds. It likes to stick sometimes. Scared the shit outta me when I first figured it out.”

    “Oh, I fixed that already.”

    “…You did?”

    “Mhm.” Alex carefully limped towards the flowering desert willow where she had left the chopper parked. “Good luck.”

    Kara rolled her lips into her mouth and then gave a short nod before departing, but Alex’s voice was still right behind her.

    “…Kara?”

    There wasn’t time enough to respond before the feeling of Alex’s arms wrapping around Kara’s shoulders took her over completely. Her sister had landed against her with an unanticipated kind of abruptness, almost as if she were afraid to see Kara leave again. Kara knew that she wasn’t. Perhaps it was just a hug that was, for lack of a better explanation, a “long time coming”. Kara turned inside of Alex’s arms and then laid her cheek down upon her shoulder, and there they took long and steady breaths in each other’s company. A comfortable silence was spent, wherein so much was said. For Kara, it was confirmation enough. The silent but physical acknowledgement that Alex was indeed there to stay. She was content at last with the world, and with the people in it as well.

    Kara gave one last squeeze before letting go, and the words that she’d spent so many years waiting on went out from her mouth with the ease of a warm, summer-like breeze…

    “Welcome home.”

     

     

    Lena slid the book carefully back into its spot on the shelf.

    The amber glow of her lantern light had caught its title, withered and faded nearly beyond recognition, causing her to pull it free and to admire it for a long while. Thereupon its spine were its bold, black letters “The Price of Salt” amidst a pale teal background. There was some foxing of the first several pages, but Lena supposed that was what made the copy so special. Age spots told a story of their own.

    She smiled whenever she heard the small -creak- of the pueblo’s front door opening, and a relief slowly cascaded from her shoulders down.

    There would always be the anxiety-laden hours, or sometimes days, that she spent awaiting Kara’s return, but the feeling she got each and every time that she returned home greatly outweighed any of her previous worry. Kara was just as dependable as she was brave. She wouldn’t align herself to a continued cause and not consider the fact that there was someone now waiting patiently at home for her, expecting her to arrive all in one piece, no less.

    Kara’s exhaled breath skirted inside the loose collar of Lena’s blouse. It tickled at first, but only because her nerve endings were alive and swimming with the blood-alike awareness that another remnant was nearby. Surely enough, all of Kara’s prior explaining didn’t do it justice. It was a phenomenon which needed to be felt in order to be understood. Kara kissed gently along the side of Lena’s neck, and then something ran into her hands.

    Another book.

    Lena looked down and instantly recognized its cover.

    “Journey to the Center of the Earth?” Lena huffed a surprised breath of a laugh, “Where in the world did you find it?”

    Kara hummed sweetly against Lena’s ear and replied, “Traded my silver buckle for it.”

    Lena spun around, startling Kara, but only slightly. She looked down to the bare leather of Kara’s belt and frowned, “But I liked that one…” She gave a small pout for good measure, just to emphasize her point.

    “Oh?”

    “Mm, I rather liked the sound it made whenever I pulled it apart.”

    Lena’s bottom lip went into her teeth towards the end of her sentence, and the skin of her cheeks suddenly ran hot. Just the thought of Kara’s belt coming undone made her blood race. She reached to place the Jules Verne novel along the top tier of the wall-mounted shelf. She meant to come back to it just as soon as the thrumming in her veins was dealt with.

    Kara grabbed her up by her hips quickly and then pulled her close. “I missed you.”

    The shiver that went up the length of Lena’s spine was undeniable. She couldn’t have hidden it even if she had tried. It terminated with a small whimper through her nose, and then her hands were smoothing back down the granite block of Kara’s abdomen all the way to her belt. Its naked leather was smooth against her fingertips.

    “How much?” Lena asked, husking the words breathily against the strong point of Kara’s chin.

    Kara bowed forward and stole a quick kiss. “So much.”

    Lena didn’t skip a beat after that. “Show me.”

    The clatter and scrape of the narrow reading table as it went against the wall with Lena’s weight nearly masked the squeak that came out from her mouth. Kara’s return home from every mission was… rigorous… to say the least. But it wasn’t as if Lena didn’t possess the stamina now to match her, not to mention the lack of fear that Kara might injure her in the process.

    The days of calm and careful were gone.

    -fwump-

    -knock-

    -crash-

    Lena had pulled the length of Kara’s belt through each loop so quickly that it slapped its leather end with subtle -pop- sounds, causing Kara to growl from deep within her chest. The waist of her jeans went over her hips, as did Lena’s, leaving just a moment more to pull Kara’s slightly torn henley right over her head. The way that Lena’s fingernails scraped along each rung of Kara’s ribcage only made the blonde growl again, this time with her face buried deep into the tender crook of Lena’s neck.

    Kara rolled her hips forward along the top of Lena’s thigh, and the landslide that was the relief-filled sigh to follow went all the way down to her toes.

    “God—” Kara shuddered, “I thought about you every day.”

    Lena clutched at Kara’s waist, desperate to keep her close, “Hm- felt like years.”

    “I’m sorry.”

    “Don’t be sorry…” Lena craned to capture Kara’s lips with a kiss, “You’re mine again, that’s what matters.”

    The table that Lena had been placed upon knocked into the wall again with the force of Kara’s weight held against it. She spread Lena’s legs apart with the brunt of her knuckles and then smoothed two of her fingers all the way inside. It forced a hard-fought moan out from Lena’s mouth, who tried everything under the sun to keep from throwing Kara to the floor and having her way with her right then and there. She thought to herself for a split-second, why not? She was strong enough. But the way that Kara pressed into her as if she were the cool spring to quench the fire in her limbs was far too powerful to deny.

    It was the -creak- of wooden table legs against a clay floor.

    The intonated moans that were loosed against the warmed skin of Lena’s neck.

    Their rhythm of push and pull suddenly crested with Kara’s brow dropping against the wall as she muffled a gratified bellow through the hard clench of her teeth. Less inhibited now by the fact that Lena was nearly as unbreakable as herself, Kara was frequently beholden to her own body’s defiant way of unraveling just a bit too soon. Lena brushed some of her hair from her eyes and laughed, twitching once with the feeling of Kara’s slender fingers still plunged deep inside of her.

    “Mmm,” She warbled from behind her lips and began panting excitedly, “…don’t stop.”

    An incoherent sort of affirmation rumbled its way to the lobe of her ear where Kara sucked happily, and instead of replying, Kara sank straight to her knees. She flashed a wide grin before bobbing her head between Lena’s thighs, just as generous as she was methodic, it would seem.

    “Yes—” Lena gasped, “Fuck, yes, yes, yes.”

     

     

    The glow of her lantern was but a steady flicker now. There was the need to squint as Lena read, for which she soon resolved just to close the book again and to lay it flat against the soft wool of the sheepskin rug. Her forests of giant mushrooms and pods of curious plesiosaurs would have to wait.

    Kara rolled over to scoop Lena inside of her arms again, one last gentle half-snore out through her nose to mark her waking. Their shared nakedness was like pulling a warm cover over them both. “M’okay?” She mumbled.

    “Perfect.” Lena sighed. She wiggled all the way around to face Kara and then thumbed lightly at the precious vertical divot of her upper lip. A dimple formed deep inside of Kara’s cheek whenever she smiled, her eyes still closed.

    “What are you thinking about?” She asked.

    “Truthfully? Lots of things.” Lena replied with a whisper and then spoke out loud, “But mostly about the future, I suppose.”

    “I see. What do you wanna do?”

    The question struck Lena with a shade of absurdity at first. Kara had asked it so plainly. “What do you mean?”

    “I mean,” Kara scooted closer and finally gave slow blinks of her eyes as they opened, “We can do whatever you want. Go wherever you want.”

    “I… I don’t know. I guess I haven’t allowed myself to think on such a grand scale before now. You don’t mean you would leave the Infinite, do you?”

    “If you wanted to.”

    Lena reared her head back slightly. “Really?”

    “Really.” Kara yawned, “So? What d’you wanna do?”

    “Hm. If anything, I’d like to be useful somewhere.”

    “Caitlin mentioned a place in need of a doctor up north. One of the metas we brought home had said something about a village in a valley overrun by sickness. Something they couldn’t cure before—” Kara stopped all of a sudden and then took a deep breath, removing herself from the rest of her statement entirely. It was clear that she didn’t like the reminder of it. Lena knew.

    “How far north?” Lena asked.

    “Somewhere smack in the middle of the Three Sisters, last I heard.”

    “Ah.”

    “I didn’t say anything until now because I wanted to give you some time to unwind. For life to go back to normal, whatever that is.”

    “What sort of sickness?”

    Kara took Lena’s arm across and then pecked a small kiss at her forehead. “I didn’t ask. Do you… wanna look into it?”

    Lena turned her head to look up at the long, foundational cracks of the ceiling. The barely lit wick of the lantern danced the shadow of Kara’s bookshelf there. This would always be home, wouldn’t it? Lena laid there and wondered a while longer. No, she decided… You can’t expect her to stay in one place forever.

    “You would come with me?”

    “Of course.” Kara replied without a drop of hesitation.

    “I do long to see mountains again…”

    “Weirdly enough, so do I.”

    Lena giggled and reached to pinch at Kara’s side. “You’re just saying that.”

    “No.” Kara shook her head, “I mean it.”

    “But… what about the Infinite? And Alex? And Nat—”

    “Who says they wouldn’t jump at the chance to come with us?”

    Lena shot her eyes back towards Kara’s baby blues. They shone brightly enough with the light from their own internal sun— Kara’s wonderfully human soul.

    “We could build something for J’onn there.” She said, “A monument of sorts. Something to remember him by.”

    “That sounds great.”

    “After all, time is finally on our side. I’m only scared that it might forget him…”

    “We won’t let that happen.”

    “So,” Lena nestled more comfortably against Kara’s bare chest, “It’s up to us, then?”

    Kara gave a tuck of her chin in agreement. “I’ll take you wherever the world needs you the most. We’ll fix it together.”

    “That might take a while.”

    “Hey, you said it yourself.” Kara smiled and closed her eyes again, “Time’s on our side now.”

    Lena’s eyes fell heavily once more. Soon the glimmer of dawn would pour through the Infinite’s grand ceiling to awaken the city that lied beneath. It would be hard leaving it all behind, but then again, she suspected a lot of that would lie ahead of them in the centuries that awaited. Might as well get used to it. She hummed sleepily against Kara’s magnificently soft stone skin and beckoned the future forward.

    “So it is.”
